# Spec — International public-good extensions to encryptalotta

Status: draft 290 — draft 290 lands **BLAKE3-256**, completing **§2.12** (the BLAKE2b / BLAKE3 hash workstream) — the one slice that section left deferred. BLAKE3 is added as a sixth algorithm row in the existing Hash & Checksum tool (no new view, no new top-level entry), beside SHA-1 / SHA-256 / SHA-384 / SHA-512 / BLAKE2b-512. The deferral reason recorded in §2.12 ("its parallel tree-mode reference implementation is substantially larger and would benefit from a vendored single-file build") is resolved by shipping the **single-threaded reference construction** hand-rolled inline (~110 lines), matching the BLAKE2b precedent: it produces byte-identical output to the multi-threaded SIMD build, it is simply not parallel. Unlike BLAKE2b's 64-bit-word emulation, BLAKE3 is a pure 32-bit design, so the inner loop is plain `Uint32` arithmetic (`>>> 0` wraparound, `b3_rotr`) with no `[lo,hi]` pairs. The implementation walks the full Merkle-tree mode — 1024-byte chunks, 64-byte blocks, the binary-counter subtree-merge stack, and the root-output XOF — so it is correct for inputs of any length (text or file), not just single-chunk inputs. No new vendored file (so the SHA-384 integrity manifest and page-weight budget are untouched), no new UI string (algorithm names are not localized), no CSP change. The JSON-LD feature description and the command-palette `hash` synonyms gained `blake3`. Verified against the **official BLAKE3 test vectors** (lengths 0 / 1 / 1024 / 1025 / 2048 / 3072, the `i mod 251` pattern) and cross-checked byte-for-byte against the audited `@noble/hashes` implementation across every chunk/block boundary to 256 KiB plus 200 random inputs. 4 new Playwright cases — empty / "abc" / the pangram (single-chunk) and a 3072-byte file (the 3-chunk, two-parent-level tree path) — assert the published vectors through the real UI (suite now 775 serial cases). With this §2.12 is feature-complete; the remaining open §2 tools are §2.9 (Argon2id) and §2.11 (age). — Historical note — draft 289 lands the **ISO 18013-5 mdoc decoder**, completing **§2.8 (the EUDI-wallet credential decoder)** across both formats — SD-JWT (drafts 20–24) and mdoc now both decode in the JWT / credential inspector. Paste an mdoc (DeviceResponse / Document / IssuerSigned, as CBOR hex or base64url) and the inspector renders the document type, every disclosed data element per namespace (decoded from the tag-24 `IssuerSignedItem`s), and the issuer-authentication MSO via the COSE_Sign1 `issuerAuth` — digest algorithm, validity window (signed / validFrom / validUntil), value-digest count, device-key presence, and the COSE protected-header signature algorithm — plus the issuer signer certificate decoded through the X.509 parser when an x5chain (COSE header label 33) is present. It reuses the draft-287/288 CBOR decoder (recursive tag-24 already expands the `MobileSecurityObject`) and the cert renderer; mdoc input is detected by the absence of JWT / SD-JWT separators plus an mdoc CBOR shape, so a JWT or SD-JWT still routes to its own path unchanged. Decode-only — no issuer-trust-anchor verification (per §2.8). 12 new `mdoc.*` UI strings × 5 locales; the JWT input label now reads "JWT / SD-JWT, or an ISO 18013-5 mdoc". 1 new Playwright case builds a complete mdoc with a tiny in-test CBOR encoder (two data elements, a full MSO with tag-0 validity dates, ES256 COSE_Sign1, x5chain cert) and asserts the rendered docType / data elements / digest algorithm / validity / alg / signer subject (suite now 771 serial cases). With this §2.8 is feature-complete. — Historical note — draft 288 makes the CBOR decoder **credential-aware**, so a pasted ISO 18013-5 mdoc / COSE / CWT decodes to a fully-readable, self-labelled tree — the substantive §2.8 mdoc-decoding capability delivered through the general CBOR tool. Three RFC 8949 semantic-tag refinements: (1) **tag 24 "encoded CBOR data item"** (§3.4.5.1) is now decoded recursively — the byte string *is* CBOR, so the mdoc `MobileSecurityObject` inside `issuerAuth` and any COSE payloads decode inline instead of showing as opaque hex; (2) **bignum tags 2 / 3** (§3.4.3) decode to the integer they encode (as a decimal string when it exceeds `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`); (3) **well-known tags are named** via a `$tagName` field from the IANA registry — COSE_Sign1 (18), COSE_Mac0 (17), COSE_Encrypt0 (16), COSE_Sign / Mac / Encrypt (96–98), CWT (61), plus the date / URI / base64 tags — so a decoded credential is self-describing. With this, pasting an mdoc's CBOR hex into Format & Convert surfaces the document version, docType, namespaces and every data element, the COSE_Sign1 `issuerAuth`, and the decoded MSO (validity dates, value digests, device key) — no dedicated tool needed; a curated mdoc *summary* view (labelled validity/docType rows) is now optional polish, not a capability gap. No new UI strings (pure decoder logic); 1 updated + 1 new Playwright case (recursive tag-24, a 2^64 bignum, COSE_Sign1 tag naming; suite now 770 serial cases). — Historical note — draft 287 lands the **hand-rolled CBOR decoder (RFC 8949)** that §2.8 names as the prerequisite for the ISO 18013-5 mdoc / EUDI-wallet credential decoder (and the foundation for any COSE / CWT / WebAuthn-attestation work). It ships as a new **CBOR (hex / base64) → JSON / YAML** input format on the Format & Convert tool: `cborParse()` accepts hex or base64, `cborDecode()` walks all eight RFC 8949 major types (definite + indefinite lengths, half/single/double floats, tags, simple values), emitting plain JS values with unambiguous wrappers for the CBOR-only types JSON lacks — byte strings → `{$bytes:"<hex>"}`, tags → `{$tag:n,$value:…}`, unknown simples → `{$simple:n}` — and integers beyond `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` as decimal strings (JSON can't carry them and `JSON.stringify` throws on BigInt). Decode-only (no CBOR encoder); a CBOR→XML request hits the same cross-format guard as YAML→XML. Hand-rolled inline, matching the project's other small parsers (ASN.1, SSH, Base32/58) rather than vendoring a `cbor.min.js`; no network, pure in-tab parse. 1 new UI string (`format.opt.cbor`) × 5 locales + the input placeholder updated. 3 new Playwright cases over RFC 8949 Appendix A vectors (map / array / int / negative / text; byte strings + tag 0 + simple values; suite now 769 serial cases). With CBOR in hand, the remaining §2.8 ISO-mdoc slice is just the COSE_Sign1 unwrap + MobileSecurityObject structure rendering on top of this decoder. — Historical note — draft 286 lands the **XAdES slice of §2.5**, completing the eIDAS signature inspector across all three signature families. The X.509 / CMS tool now also accepts a pasted **XML signature** (XAdES / XML-DSig, ETSI EN 319 132 / RFC 3275): when the input starts with `<`, `xadesSignatures()` parses it with `DOMParser` and collects every `<ds:Signature>` carrying a `SignedInfo`, and `renderXadesInto()` surfaces — per signature — the **XAdES level** (B-B / B-T / B-LT / B-LTA, inferred from which qualifying properties are present: `SignedProperties` → B, `SignatureTimeStamp` → T, `CertificateValues`/`RevocationValues` → LT, `ArchiveTimeStamp` → LTA), the signature method and canonicalization method (mapped from their algorithm URIs), each `<ds:Reference>` digest method, the `xades:SigningTime`, and every embedded `<ds:X509Certificate>` decoded through the shared X.509 parser. Namespace-blind by `localName` like the SEPA / UBL / LOTL tools; no network, no signature-cryptographic verification (the tool reports structure + identity, not a validity verdict). 8 new UI strings (`xades.*`) × 5 locales, reusing `cms.row.signingTime` / `cms.cert` / `pades.sig`; the input label now enumerates all four accepted inputs. 1 new Playwright case decodes a B-B XAdES signature with an embedded EC cert (suite now 766 serial cases). **§2.5 is now feature-complete to its drafted scope** — CAdES / PKCS#7 (284), PAdES signed-PDF (285), and XAdES (286) — the three signature encodings the spec named, each reusing the one ASN.1 / X.509 engine (or `DOMParser`) the project already ships. — Historical note — draft 285 adds the **PAdES slice of §2.5**: the X.509 / CMS tool now ingests a **signed PDF** and decodes its embedded signature(s). A PAdES signature is a PDF signature dictionary `<< /Type /Sig /SubFilter /ETSI.CAdES.detached /ByteRange […] /Contents <hex CMS> >>` whose `/Contents` is the zero-padded DER CMS SignedData; `pdfExtractSignatures()` scans every hex-string `/Contents` (skipping the page `/Contents N 0 R` indirect refs, which are not hex strings), hex-decodes it, keeps the ones that are a CMS SignedData, and hands each to the draft-284 CAdES decoder — the trailing zero padding is ignored because `asn1Read` reads only the declared outer length. Reuses the byte-walking from draft 283's Factur-X extractor; the CMS rendering was refactored into a shared `renderCmsInto()` so the pasted-CMS and PAdES paths render identically. A new `btn-tls-pdf` file input sits beside the cert textarea (mirroring the UBL inspector's PDF loader); 5 new UI strings (`btn.tlsLoadPdf` + `tls-pdf.aria` + `pades.*`) × 5 locales, reusing the generic `ubl.pdf.reading` / `ubl.pdf.error` / `ubl.pdf.notPdf` messages. 2 new Playwright cases (extract + decode a real CMS-in-PDF; report an unsigned PDF; suite now 765 serial cases). With CAdES (284) + PAdES (285) the §2.5 inspector reads both standalone CMS signatures and signed PDFs; only XAdES (XML signatures, `DOMParser`-based) remains a deferred slice. — Historical note — draft 284 opens **§2.5 (the eIDAS signature inspector)** with its foundational **CAdES / PKCS#7 (CMS, RFC 5652) decoding slice**, landed inside the existing X.509 / TLS Certificate Parser exactly as the spec prescribes ("extend the existing X.509 parser — it already does ASN.1"), not as a separate tool. The parser now detects a PKCS#7 / CMS ContentInfo (signedData OID `1.2.840.113549.1.7.2`, by PEM label or structure) and surfaces, for a SignedData blob: a summary (N embedded certificates, M signers), every embedded X.509 certificate (reusing the existing cert renderer), and each SignerInfo's digest algorithm, signature algorithm and signed signing-time attribute (RFC 5652 §11.3). Detached/attached signatures, certs-only bundles (`.p7b` / `.p7c`) and CAdES-BES all decode; positional SignerInfo parsing handles the `IssuerAndSerialNumber` sid (itself a SEQUENCE) without mistaking it for an AlgorithmIdentifier, and embedded certs are sliced out of the `certificates [0]` field by re-walking it at tracked offsets so the shared `asn1Read` is left untouched (no regression to the cert / TLS path). No network, no revocation checking — the tool reports only what the blob carries (per §2.5). Reuses the existing ASN.1/DER reader, the OID table (extended with the CMS + digest OIDs) and `pemToArrayBuffer`; 7 new UI strings (`tls-cert.row.type` + `cms.*`) × 5 locales; the input label now reads "PEM X.509 certificate, or a PKCS#7 / CMS bundle or signature (.p7b / .p7c / CAdES)". 1 new Playwright case decodes a real openssl-generated CMS signature (1 cert / 1 signer / signingTime; suite now 763 serial cases). PAdES (extract the PDF `/Sig` CMS — the PDF byte-walking is already in place from draft 283's `pdfExtractInvoiceXml`) and XAdES remain deferred slices of §2.5. — Historical note — draft 283 lands the **last deferred CII slice: hybrid PDF/A-3 attachment extraction**, so the §2.7 inspector now ingests a Factur-X / ZUGFeRD PDF directly, not just the standalone XML. Factur-X / ZUGFeRD embed the EN 16931 invoice as a PDF/A-3 attachment (conventionally `factur-x.xml` / `zugferd-invoice.xml` / `xrechnung.xml`) stored as a FlateDecode (zlib) stream. Rather than vendor a PDF library and parse the full object graph + cross-reference tables (which would break the 2 MB page-weight budget), `pdfExtractInvoiceXml()` walks every `stream … endstream` block, inflates each with the browser-native `DecompressionStream` (`deflate` → `deflate-raw` → raw, the last only when the bytes begin with `<`), and returns the first block decoding to a `<rsm:CrossIndustryInvoice>` or UBL `Invoice-2` / `CreditNote-2` document — the invoice-root signature check keeps the PDF's own XMP metadata stream (`<x:xmpmeta>`) from being mistaken for the invoice. Entirely client-side (`file.arrayBuffer()` → in-tab inflate; no network — `connect-src 'none'` and the audit's outbound-vector scan are untouched, since `Blob` / `Response` / `DecompressionStream` are not fetch/XHR/WebSocket/EventSource/sendBeacon), wired to a new `btn-ubl-pdf` file input beside the textarea; the stale "Factur-X CII not yet" input label was corrected and now reads "…or load a hybrid PDF below". 7 new UI strings (`btn.ublLoadPdf` + `ubl.pdf.*`) × 5 locales; 3 new Playwright cases (extract+parse an embedded CII PDF, report a PDF with no invoice, reject a non-PDF) — the suite builds the test PDF in-process with `zlib.deflateSync` and feeds it through `setInputFiles`, so no binary fixture is committed (suite now 762 serial cases). The CII inspector is now feature-complete to its drafted scope: detection, rendering, 24 conformance gates, profile identification, and hybrid-PDF ingestion. — Historical note — drafts 281 + 282 land the **deferred CII profile-highlighting slice** plus the seller-VAT value gate, extending the CII-native family to twenty-four gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary. **Draft 281 — CII Factur-X / ZUGFeRD / XRechnung profile identification** (`ciiCheckProfile`): the profile-level-highlighting slice deferred since draft 258 — EN 16931 BT-24 carries the CustomizationID URN naming the conformance profile the document was authored to (Factur-X / ZUGFeRD MINIMUM → BASIC WL → BASIC → EN 16931 (COMFORT) → EXTENDED, plus the XRechnung / PEPPOL CIUS variants; ZUGFeRD 2.x is the German co-publication of Factur-X and shares the same `urn:factur-x.eu:1p0:*` / `urn:cen.eu:en16931:2017` URN family). A new `ciiProfileName()` helper maps the URN (ordered so "basicwl" is tested before "basic" and the CIUS variants before the bare EN 16931 URN they extend); informational rather than pass/fail — emits a ✓ row naming a recognised profile or a neutral row echoing the raw URN for a newer/private profile; no-ops when the CustomizationID is absent (draft 275 owns BR-01 presence). **Draft 282 — CII seller VAT identifier validity** (`ciiCheckSellerVat`): EN 16931 BT-31 — the Seller VAT id is carried in CII as `ram:SellerTradeParty/ram:SpecifiedTaxRegistration/ram:ID` with `schemeID="VA"` (UNCL 1153; "FC" would be a fiscal number, not the VAT id); reuses the shared `vatValidate()` (the §2.3 EU VAT tool's format + checksum), the CII analogue of `ublCheckSellerVat`; value-validation only — skips when the seller declares no VAT registration (BR-CO-26 permits tax- or legal-registration identification instead), firing only on a present-but-invalid VAT id. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 759 serial cases). Housekeeping this cycle — the README CII cheat sheet gained a **Factur-X / ZUGFeRD profile table** (URN → scope → use across the six profiles), the gate table rows 281 / 282, and the deferral note was corrected (profile highlighting now ships; only hybrid PDF/A-3 attachment extraction remains deferred). The CII gate family now stands at twenty-four (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown sum 268 / VAT cross-product 269 / VAT category code 270 / party countries 271 / country-code validity 272 / line cross-product 273 / due-date ordering 274 / CustomizationID 275 / tax-scheme type code 276 / item name 277 / quantity unit code 278 / net-price presence 279 / line-quantity presence 280 / profile identification 281 / seller-VAT validity 282) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 279 + 280 complete the **CII per-line mandatory-field set**, extending the CII-native family to twenty-two gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary; both confirm on the existing fixture (no fixture surgery) and are the presence companions to the line net cross-product (draft 273), which silently skips a line missing its price or quantity. **Draft 279 — CII line net-price presence** (`ciiCheckPriceAmount`): EN 16931 BR-26 requires every invoice line to declare the item net price (BT-146) — in CII the `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeAgreement/ram:NetPriceProductTradePrice/ram:ChargeAmount` (≈ the UBL `cac:Price/cbc:PriceAmount`); read as a *direct child* of NetPriceProductTradePrice so a nested AppliedTradeAllowanceCharge (`ram:ActualAmount`) is never mistaken for the net price; the CII analogue of `ublCheckPriceAmountPresence`; flags each priceless line by LineID. **Draft 280 — CII line billed-quantity presence** (`ciiCheckLineQuantity`): EN 16931 BT-129 requires every invoice line to declare the quantity invoiced — in CII the `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeDelivery/ram:BilledQuantity` (≈ the UBL `cbc:InvoicedQuantity` / `cbc:CreditedQuantity`); the quantity-value companion to the `@unitCode` gate (draft 278); the CII analogue of `ublCheckLineQuantity`; flags each quantity-less line by LineID. Together with item name (277), unit code (278), line total (262) and the line cross-product (273) these close the per-line mandatory set: name, quantity, unit, price, and line total. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 755 serial cases). The CII gate family now stands at twenty-two (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown sum 268 / VAT cross-product 269 / VAT category code 270 / party countries 271 / country-code validity 272 / line cross-product 273 / due-date ordering 274 / CustomizationID 275 / tax-scheme type code 276 / item name 277 / quantity unit code 278 / net-price presence 279 / line-quantity presence 280) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 277 + 278 add the **CII line-item descriptive-presence** gates, extending the CII-native family to twenty gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary; both confirm on the existing fixture (no fixture surgery). **Draft 277 — CII line item-name presence** (`ciiCheckItemName`): EN 16931 BR-25 requires every invoice line to name the item supplied (BT-153) — in CII the `ram:SpecifiedTradeProduct/ram:Name` (≈ the UBL `cac:Item/cbc:Name`); a line without a name is rejected at the schematron gate and gives the buyer's AP / ERP nothing to reconcile the charge against; the CII analogue of `ublCheckItemName`, flagging each unnamed line by its LineID, no-ops on a no-line document. **Draft 278 — CII billed-quantity unit-code presence** (`ciiCheckQuantityUnitCode`): EN 16931 BT-130 — every line's billed quantity must declare its unit of measure as the `@unitCode` attribute (UN/ECE Recommendation 20 / 21) on `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeDelivery/ram:BilledQuantity` (≈ the UBL `InvoicedQuantity/@unitCode`); a bare quantity with no unit is rejected at the schematron gate because the receiver cannot interpret "3" without knowing pieces vs kilograms vs hours; the CII analogue of `ublCheckQuantityUnitCode`, flagging each line whose BilledQuantity carries a value but no `@unitCode`, skipping a line with no billed quantity. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 751 serial cases). Housekeeping this cycle — the README `Conformance-gate cheat sheet` section gained a dedicated **CII-native gates** subsection: a monetary-chain reconciliation diagram (Σ lines → line total → tax-basis → grand total → due payable, with the VAT-breakdown branch) and a 20-row table mapping each CII gate to its EN 16931 code, `ram:` anchor, and UBL analogue; the section title and the stale draft-range pointer (16 → 266) were corrected to 16 → 278. The CII gate family now stands at twenty (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown sum 268 / VAT cross-product 269 / VAT category code 270 / party countries 271 / country-code validity 272 / line cross-product 273 / due-date ordering 274 / CustomizationID 275 / tax-scheme type code 276 / item name 277 / quantity unit code 278) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 275 + 276 add the **CII profile-anchor + tax-scheme validity** gates, extending the CII-native family to eighteen gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary. **Draft 275 — CII specification-identifier (CustomizationID) presence** (`ciiCheckCustomizationId`): EN 16931 BR-01 requires every invoice to declare a Specification identifier (BT-24) — in CII the `ram:GuidelineSpecifiedDocumentContextParameter/ram:ID` under `ram:ExchangedDocumentContext` (≈ the UBL `cbc:CustomizationID`), the URN naming the EN 16931 / Factur-X profile the receiver validates against; a document without it is rejected at the schematron gate because the routing layer cannot select the rule set; the CII analogue of `ublCheckCustomizationIdPresence`, and because BR-01 is unconditional this gate always emits a row (a missing context block flags, never silently skips). **Draft 276 — CII tax-scheme type-code validity** (`ciiCheckTaxTypeCode`): EN 16931 restricts the tax scheme to UNCL 5153 code "VAT" — in CII each `ram:ApplicableTradeTax` declares its scheme in `ram:TypeCode` (the analogue of the UBL `cac:TaxScheme/cbc:ID`); PEPPOL / Factur-X reject a tax block whose TypeCode is anything other than "VAT", and the per-category VAT rollup cannot bin a non-VAT scheme; the CII analogue of `ublCheckTaxScheme`; reads `ram:TypeCode` as a *direct child* so it never picks up the document-level `ram:ExchangedDocument/ram:TypeCode` (BT-3, owned by draft 264); walks every `ram:ApplicableTradeTax` (header + line), no-ops when none declares a TypeCode. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 747 serial cases). The CII gate family now stands at eighteen (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown sum 268 / VAT cross-product 269 / VAT category code 270 / party countries 271 / country-code validity 272 / line cross-product 273 / due-date ordering 274 / CustomizationID 275 / tax-scheme type code 276) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 273 + 274 open the **CII line-level arithmetic + temporal-ordering surface**, extending the CII-native family to sixteen gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary. **Draft 273 — CII line-level net cross-product** (`ciiCheckLineAmount`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-04 / PEPPOL EN16931-R120 (`ublCheckLineAmount`) — on each line, the LineTotalAmount (BT-131, `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeSettlement/…/ram:LineTotalAmount`) must equal the net price (BT-146, `ram:NetPriceProductTradePrice/ram:ChargeAmount`) × the billed quantity (BT-129, `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeDelivery/ram:BilledQuantity`) / the price base quantity (BT-149, `ram:NetPriceProductTradePrice/ram:BasisQuantity`, defaulting to 1); the header line-sum gate (draft 265) verifies the per-line totals add up to the header figure, but a line total can be internally inconsistent with its own price × quantity while the lines still sum correctly — the per-line companion to draft 265 exactly as draft 269 is to draft 268; `ram:ChargeAmount` / `ram:BasisQuantity` are read as *direct children* of NetPriceProductTradePrice so a nested AppliedTradeAllowanceCharge (which carries `ram:ActualAmount`) is never mistaken for the net price; integer-cent, ±1 cent tolerance; skips a line missing its price / quantity / total (presence is draft 262's territory). **Draft 274 — CII payment-due-date temporal ordering** (`ciiCheckDueDate`): the CII analogue of the UBL DueDate ≥ IssueDate gate (`ublCheckDates`) — the payment due date (BT-9, `ram:ApplicableHeaderTradeSettlement/ram:SpecifiedTradePaymentTerms/ram:DueDateDateTime`) must not precede the issue date (BT-2, `ram:ExchangedDocument/ram:IssueDateTime`); EN 16931 does not number this as a business rule, but payment cannot fall due before the invoice is issued and PEPPOL / Factur-X implementations universally reject a backdated due date; both dates decode through the existing `ublCiiDate` helper (`udt:DateTimeString` format-102 CCYYMMDD → ISO); no-ops when either date is absent or unparsable. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 743 serial cases). The CII gate family now stands at sixteen (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown sum 268 / VAT cross-product 269 / VAT category code 270 / party countries 271 / country-code validity 272 / line cross-product 273 / due-date ordering 274) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 271 + 272 open the **CII party-address conformance surface**, the first CII gates to reach past the trade-party name (draft 261) into the postal address, extending the CII-native family to fourteen gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary. **Draft 271 — CII seller/buyer postal-address country presence** (`ciiCheckPartyCountries`): EN 16931 BR-09 requires the Seller country code (BT-40) and BR-11 the Buyer country code (BT-55) — in CII each is carried directly in `ram:{Seller,Buyer}TradeParty/ram:PostalTradeAddress/ram:CountryID` (no separate IdentificationCode wrapper as in UBL); the country code anchors which member state's VAT regime governs each side of the supply, so a document missing it is rejected at the EN 16931 schematron gate; the CII analogue of the UBL BR-09 / BR-11 gates (`ublCheckSellerPostalAddressCountry` / `ublCheckBuyerPostalAddressCountry`); scoped to whichever of seller / buyer is present so it never double-flags a party the party-name gate (draft 261) already reports as absent; no-ops when neither trade party is present. **Draft 272 — CII country-code ISO 3166-1 validity** (`ciiCheckCountryCodes`): EN 16931 BT-40 / BT-55 — the seller and buyer country codes must be real ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 codes; reuses the shared `ISO_3166_1_ALPHA2` set (the CII analogue of `ublCheckCountryCode`); walks the seller + buyer `ram:PostalTradeAddress/ram:CountryID`, no-ops when neither carries a code (presence is draft 271's territory) and fires only on a present-but-invalid code. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 739 serial cases). The CII gate family now stands at fourteen (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown sum 268 / VAT cross-product 269 / VAT category code 270 / party countries 271 / country-code validity 272) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. **Housekeeping this cycle** — added `.github/workflows/audit.yml`, a push / pull-request / manual (`workflow_dispatch`) CI gate that re-runs `scripts/audit-release.js` server-side (Node built-ins only — no `npm install`, no browser, no network), so the 14 mechanical release-gate invariants (innerHTML hygiene, STRINGS parity, i18n key resolution, vendored SHA-384 cross-check, CSP `connect-src 'none'` + no outbound vectors, page-weight budget, locale-variant sync, robots/sitemap/JSON-LD presence, SBOM + portable-build presence, regulator-preset freshness) are enforced in GitHub Actions even when the local pre-commit hook is bypassed (`--no-verify`) or absent on a fresh clone. The Playwright end-to-end suite (~739 serial cases, `workers: 1`) and the responsive-overflow sweep stay a local / manual gate by design — too slow / browser-dependent for a per-push gate. This closes the long-standing gap that the only prior workflow (`dep-check.yml`) was a quarterly cron, so nothing ran on push. — Historical note — drafts 269 + 270 open the **CII VAT-decomposition validation surface**, extending the CII-native family to twelve gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary. With the header monetary chain reconciled end to end (drafts 259–268), these two descend into the per-rate VAT breakdown the chain's tax total rolls up from. **Draft 269 — CII VAT per-rate cross-product** (`ciiCheckVatCrossProduct`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-17 (`ublCheckTaxCrossProduct`) — on each header-level `ram:ApplicableTradeTax`, the CalculatedAmount (BT-117) must equal the BasisAmount (BT-116) × the RateApplicablePercent (BT-119) / 100; draft 268's BR-CO-14 breakdown sum verifies the per-rate amounts add up to the rolled-up header tax, but a breakdown can sum correctly while each per-rate amount is internally inconsistent with its own base and rate, which PEPPOL rejects at the BR-CO-17 schematron gate — the internal-arithmetic companion to draft 268 (mirroring how UBL draft 249 followed draft 247); scoped to direct children of `ram:ApplicableHeaderTradeSettlement`; integer-cent, ±1 cent tolerance; no-ops on any block whose BasisAmount, CalculatedAmount, or RateApplicablePercent is missing or unparsable. **Draft 270 — CII VAT category-code UNCL 5305 validity** (`ciiCheckVatCategoryCode`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-04 (`ublCheckVatCategories`) — every `ram:ApplicableTradeTax/ram:CategoryCode` (BT-118 at the header VAT-breakdown, BT-151 at the line) must be a real UNCL 5305 value (S / Z / E / AE / K / G / O / L / M); an out-of-set code is schema-valid plain text but rail-rejected and mis-bins the amount in the per-category VAT rollup; reuses the shared `UBL_VAT_CATS` set and walks every `ram:ApplicableTradeTax` (header + line) to mirror the document-wide UBL check; no-ops when no block declares a code. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 735 serial cases). The CII gate family now stands at twelve (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown sum 268 / VAT cross-product 269 / VAT category code 270) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 267 + 268 close the **CII header monetary-reconciliation surface** and add an internal VAT cross-check, extending the CII-native family to ten gates over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary. **Draft 267 — CII payable-amount reconciliation** (`ciiCheckPayable`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-16 — on `ram:SpecifiedTradeSettlementHeaderMonetarySummation`, the DuePayableAmount (BT-115) must equal the GrandTotalAmount (BT-112) − the TotalPrepaidAmount (BT-113) + the RoundingAmount (BT-114), the figure the buyer actually remits once any prepayment and cash-rounding adjustment are applied to the tax-inclusive grand total; EN 16931 types BT-113 / BT-114 as optional so an absent prepaid or rounding figure defaults to zero; integer-cent comparison, ±1 cent tolerance; no-ops when BT-112 or BT-115 is absent or a present BT-113 / BT-114 is unparsable. This closes the CII header monetary chain end to end — Σ lines → header line total (265) → tax-exclusive basis (266) → grand total (260) → due payable (267). **Draft 268 — CII VAT-breakdown sum** (`ciiCheckVatBreakdown`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-14 (`ublCheckTaxTotalBreakdownSum`) — the header TaxTotalAmount (BT-110) must equal the sum of every per-category VAT-breakdown amount (BT-117, `ram:CalculatedAmount` on each header-level `ram:ApplicableTradeTax` block); a breakdown can reconcile against the grand total (draft 260) while the per-rate amounts fail to add up to the rolled-up header tax, which PEPPOL rejects at the BR-CO-14 schematron gate; scoped to *direct children* of `ram:ApplicableHeaderTradeSettlement` so it never picks up a per-line `ram:ApplicableTradeTax` (which lives under `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeSettlement`); ±1 cent tolerance; no-ops when the header tax total is absent, there are no ApplicableTradeTax blocks, or a CalculatedAmount is unparsable. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 731 serial cases). The CII gate family now stands at ten (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266 / payable 267 / VAT-breakdown 268) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 265 + 266 complete the **CII monetary-summation arithmetic surface** that the grand-total gate (draft 260) opened, extending the CII-native family further over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary. **Draft 265 — CII line-sum reconciliation** (`ciiCheckLineSum`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-10 — the sum of every per-line net amount (BT-131, `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeSettlement/…/ram:LineTotalAmount`) must equal the header `ram:LineTotalAmount` (BT-106) on `ram:SpecifiedTradeSettlementHeaderMonetarySummation`; integer-cent comparison, ±1 cent rounding tolerance; no-ops when the header figure is absent, a per-line figure is unparsable, or there are no lines (draft 262 owns those presence defects), so it fires only when present-and-parseable figures disagree. **Draft 266 — CII tax-basis arithmetic** (`ciiCheckTaxBasis`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-13 — the TaxBasisTotalAmount (BT-109) must equal LineTotalAmount (BT-106) − AllowanceTotalAmount (BT-107) + ChargeTotalAmount (BT-108), the document-level allowance/charge net the tax-exclusive basis carries into draft 260's BR-CO-15 grand-total invariant; EN 16931 types BT-107 / BT-108 as optional so an absent total defaults to zero; ±1 cent tolerance; no-ops when BT-106 or BT-109 is absent or a present BT-107 / BT-108 is unparsable. With these the CII header monetary chain reconciles end to end — Σ lines → header line total (265) → tax-exclusive basis (266) → grand total (260) — the arithmetic spine the value- and presence-gates hang off. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (suite now 727 serial cases). The CII gate family now stands at eight (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264 / line-sum 265 / tax-basis 266). — Historical note — drafts 263 + 264 add the first **value-validation** gates to the CII-native family (the 259–262 gates were presence / arithmetic), reusing the existing shared code-list logic. **Draft 263 — CII invoice-currency ISO 4217 validity** (`ciiCheckCurrencyCode`): EN 16931 BT-5 requires `ram:InvoiceCurrencyCode` to be a real active ISO 4217 code; reuses the shared `ISO_4217_ACTIVE` set (the CII analogue of the UBL DocumentCurrencyCode check). **Draft 264 — CII document-type-code UNCL 1001 validity** (`ciiCheckTypeCode`): EN 16931 BT-3 — in CII the document purpose is conveyed entirely by `ram:ExchangedDocument/ram:TypeCode` (no Invoice / CreditNote root split as in UBL), so the allowed set is the union of the UBL invoice + credit-note type codes (380 / 381 / 384 / 389 / 875 / 876 / 877 / 396 / 532); the CII analogue of the UBL InvoiceTypeCode / CreditNoteTypeCode value check. Both no-op when their field is absent (presence is draft 259's territory). 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases. The CII gate family now stands at six (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262 / currency 263 / type code 264). A housekeeping note: the full Playwright suite (723 serial cases) ranges from ~14 min to ~1.8 h depending on host load; left at `workers: 1` deliberately — on a CPU-saturated host, more parallel browser contexts increase contention and worsen the environmental flakiness rather than help, so the retry-on-flake (`--retries=2`) approach is kept instead. — Historical note — drafts 261 + 262 extended the **CII-native conformance-gate family** (opened by drafts 259 + 260) further over the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary, wired into the CII branch after `ublRenderCII()`. **Draft 261 — CII party-name presence** (`ciiCheckPartyNames`): EN 16931 requires every Cross Industry Invoice to name its seller (BT-27, `ram:SellerTradeParty/ram:Name`) and buyer (BT-44, `ram:BuyerTradeParty/ram:Name`) — the CII analogue of the UBL BR-06 / BR-07 presence rules; flags each missing name by BT code and `ram:` path. **Draft 262 — CII line presence + per-line monetary anchor** (`ciiCheckLines`): EN 16931 BR-16 requires at least one line (`ram:IncludedSupplyChainTradeLineItem`), and each line must declare its net amount (BT-131, `ram:SpecifiedLineTradeSettlement/…/ram:LineTotalAmount`) — the per-line figure the header totals (draft 260) reconcile against; flags a no-line document, then each line missing its LineTotalAmount. 5 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 5 new Playwright cases. The CII gate family now stands at four (header presence 259 / totals 260 / party names 261 / lines 262) and continues toward parity with the UBL set. — Historical note — drafts 259 + 260 opened the **CII-native conformance-gate family**, the first checks to run on a UN/CEFACT Cross Industry Invoice (the draft-258 renderer was render-only; the ~118 `ublCheck*` gates are UBL-syntax-specific and never fire on CII). Both walk the `rsm:`/`ram:` vocabulary and are wired into the CII branch of the inspector, after `ublRenderCII()`. **Draft 259 — CII mandatory-header presence** (`ciiCheckHeaderPresence`): EN 16931 requires every Cross Industry Invoice to declare an invoice number (BT-1, `ram:ExchangedDocument/ram:ID`), an issue date (BT-2, `ram:IssueDateTime`), a document type code (BT-3, `ram:TypeCode`) and an invoice currency (BT-5, `ram:InvoiceCurrencyCode`) — the CII analogue of the UBL BR-01 / BR-02 / BR-03 / BR-05 presence rules; emits one ✓ when all four are present, or one ✗ per missing field naming its BT code and `ram:` path. **Draft 260 — CII grand-total arithmetic** (`ciiCheckTotals`): the CII analogue of UBL BR-CO-15 — on `ram:SpecifiedTradeSettlementHeaderMonetarySummation`, the GrandTotalAmount (BT-112) must equal TaxBasisTotalAmount (BT-109) + TaxTotalAmount (BT-110), integer-cent comparison with ±1 cent tolerance; no-ops when any of the three figures is absent. 4 new `cii.*` UI strings × 5 locales; 4 new Playwright cases (header presence confirm / flag-missing-BT-1; totals confirm / flag). This starts the parallel `ciiCheck*` family that will grow toward parity with the UBL gate set on the CII syntax. — Historical note — draft 258 was a **pivot from incremental conformance gates to net-new feature work**, after the gate catalogue reached saturation. The planned BR-CO-26 seller-identifier gate was investigated and **rejected as redundant** (the existing seller `PartyTaxScheme` chain — drafts 217 + 229 — already strictly requires the seller's BT-31 VAT identifier, which is one of BR-CO-26's three alternatives, so any document passing 217/229 trivially passes BR-CO-26; the "grep the existing set first" lesson catching a duplicate before it shipped, exactly as with draft 248). Draft 258 instead opens **UN/CEFACT Cross Industry Invoice (CII) support** in the §2.7 invoice inspector — the XML syntax inside Factur-X / ZUGFeRD (and Order-X) hybrid PDFs. CII carries the same EN 16931 semantic model as UBL but in a different element vocabulary (`rsm:`/`ram:` rather than UBL's `cbc:`/`cac:`). This first slice is **render-only**: `ublDetectDocType` now recognises a `<rsm:CrossIndustryInvoice>` root and dispatches to a sister walker `ublRenderCII()` that surfaces the same six row groupings as the UBL renderer — document context (`GuidelineSpecifiedDocumentContextParameter`/`ID` ≈ CustomizationID, `BusinessProcess…` ≈ ProfileID), header (`ExchangedDocument`/`ID` + `TypeCode` + `IssueDateTime`, with the `udt:DateTimeString` format-102 `CCYYMMDD` decoded to ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`), parties (`SellerTradeParty`/`BuyerTradeParty` `Name`), currency (`InvoiceCurrencyCode`), the five-figure `SpecifiedTradeSettlementHeaderMonetarySummation` (line / tax-basis / tax / grand / due-payable), and per-line `IncludedSupplyChainTradeLineItem` (LineID, product name, net price, billed quantity + unitCode, line total). It reuses the existing namespace-blind `sepaFirst`/`sepaTextOf`/`sepaAllLocal` helpers (localName ignores the prefix) and the `tlsRenderRow` row component, so it inherits the responsive layout for free. **Deferred to later slices** (documented in §2.7): hybrid PDF/A-3 attachment extraction (the `/EmbeddedFiles` walk shared with the §2.5 PAdES work — a user can paste the standalone CII XML today), CII-native EN 16931 conformance gates (the existing ~118 gates are UBL-syntax-specific and do not run on CII), and ZUGFeRD / Factur-X profile-level highlighting (Minimum / Basic WL / Basic / EN 16931 / Extended). 2 new UI strings (`ubl.row.typeCode` + an extended `err.ublNoRoot`) × 5 locales; 2 new Playwright cases (CII detection + field render; monetary summation + decoded date + line item). SEPA remains saturated. — Historical note — drafts 256 + 257 landed as a pair. Draft 256 completes the **line-level AllowanceCharge** trio (amount 254 / discriminator 255 / reason 256): line-level `<cac:InvoiceLine>/<cac:AllowanceCharge>` reason presence (BR-42 / BR-44 — every line allowance/charge must carry a reason text or reason code), the line-level analogue of the document-level draft 252. Line-level AllowanceCharge carries no VAT category (the line's ClassifiedTaxCategory governs), so the trio is the full line-level mandatory set. Draft 257 descends one level deeper into the **document-level** AllowanceCharge: `<cac:TaxCategory>/<cac:TaxScheme>/<cbc:ID>` presence (BR-CO-08 — the VAT category code gated by draft 253 must name its tax scheme, canonically "VAT", so the AP can confirm the adjustment is VAT-regime), the AllowanceCharge analogue of drafts 233 / 235. Both reuse the existing `ublLineAllowanceCharges()` / `ublDocAllowanceCharges()` collectors and no-op when their AllowanceCharge level is absent. With this the document-level AllowanceCharge surface (amount / discriminator / reason / VAT-category / VAT-scheme — drafts 250 / 251 / 252 / 253 / 257) and the line-level surface (amount / discriminator / reason — drafts 254 / 255 / 256) are both effectively complete. Remaining untouched UBL territory: PaymentTerms (BT-20) / the BR-CO-25 payable→due-date-or-terms conditional, and the document-level note (BT-22). SEPA stays saturated. Historical note — drafts 254 + 255 extended the AllowanceCharge work down to the **line level**: line-level `<cac:InvoiceLine>/<cac:AllowanceCharge>` **amount** presence (BR-41 / BR-43, BT-136 / BT-141, draft 254) and **ChargeIndicator** xs:boolean presence (draft 255), the line-level analogues of the document-level drafts 250 / 251. Each invoice-line allowance (BG-27) / charge (BG-28) feeds the PEPPOL EN16931-R120 line-net cross-product (LineExtensionAmount = price × quantity − Σ line allowances + Σ line charges), so a line block missing its amount or carrying a non-boolean ChargeIndicator breaks the line total; UBL 2.1 types both at 0..1 / mandatory-boolean so the defects are schema-valid yet rail-rejected. A new `ublLineAllowanceCharges()` helper collects these blocks (direct children of each InvoiceLine / CreditNoteLine), mirroring `ublDocAllowanceCharges()` at the line level; both gates no-op on documents with no line-level AllowanceCharge. Remaining untouched UBL territory after this: the line-level AllowanceCharge reason (BR-42 / BR-44) and the PaymentTerms / Note fields. SEPA stays saturated. Historical note — drafts 252 + 253 completed the UBL **document-level AllowanceCharge** family the 250 + 251 pair opened. The four-gate quartet now covers every EN 16931 mandatory aspect of a document-level `<cac:AllowanceCharge>` (a direct child of `<Invoice>` / `<CreditNote>`): **Amount** (BR-31 / BR-36, draft 250), **ChargeIndicator** boolean (draft 251), **reason** (BR-33 / BR-38, draft 252 — every allowance/charge must carry `<cbc:AllowanceChargeReason>` or `<cbc:AllowanceChargeReasonCode>`, the text/code that lets the buyer's AP post the adjustment to the right GL account; at least one of the two satisfies the rule), and **VAT category** (BR-32 / BR-37, draft 253 — every allowance/charge must declare a `<cac:TaxCategory>/<cbc:ID>` UNCL 5305 code so the BR-CO-18 per-category VAT rollup bins the adjustment into the right bucket). Both new gates scope to root-level `<cac:AllowanceCharge>` so they never touch a line-level cac:InvoiceLine/cac:AllowanceCharge (BR-42 / BR-44 own those), and both no-op on documents that carry no document-level AllowanceCharge. SEPA remains saturated (the draft-248 floor was withdrawn last cycle as a draft-104 duplicate), so the pair stays on the UBL side; next the line-level AllowanceCharge family (BR-41–44) and PaymentTerms / Note are the remaining untouched UBL territory before the inspectors saturate too. Historical note — drafts 250 + 251 both opened the UBL **document-level AllowanceCharge** family (`<cac:AllowanceCharge>` as a direct child of `<Invoice>` / `<CreditNote>` — BG-20 allowances / BG-21 charges). The pair shifts entirely to the UBL side because the SEPA monetary surface is saturated: draft 248 (a per-transaction €0.01 amount floor) was opened last cycle and then **withdrawn this cycle as a duplicate of the draft-104 strictly-positive amount gate** (`sepaCheckPositiveAmounts` already rejects zero and negative `<InstdAmt>` / `<Amt>`, so a separate floor gate produced a second redundant row for the same defect). The two new gates: **UBL document-level AllowanceCharge amount presence** (§2.7 polish, EN 16931 BR-31 / BR-36 — every document-level allowance BT-92 and charge BT-99 must declare its `<cbc:Amount>`, the value the BR-CO-13 invariant folds into the tax-exclusive total; UBL 2.1 types AllowanceCharge.Amount at 0..1 so a block carrying only a ChargeIndicator and a reason is schema-valid yet rail-rejected, and ublCheckTotals' BR-CO-13 silently treats the missing adjustment as zero; scoped to root-level so it never touches a line-level cac:InvoiceLine/cac:AllowanceCharge, draft 250) and **UBL document-level AllowanceCharge ChargeIndicator boolean** (§2.7 polish, UBL 2.1 / EN 16931 — `<cbc:ChargeIndicator>` is a schema-mandatory xs:boolean 1..1, the sole discriminator that tells the receiver whether the block is a charge (true → BG-21, added to the tax-exclusive total) or an allowance (false → BG-20, subtracted); a missing, empty, or non-boolean value leaves the adjustment sign undefined so BR-CO-13 cannot bin it and PEPPOL rejects at the schematron gate; the discriminator companion to draft 250's amount gate, draft 251). Both no-op safely on documents that carry no document-level AllowanceCharge. Lesson banked from the draft-248 withdrawal: before opening a gate, grep the existing `sepaCheck*` / `ublCheck*` set for an equivalent — the catalogue is dense enough that adjacent ideas collide.
Audience: maintainers and contributors
Scope: how to extend encryptalotta.com so it serves an international audience — with a deliberate emphasis on Europe and other regions that lean heavily on open-source — without compromising the site's existing character (single-page, fully client-side, no analytics, no CDN, no tracking, no build step beyond a couple of vendored scripts).

This document is a menu, not a commitment. Workstreams are independent; each section is written so it can be picked up on its own.

---

## 0. Guiding principles

These constraints come first; everything below must respect them.

1. **Client-side only.** Nothing in this spec introduces a server, a build pipeline that requires a server, or any runtime network call. Every new tool runs in the browser. Anything that cannot run in the browser is out of scope, full stop.
2. **No third-party requests.** No CDN, no fonts, no analytics, no telemetry. Vendored dependencies only. New deps must be small, MIT/BSD/Apache-2-licensed, and copy-pasteable as a single file.
3. **No fingerprinting.** Locale comes from `navigator.language` and `localStorage` only. Country is never inferred (no IP geolocation, no `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`-based country guessing). The same site is served to everyone; users opt in to a locale.
4. **No PII validators.** See [§7 Tensions](#7-tensions-and-non-goals). Commercial identifiers (IBAN, VAT, BIC, GS1) are fine. National identity numbers are not.
5. **No compliance theater.** No cookie-banner builders, no DPA templates, no "GDPR auditor" tools. The site is a technical toolbox, not a legal product.
6. **Translation quality > translation breadth.** A new locale ships only after a native reviewer has signed off on the strings that appear on screen. Machine-quality drafts are fine as scaffolding; they are not fine as a shipped locale unless explicitly labeled "machine-translated, awaiting review" in the UI.
7. **Reversibility.** Every workstream below should be revertable by deleting files. No global rewrites, no architectural commitments that lock the project in.

---

## 1. Internationalization (i18n) workstream

### 1.1 Current state (as of this writing)

- Five locales shipped: `en` (source of truth), `fr`, `zh-CN`, `de`, `hi`.
- Strings live inline in `index.html` as a single `STRINGS` object, round-tripped through `i18n/strings.csv` via `scripts/export-strings-csv.js` and `scripts/import-strings-csv.js`.
- Pre-rendered SEO variants generated by `scripts/build-i18n-variants.js` at `/fr/`, `/zh/`, `/de/`, `/hi/`.
- Auto-detection from `navigator.language` on first visit; user choice persisted in `localStorage`.
- `<link rel="alternate" hreflang>` declared for all five locales plus `x-default`.

### 1.2 Proposed locale roadmap

Locales are organized into tiers by impact-per-effort. **No locale ships until its translations have been reviewed by a native speaker who is also technically literate** (the crypto vocabulary matters).

#### Tier 1 — high impact, high familiarity (work starts here)

| Code   | Language          | Script | Direction | Plural forms | Notes |
|--------|-------------------|--------|-----------|--------------|-------|
| `es`   | Spanish (neutral) | Latin  | LTR       | 2            | Aim for a neutral/international register (avoid Iberian-only or LatAm-only idioms). Defer regional splits (`es-ES`, `es-MX`, `es-AR`) until there is clear demand. |
| `pt-BR`| Portuguese (Brazil) | Latin | LTR     | 2            | Distinct enough from `pt-PT` in tech vocabulary that one neutral `pt` is *not* a good idea. Ship `pt-BR` first (larger tech community). |
| `pt-PT`| Portuguese (Portugal) | Latin | LTR   | 2            | Add after `pt-BR`; diff against `pt-BR` is small but matters (e.g., "ficheiro" vs "arquivo"). |
| `it`   | Italian           | Latin  | LTR       | 2            | |
| `pl`   | Polish            | Latin  | LTR       | 3 (one/few/many) | Complex pluralization — use `Intl.PluralRules`. |
| `uk`   | Ukrainian         | Cyrillic | LTR     | 3            | Treat separately from `ru`. Many speakers actively avoid Russian-language tools. |
| `ar`   | Arabic (MSA)      | Arabic | **RTL**   | 6 (zero/one/two/few/many/other) | Use Modern Standard Arabic. Unlocks the RTL infrastructure for `he`, `fa`, `ur`. |

#### Tier 2 — high impact, after Tier 1 has shaken out the RTL and plural infrastructure

| Code   | Language     | Script | Direction | Plural forms | Notes |
|--------|--------------|--------|-----------|--------------|-------|
| `ru`   | Russian      | Cyrillic | LTR     | 3            | |
| `tr`   | Turkish      | Latin  | LTR       | 2            | Privacy-conscious audience. |
| `nl`   | Dutch        | Latin  | LTR       | 2            | |
| `ja`   | Japanese     | Mixed (Kanji/Kana) | LTR | 1     | No pluralization. Watch line-break behavior — set `word-break: keep-all` on CJK content. |
| `ko`   | Korean       | Hangul | LTR       | 1            | |
| `id`   | Indonesian   | Latin  | LTR       | 1            | No pluralization in the European sense. |
| `vi`   | Vietnamese   | Latin  | LTR       | 1            | Diacritics-heavy — confirm font coverage. |

#### Tier 3 — smaller audiences but high alignment with project's mission

| Code   | Language     | Script | Direction | Notes |
|--------|--------------|--------|-----------|-------|
| `sv`   | Swedish      | Latin  | LTR       | |
| `cs`   | Czech        | Latin  | LTR       | 3 plural forms |
| `he`   | Hebrew       | Hebrew | **RTL**   | Trivial once `ar` lands. |
| `fa`   | Persian/Farsi| Arabic | **RTL**   | Trivial once `ar` lands. |
| `ur`   | Urdu         | Arabic | **RTL**   | Trivial once `ar` lands; expands Hindi audience. |
| `bn`   | Bengali      | Bengali| LTR       | |
| `ta`   | Tamil        | Tamil  | LTR       | |
| `sw`   | Swahili      | Latin  | LTR       | |

#### Tier 4 — defer

Regional English variants (`en-GB`, `en-IN`) are useful for `hreflang` mapping but do not need separate string files unless date/number conventions diverge meaningfully. Use `hreflang` aliasing to point them at `en`.

`zh-TW` (Traditional Chinese) is a real audience but the diff against `zh-CN` is non-trivial (vocabulary, not just characters). Defer until a Traditional reviewer is available.

### 1.3 Translation methodology — "multiple passes"

The bar this project should hold: **a native speaker who works in tech can read the UI without wincing.**

Procedure for each new locale:

1. **Pass 1 — machine draft.** Run an LLM (or DeepL for European locales, which is genuinely strong on EU languages) against the `en` column of `strings.csv`. Mark every cell `machine` in the per-locale `_status` column.
2. **Pass 2 — glossary lock.** Before reviewing prose strings, lock the translations of the core technical glossary (see [§1.7](#17-core-glossary-translation-targets)) for that locale. Every occurrence of "encrypt", "decrypt", "key", "passphrase", etc. must use the same target term across the file. The CSV makes this a global find-and-replace; do it in one pass.
3. **Pass 3 — native review.** A native speaker who reads code goes through the CSV row by row. They flip `_status` from `machine` to `reviewed` once they're satisfied. They are explicitly allowed to mark cells `awkward` or `needs-context` and leave them for the maintainer to revisit.
4. **Pass 4 — in-context review.** Build the variant (`scripts/build-i18n-variants.js`), open `/<locale>/index.html`, click through every tool. Strings that read fine in a spreadsheet sometimes read wrong in a button. Note overflows, truncations, line-wrap problems, mismatched case (English title case doesn't transfer to most languages).
5. **Pass 5 — RTL pass (RTL locales only).** Same as Pass 4 but in the RTL build, with eyes on icon directionality (arrows, chevrons, progress indicators), tabular alignment, and any place CSS uses `left`/`right` instead of `inline-start`/`inline-end`.
6. **Pass 6 — date/number/plural smoke test.** Generate a UUID v7, decrypt a file, decode a cron expression. Confirm dates render in the locale's conventional order, decimal separators are correct, and pluralized counts agree.
7. **Sign-off.** All cells `reviewed` (or explicitly `n/a` for strings that don't apply, e.g., English-only acronyms). Add the locale to `VARIANTS` in `build-i18n-variants.js`, to the `hreflang` block in `index.html`, and to the language picker.

Anything short of this ships with a visible banner: *"This locale is machine-translated and awaiting native review — corrections welcome at [link]."*

### 1.4 RTL support (one-time architectural change) — **🟡 CSS logical-property refactor shipped (draft 13)**

**Status notes (draft 13):** Step 1 of the §1.4 plan is done. Every physical-direction CSS property in `index.html` was replaced with its logical equivalent:

- `padding-left` → `padding-inline-start` (1 site: `.home-section h3`).
- `margin-left` → `margin-inline-start` (1 stylesheet site: `.warning-box ul`; 9 inline `style="margin-left: 8px;"` button-spacers across the regex/diff/CSV/format/encode/timestamp/URL/TOTP/PEM swap buttons).
- `right: 22px` → `inset-inline-end: 22px` (1 site: `.tool-card::after` arrow indicator).
- `border-left` → `border-inline-start` (4 sites: `.alert-{warning,danger,success,info}`).
- `text-align: left` → `text-align: start` (2 sites: `.tool-card`, `.key-info-table th/td`).

A grep for `(margin|padding|border)-(left|right)|text-align:\s*(left|right)` over `index.html` now returns zero hits. The one remaining JS-set property (`wrapper.style.textAlign = 'center'` in the QR multi-share renderer) is direction-neutral and intentional.

One new Playwright case (`rtl: home tool cards mirror under dir=rtl`) flips `document.documentElement.dir = 'rtl'` at runtime, then asserts `getComputedStyle('.tool-card', '::after').insetInlineEnd === '22px'` to prove the logical property resolves correctly in RTL mode. Cleaner than a full visual diff, and the harness has no headless RTL fonts to fight with. Full suite: 215 passed.

What remains for §1.4 before an RTL locale can ship:

- Step 2 — runtime `<html dir="rtl">` flip in the `setLang()` path when an RTL locale is the active selection (one-line conditional; deferred until the first RTL locale is added to `SUPPORTED_LANGS`).
- Step 3 — icon audit: chevrons / arrows / progress indicators get `transform: scaleX(-1)` under `[dir="rtl"]` where they are direction-bearing. (The single `→` indicator on `.tool-card::after` will need this when the first RTL locale ships.)
- Step 4 — wrap `<pre>` / `<code>` / `<output>` code blocks with `direction: ltr; unicode-bidi: embed` so PGP/Base64/hex output stays LTR even inside an RTL page.
- Step 5 — `dir="ltr"` on code-like form fields (regex patterns, cron expressions, IBANs, hex).
- Step 6 — `word-break: keep-all` under `:lang(zh), :lang(ja), :lang(ko)` for CJK line-wrap behavior.

Steps 2–6 are deferred to the slice that ships the first RTL locale (Arabic, per the §1.2 roadmap), since none of them are observable until then.

Currently the CSS uses `left`/`right`/`margin-left`/`padding-right`/`text-align: left` in places. RTL support is a one-time refactor that then makes every future RTL locale free.

Concrete changes:

1. Replace physical properties with logical properties throughout the stylesheet:
   - `left` → `inset-inline-start`
   - `right` → `inset-inline-end`
   - `margin-left` → `margin-inline-start`
   - `padding-right` → `padding-inline-end`
   - `text-align: left` → `text-align: start`
   - `border-left` → `border-inline-start`
   - etc.
2. Set `<html dir="rtl">` for RTL locales, both at runtime (when the user switches) and in `build-i18n-variants.js` (so crawlers see it).
3. Audit icons: arrows ("back", "next", chevrons in collapsible sections, copy-to-clipboard) need a CSS `transform: scaleX(-1)` under `[dir="rtl"]` *unless* they are direction-agnostic (search, settings, lock).
4. Code and crypto output (Base64, hex, PGP blocks, JSON) stays LTR even inside RTL pages. Wrap with `<bdo dir="ltr">` or apply `direction: ltr; unicode-bidi: embed` to `<pre>` and `<code>` and `<output>` containers.
5. Form fields that accept code-like input (regex patterns, cron expressions, IBANs, hex) get `dir="ltr"` even in RTL contexts.
6. Verify line wrapping in CJK locales separately: set `word-break: keep-all` on CJK content (`:lang(zh), :lang(ja), :lang(ko)`) to prevent breaks mid-word.

Acceptance: the existing site rendered in `dir="rtl"` looks intentional, not mirrored-by-accident. Tested with at least Arabic and Hebrew before either locale ships.

### 1.5 Locale-aware formatting via `Intl` — **🟡 three slices shipped (drafts 11, 12, 14)**

**Status notes (draft 14):** First `Intl.ListFormat` call-site. The BIC validator's `tags` row (which surfaces "test BIC", "passive participant", "primary branch" as per-locale prose) now renders via `intlList(r.tags)` instead of `r.tags.join(', ')`. For a multi-tag BIC like `AAAADEB0XXX` (location ends in 0 → test; branch is XXX → primary) the German output becomes `Test-BIC (Ort endet auf 0) und Hauptniederlassung (XXX)` instead of `Test-BIC (Ort endet auf 0), Hauptniederlassung (XXX)`. One new Playwright case asserts the `und` connector in the de variant.

The other `.join(', ')` sites in `index.html` were considered and intentionally left alone: TLS SAN lists, CIDR / cron internal fields, and the X.509 DN parts are technical lists where a "and" / "und" final-joiner would feel wrong (they are tokens, not prose). The passphrase-strength dialog joins issue phrases that are still hardcoded English; wiring `intlList` there is a follow-up bundled with translating the issue strings themselves.

Full Playwright suite: 216 passed.

---

**Status notes (drafts 11, 12 — superseded heading kept here for diffability):**

**Status notes (draft 12):** Plural + list helpers landed.

- `intlPlural(baseKey, n, vars?)` — picks `<baseKey>.<rule>` via `Intl.PluralRules` against the active document lang, falling back through `<baseKey>.other` and then the bare `baseKey`. `n` is auto-injected as `{n}` already formatted via `intlNumber()`, so callers don't pass it twice. Engine-side fallback (`n === 1 ? 'one' : 'other'`) keeps the helper safe if Intl.PluralRules rejects the tag.
- `intlList(items, opts?)` — wraps `Intl.ListFormat` with `{type: 'conjunction', style: 'long'}` defaults. No call-sites converted in this slice — landed as scaffolding for future Shamir / multi-recipient phrasing. Falls back to a plain `, `-join on older engines.

First plural call-site: the regex tool's match-count status row. The legacy `regex.match.count: '{n} match(es)'` key (with its awkward parenthesized form) was removed across all five locales and replaced with `regex.match.count.one` / `regex.match.count.other` pairs:

- en: `1 match` / `{n} matches`
- fr: `1 correspondance` / `{n} correspondances`
- de: `{n} Treffer` (invariant — German uses the same form for both)
- zh-CN: `{n} 个匹配` (no plural distinction)
- hi: `{n} मिलान` (no plural distinction)

The call-site now reads `intlPlural('regex.match.count', matches.length)` — one argument, no manual `{n}` injection, no parenthesized fallback. Strings table grew from 1097 → 1098 keys × 5 locales (one net addition because the old single key was removed and two replacements added).

Two new Playwright cases cover the slice:

- English regex: `cat` → `1 match`; `cat cat cat` → `3 matches`.
- French regex: `chat` → `1 correspondance`; `chat chat chat` → `3 correspondances`.

Full Playwright suite: 214 passed.

---

**Status notes (draft 11):** Two small helpers landed alongside `t()` / `tFormat()`:

**Status notes (draft 11):** Two small helpers landed alongside `t()` / `tFormat()`:

- `intlNumber(n, opts?)` — wraps `Intl.NumberFormat` against `document.documentElement.lang`. Falls back to `String(n)` if Intl throws on an unknown tag.
- `intlRelativeFromNow(date)` — cascades through second/minute/hour/day/month/year buckets, then renders via `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` with `{numeric: 'auto'}` so locales that have "yesterday"/"hier"/"gestern" surface them. Falls back to the legacy `timestamp.rel.{past,future}` template strings when `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` is unavailable.

Four call-sites converted in this slice:

1. PBKDF2 derivation time (`elapsed.toFixed(0) + ' ms'` → `intlNumber(...) + ' ms'`) — French/German thousands separators now apply once derivation crosses 1 000 ms.
2. CIDR `Total addresses` / `Usable hosts` (`r.total.toLocaleString()` → `intlNumber(r.total)`) — the prior `toLocaleString()` used the browser locale; the new path uses the active document lang, so a runtime language switch immediately re-formats.
3. PGP key info `Created` / `Expires` rows now pass the explicit document lang to `Date.prototype.toLocaleString(intlLang())`.
4. Unix-timestamp `tsRelative()` now delegates to `intlRelativeFromNow()`, so a French user sees `il y a 5 minutes` and a German user sees `vor 5 Minuten` without per-locale template strings.

The legacy `timestamp.rel.{past,future}` + `timestamp.unit.*` strings are kept as the fallback path; they are no longer the primary render but still cover Safari versions that pre-date `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` (2018). Removing them is a follow-up once the fallback is no longer needed.

Three new Playwright cases cover the slice (`tests/specs/01-tools.spec.js`):

- PBKDF2 in French — derivation completes, document lang flips to `fr`.
- CIDR `10.0.0.0/8` in German — total renders as `16.777.216`.
- Timestamp tool in French — relative phrasing matches `/il y a\s+5\s+minutes/i`.

Full Playwright suite: 212 passed.

Remaining §1.5 work (deferred to a future slice): `Intl.PluralRules` integration for `{n} shares` / `{n} matches`-style strings (needs the CSV schema extension below), `Intl.ListFormat` for Shamir / multi-recipient phrasing, and `Intl.Collator` for any palette sort that lands with §1.6.

The browser's `Intl` namespace handles this without dependencies. Touch points:

- **`Intl.NumberFormat`** — Decimal commas in French/German/Italian/Spanish/Polish/etc. Affects: PBKDF2 derivation time display, file sizes, "5 minutes ago" thresholds, color converter percentages, CIDR host counts, hash collision probabilities.
- **`Intl.DateTimeFormat`** — Unix timestamp converter (already shows UTC + local + relative; add locale-formatted absolute). Cron decoder next-fire times. TLS / PGP key validity windows.
- **`Intl.RelativeTimeFormat`** — Replace the hand-rolled "5 minutes ago" with `RelativeTimeFormat`. Handles Polish/Russian/Arabic plurals correctly.
- **`Intl.PluralRules`** — Strings like `"{n} shares"`, `"{n} matches"`, `"{n} bytes"`. Use a small helper:

  ```js
  function plural(locale, n, forms) {
    const rule = new Intl.PluralRules(locale).select(n);
    return (forms[rule] || forms.other).replace('{n}', n);
  }
  // forms = { one: '{n} share', few: '{n} shares', many: '{n} shares', other: '{n} shares' }
  ```

  Strings file gets one entry per plural category, not one entry per language hard-coded. The CSV schema needs a small extension: a `key.plural.one`, `key.plural.few`, `key.plural.many`, `key.plural.other` convention. Backwards compatible — existing entries are treated as `other`.

- **`Intl.ListFormat`** — "share A, share B, and share C" / "share A, share B y share C". Surfaces in Shamir, QR multi-part output, multi-recipient encryption.
- **`Intl.Collator`** — If the command palette ever sorts strings alphabetically per locale (German ß, Swedish å, Czech č all sort differently), use `Collator`. Not urgent.

### 1.6 Command-palette synonyms — **🟡 palette + en synonyms shipped (draft 15); locale-specific synonyms deferred**

**Status notes (draft 15):** The command palette landed end-to-end:

- **UI** — a modal dialog (`#palette` / `#palette-overlay`) with overlay, accessible title (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-labelledby`), search input, and a `role="listbox"` results list. Hidden by default; CSS uses logical properties (`inset-inline`, `margin-inline: auto`) so RTL just works once an RTL locale ships. ~14 lines of CSS, ~13 lines of HTML.
- **Trigger** — `⌘K` on macOS, `Ctrl+K` elsewhere. The document-level `keydown` listener skips when `e.isComposing` so multibyte IME composition (Chinese / Japanese / Korean) isn't stolen.
- **Filter** — for each entry in `TOOL_ORDER`, the haystack is `tool-id + localized-label + English-synonyms`, all run through `toLocaleLowerCase(lang)` for locale-correct case folding (Turkish dotted i, German ß, etc.). Multiple whitespace-separated query terms are AND'd against the haystack.
- **Keyboard** — `ArrowDown` / `ArrowUp` cycle the active result; `Enter` navigates via `location.hash`; `Esc` closes; click on the overlay closes; mouse hover updates the highlight via CSS.
- **Synonyms** — `TOOL_SYNONYMS` is a flat JS object keyed by tool id, 42 entries, English only. Whitespace-separated tokens so the source reads like search keywords, not prose. The spec is emphatic that locale-specific synonyms (`chiffrer` / `cifrar` / `verschlüsseln` / `암호화` / etc.) are the single most error-prone localization gap and should ship en-only first — and that polyglot users typing English should always work in every locale. Both invariants are preserved.
- **Strings** — three new UI strings (`palette.title`, `palette.placeholder`, `palette.empty`) added to all five locales (en/fr/de/zh-CN/hi). Locked against the §1.7 glossary where the terms apply (German `Befehlspalette` / `Werkzeuge`; French `palette de commandes` / `outils`). STRINGS now 1101 keys × 5 locales (up from 1098).

Seven new Playwright cases cover the slice:

1. `Ctrl+K` opens; `Esc` closes; input is focused.
2. Synonym `"swift"` finds the BIC tool; `Enter` navigates.
3. Synonym `"epoch"` finds the timestamp tool.
4. Multi-term `"ed signature"` finds Ed25519.
5. `ArrowDown` then `Enter` selects the second match.
6. `"zzzzznotarealtool"` shows the empty state.
7. French locale + English query (`"swift"`) still matches via the en-synonym fallback (proves the polyglot invariant).

Full Playwright suite: 223 passed.

**Deferred to a follow-up:**

- **Per-locale synonyms.** The spec lists illustrative French / German / Spanish / Polish / Arabic / Japanese / Russian / Portuguese examples per tool. Implementing these is gated on §1.3 native review per locale — the failure mode (`crypter` as a French synonym for `encrypt` when `chiffrer` is the actual term used by French cryptographers; `cifrar` vs. `encriptar` for Spanish) is exactly the textbook MT failure the spec warns against. Will be added incrementally as native reviewers come online per locale.
- **Synonym schema in the CSV.** Locale-specific synonyms, when they arrive, will plug into the existing STRINGS table with a `nav.tool.<id>.synonyms` key. The audit script's en-only carve-out (currently scoped to `tool.<id>.{title,metaDescription,about.{what,how,why}}`) will be extended to allow synonyms to drop to en-only when a locale hasn't been reviewed yet — same pattern as the existing SEO prose carve-out.

The `⌘K` command palette currently matches against English tool names. A French user typing "chiffrer" finds nothing. Fix:

1. Extend the `STRINGS` schema with a per-tool `synonyms` field: a comma-separated list of terms in the current locale that should match that tool.
2. Localize the synonyms list. **This is the single highest-friction localization gap on most tools.** It is also the one most prone to machine-translation failure (machine translators don't know that `chiffrer` is "encrypt" in a crypto context — they often produce `crypter` which is a false friend).
3. Search matches against `label + synonyms + key`, all lowercased, all locale-folded (`String.prototype.toLocaleLowerCase(locale)`).
4. Always keep the English keys in the synonyms set even for non-English locales, so polyglot users can still find tools by typing English.

Concrete examples per locale (illustrative, not exhaustive — native reviewers will expand these):

- French: `encrypt` → also match `chiffrer`, `crypter` (incorrect but common), `coder`
- German: `encrypt` → also match `verschlüsseln`, `chiffrieren`
- Spanish: `encrypt` → also match `cifrar`, `encriptar`
- Polish: `encrypt` → also match `zaszyfrować`, `szyfrowanie`
- Arabic: `encrypt` → also match `تشفير`, `شفّر`
- Japanese: `encrypt` → also match `暗号化`, `あんごうか`
- Russian: `encrypt` → also match `зашифровать`, `шифрование`
- Portuguese: `encrypt` → also match `cifrar`, `criptografar`
