**Translation pitfalls observed during these passes** (notes for the maintainer):

- **Spanish "encriptar" vs "cifrar".** Both are widely used; cryptographers prefer "cifrar". Lock one project-wide. I recommend `cifrar` (the academic / industry-standard term in Spain and most LatAm cryptography literature) but include `encriptar` in the palette synonyms.
- **French "chiffrer" vs "crypter".** `chiffrer` is correct; `crypter` is a frequent calque from English and is technically wrong (in French cryptographic literature "crypter" means *to encode in an unknown cipher*). Use `chiffrer` in UI strings, include `crypter` as a palette synonym only.
- **Portuguese "criptografar" vs "cifrar".** Both used; in pt-BR `criptografar` is more common; in pt-PT `cifrar` is more common. Pick per-locale.
- **German "verschlüsseln" vs "chiffrieren".** Use `verschlüsseln` (modern); `chiffrieren` is archaic.
- **Russian/Ukrainian "ключ".** Same word as "key" in any sense (door key, musical key). Always pair with the modifier (открытый/закрытый, відкритий/закритий) in UI labels, never alone.
- **Polish verb aspect.** Polish has perfective/imperfective verb pairs. Button labels should use the perfective (`Zaszyfrować` — "to encrypt [completely]"), not imperfective (`Szyfrować` — "to be encrypting"). This is a frequent machine-translation error.
- **Arabic gendered verbs.** Imperative button labels in Arabic carry implicit gender. Use the masculine singular form by convention (this is the standard for software UI in Arabic).
- **Japanese "暗号化" vs "暗号化する".** Use the noun form (`暗号化`) for menu labels, and the verb form (`暗号化する`) only inside sentences. Buttons are typically the noun.
- **Chinese "密钥" vs "秘钥".** Both appear in the wild; `密钥` is correct and standard. `秘钥` is a common typo / non-standard variant. Audit `zh-CN` strings for this — it's easy to slip through.
- **Indonesian "berkas" vs "file".** `berkas` is the official Indonesian word but `file` is far more common in tech contexts. Pick `file` for consistency with how Indonesian developers actually speak about software. (Reverse argument: `berkas` is more accessible to non-technical users. Native reviewer to decide.)
- **Turkish vowel harmony.** Suffixes attached to dynamic content (e.g., `{filename}'i indir`) need to obey vowel harmony, which can't be done with simple string interpolation. Prefer rewording (`İndir: {filename}`) over interpolated suffixes.

These notes are explicitly the kind of thing that takes multiple passes — Pass 1 (machine) will get every one of them wrong; Pass 3 (native review) is where they get caught.

### 1.8 Per-locale CSS / typography checklist

- **Latin-script locales:** existing fonts sufficient (system stack).
- **Cyrillic (`ru`, `uk`):** confirm system fonts on macOS, Windows, and Linux cover Cyrillic glyphs at all weights. They do.
- **Arabic (`ar`):** RTL pass + line-height bump (Arabic glyphs sit lower). Avoid italics — Arabic doesn't italicize.
- **CJK (`zh`, `ja`, `ko`):** `word-break: keep-all`, slight line-height bump. Avoid all-caps styling.
- **Indic (`hi`, `bn`, `ta`):** confirm conjunct rendering. macOS and Android handle this well; older Windows fonts can break Bengali ligatures. No fix in CSS — note it in the README.
- **Vietnamese (`vi`):** diacritics stack tall; line-height bump.

---

## 2. New tools — Europe-resonant additions

Each tool below follows the existing project pattern: pure browser code, zero network, vendored deps only, ASCII-armored or hex output where applicable, constant-time comparisons where they matter.

### 2.1 IBAN validator / generator — **✅ shipped (draft 2)**

**Why:** Every European has typed an IBAN wrong. The tool gives instant feedback on a string that is otherwise opaque.

**Status notes (draft 2):** Landed as the `iban` tool with full English, French, and German UI strings (banking terms locked against the §1.7 glossary). zh-CN and hi have machine-quality drafts pending native review. Country table covers ~80 IBAN-issuing jurisdictions; BBAN sub-fields surfaced where the registry defines them. Generator uses `crypto.getRandomValues` for the BBAN and is labeled "For test data only." Functional tests cover the canonical ECBS example (`DE89 3704 0044 0532 0130 00`), a flipped-digit negative case, and a generator → validator round-trip.

**Spec:**
- Input: a string. Tolerate spaces, dashes, lowercase.
- Validate:
  1. Strip non-alphanumerics, uppercase.
  2. First two chars are an ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country code with a known IBAN length (table of ~80 countries, ship inline).
  3. Total length matches that country's expected length.
  4. MOD-97 checksum: rearrange `CCKK BBAN` → `BBAN CCKK`, map letters A-Z → 10-35, compute the resulting integer modulo 97, expect 1.
- Output:
  - Pass/fail with reason.
  - Country, bank code, account number, branch code parsed from the BBAN per the country's structure (these subfields vary; ship a per-country regex/structure table).
  - Pretty-printed form (groups of 4).
- Generator side: produce a *valid* IBAN for a given country and BBAN (fill in the two checksum digits using MOD-97). Useful for test data. Label it clearly: **"For test data. Do not use for real transactions."**

**Dependencies:** none. Hand-written, ~120 lines.

**Security notes:** IBANs are commercial identifiers, not PII in any meaningful sense, but the tool should still process input only locally. Pasted IBANs should never appear in the URL, in `localStorage`, or in `document.title`.

### 2.2 BIC / SWIFT code checker — **✅ shipped (draft 2)**

**Why:** Companion to IBAN. ISO 9362 format check.

**Status notes (draft 2):** Landed as the `bic` tool. Validates 8- and 11-character forms, decodes bank / country / location / branch, cross-checks the country segment against the IBAN country table, and flags conventional markers (`0`-terminated → test BIC; `1`-terminated → passive participant; branch `XXX` → primary). en/fr/de translations reviewed against standard banking terminology; zh-CN/hi are machine-quality drafts.

**Spec:**
- Format: `BBBB CC LL [XXX]` — 4 bank, 2 country (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2), 2 location, optional 3 branch.
- Validate length (8 or 11), uppercase, alphanumeric, valid country code, location code rules (second character `0` = test BIC, etc.).
- No checksum (BIC has no checksum), but expose the parsed components and flag test BICs (location code ends in `0`).

**Dependencies:** none.

### 2.3 EU VAT number validator (offline) — **✅ shipped (draft 3)**

**Why:** Catches 90% of typos without ever touching VIES.

**Status notes (draft 3):** Landed as the `vat` tool. Format check for all 27 EU member states + GB, NO, CH, XI (Northern Ireland). Checksum implemented for AT, BE, DE, DK, EE, EL, ES (CIF only), FI, FR, HR, HU, IT, LU, MT, PL, PT, SE, SI, SK, CH, NO (≈ 70% of supported set). Countries without a published or stable algorithm (BG, CY, CZ, GB legacy, IE, LT, LV, NL post-2020, RO, XI) report "format only" without claiming more. ES support is intentionally limited to the CIF subset; individual NIF (`12345678Z`-style) and NIE (`X1234567Z`-style) inputs are explicitly rejected with a message pointing at the spec, satisfying the §7.1 tension constraint. en/fr/de translations locked against standard tax terminology (German `USt-IdNr.`, French `TVA`); zh-CN/hi are machine-quality drafts. Functional tests cover real public VAT numbers from DE/FR/IT (Ferrero `IT00159560366`), the NL "format-only" path, the ES NIF rejection, and a DE flipped-digit negative case.

**Spec:**
- Per-country format and checksum table for all 27 EU member states + GB, NO, CH (close enough to ship together).
- Each country has a different algorithm:
  - DE: 9 digits, weighted checksum.
  - FR: 2-char prefix + 9 digits, MOD-97 derived from the SIREN.
  - IT: 11 digits, Luhn-style checksum.
  - ES: 1 letter + 7 digits + 1 letter/digit, several sub-formats (NIF, NIE, CIF).
  - NL: 9 digits + B + 2 digits, no checksum.
  - PL: 10 digits, weighted checksum.
  - etc.
- Output: pass/fail per the format and (where one exists) per the checksum, plus a clearly-labeled "this is a syntactic check — to verify the VAT number is actively registered, use VIES at the EU Commission's website" disclaimer with a link out.

**Dependencies:** none. ~300 lines of country-specific code.

**Tension flag:** ES NIF/NIE overlap with personal tax IDs. **Validate VAT formats only**, never NIF/NIE for individuals. The CIF (corporate) format is fine. This is one of the rare cases where the line between "commercial identifier" and "PII" is fuzzy — when in doubt, omit the country.

per-DrctDbtTxInf <DbtrAgt>/<FinInstnId> routing-identifier content presence on pain.008 — the debtor-bank FinInstnId (AT-06 SDD) must carry BIC/BICFI, ClrSysMmbId or Othr and not a name/address alone, the third FinInstnId-content position, mirror of drafts 238 / 240 on the SDD debtor side, one level deeper than the draft-214 wrapper gate (draft 242); per-PmtInf <CdtrAgt>/<FinInstnId> routing-identifier content presence on pain.008 — the creditor-bank FinInstnId (AT-12 SDD) must carry BIC/BICFI, ClrSysMmbId or Othr and not a name/address alone, the fourth and final FinInstnId-content position, mirror of draft 218’s wrapper gate, completing the 238 / 240 / 242 / 244 content quartet (draft 244); per-transaction <InstdAmt> / <Amt> EPC SEPA amount cap of €999,999,999.99 on pain.001 / pain.008 — the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook (AT-04) and the SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook (AT-06) both cap a single instruction at 999,999,999.99 euro, the largest value the scheme settles as one inter-bank position; ISO 20022 types the amount as ActiveOrHistoricCurrencyAndAmount with totalDigits 18 so 1,000,000,000.00 is schema-valid yet rail-rejected, the order-of-magnitude companion to the draft-130 2-decimal precision cap, integer-cent comparison over the same CdtTrfTxInf / DrctDbtTxInf InstdAmt / Amt nodes (draft 246); draft 248 (per-transaction €0.01 amount floor) was opened then withdrawn as a duplicate of the draft-104 strictly-positive amount gate (`sepaCheckPositiveAmounts`, which already rejects zero and negative InstdAmt / Amt) — the SEPA monetary surface is saturated, so the 250 / 251 pair shifts to the previously untouched UBL document-level AllowanceCharge family**

**Status notes (draft 16):** Landed as the `sepa` tool. The parser is namespace-blind — it walks ISO 20022 XML by `localName` only, so the same code handles `pain.001.001.03`, `pain.001.001.09`, `camt.053.001.02`, `camt.053.001.08`, and any other minor revision without per-version branching.

- **pain.001 + pain.008**: header row (MsgId, CreDtTm, InitgPty, NbOfTxs, CtrlSum) + per-`PmtInf` debtor account/IBAN/BIC, then per-transaction (`CdtTrfTxInf` / `DrctDbtTxInf`) end-to-end ID, amount + currency (from `Amt/InstdAmt`'s `Ccy` attribute), debtor + creditor names with their IBANs in parens, remittance info.
- **camt.053**: statement ID, account IBAN, FrToDt window, every `<Bal>` (opening `OPBD` and closing `CLBD` labeled explicitly via the existing strings table; other balance-type codes fall through with their raw 4-letter code), then per-`Ntry` booking date, amount, credit/debit indicator.
- **IBAN cross-check**: every `<IBAN>` element anywhere in the document is run through the existing `ibanValidate()` (the same MOD-97 implementation that powers the IBAN tool). When all checksums pass, a single confirmation row is rendered (`IBAN MOD-97 valid (N)`); on a failure, each bad IBAN gets its own warning row.
- **Unrecognized roots**: a `<Document>` whose first child isn't one of the three known wrappers still renders the header (with the unknown local name as the document type) and then emits a `sepa.warn.unknownDoc` warning rather than refusing the file outright.

UI: a single textarea + Parse button, mirrors the existing IBAN / BIC / VAT layout. No vendored deps — `DOMParser` only. ~200 lines of parser + render JS, ~12 new strings × 5 locales (en + fr + de reviewed against standard banking terminology; zh-CN + hi are machine-quality drafts per the project pattern). 5 SEO prose keys (`tool.sepa.{title,metaDescription,about.{what,how,why}}`) en-only per the existing Phase 7 carve-out.

Four new Playwright cases:

1. pain.001 — parses headers, transactions, both IBANs, and renders `100.00 EUR` + `Invoice 2024-001`.
2. camt.053 — parses statement ID, opening / closing balance, entry amounts.
3. Flipping the last digit of the debtor IBAN — surfaces a MOD-97 warning row with the bad IBAN inline.
4. Non-ISO-20022 XML (`<foo><bar/></foo>`) — reports `No <Document> root element`.

Full Playwright suite: 227 passed. Tool count 42 → 43; JSON-LD `featureList_count`, JSON-LD description, meta description, og:description, twitter:description, and README all updated.

**What's not in this slice:** XAdES-signed SEPA files (the embedded `<ds:Signature>` inside pain.001 production files is parsed *around* by the namespace-blind walker, not verified — that's §2.5 territory); SEPA card schemes (`acmt.*`, `caaa.*`).

**Draft 22 polish:** per-PmtInf `<ReqdExctnDt>` (requested execution date, both schema shapes — bare element value for pain.001.001.03 and the wrapped `<Dt>` child form for .09+) is now surfaced as a header row above the transactions for each `<PmtInf>` group. Per-transaction `<MndtId>` is surfaced for pain.008 direct debits (sourced from `DrctDbtTx/MndtRltdInf/MndtId`); it only appears on transactions that carry one, so pain.001 credit transfers are unaffected. Two new strings (`sepa.row.reqdExctnDt`, `sepa.tx.mndtId`) × 5 locales. Two new Playwright cases (ReqdExctnDt date row, pain.008 MndtId row).

**Draft 19 polish:** schema-version detection landed — `sepaDetectDocType()` now reads the `Document`/wrapper `namespaceURI`, extracts the trailing `<msg>.<variant>.<version>` triple (e.g. `pain.001.001.03`), and surfaces it inline in the document-type row so users can distinguish pain.001.001.03 from .09 at a glance. The fallback when the namespace URI is absent or non-matching is the friendly mnemonic alone, so unknown / handwritten inputs degrade cleanly. Per-transaction `<InstrId>` is now surfaced above `<EndToEndId>` (mirroring the order banks emit them); one new UI string `sepa.tx.instrId` × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against the same banking-terminology lock as the rest of the SEPA strings; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review, consistent with the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (`pain.001.001.03` banner; InstrId surfaced alongside EndToEndId). No new vendored deps, no CSP change, no tool-count change.

**Why:** Finance/accounting developers across the EU need to debug payment files. No good browser-based tool exists.

**Spec:**
- Accept SEPA ISO 20022 XML: `pain.001.001.{03,09}` (credit transfer initiation), `pain.008.001.{02,08}` (direct debit initiation), `camt.053.001.{02,08}` (bank-to-customer statement).
- Parse with `DOMParser` (no third-party XML library — same pattern as the existing JSON/YAML/XML formatter).
- Render:
  - Header: message ID, creation datetime, initiating party, number of transactions.
  - Per-transaction table: end-to-end ID, amount + currency, debtor/creditor name + IBAN/BIC, remittance info, scheduled date.
  - Validation: schema-level sanity (required elements present), IBAN MOD-97 on each party (reuse §2.1), currency code is ISO 4217.
- Output: a pretty HTML table; one-click copy as CSV.

**Dependencies:** none (DOMParser is built in).

### 2.5 eIDAS digital signature inspector — PAdES / CAdES / XAdES — **✅ feature-complete inside the X.509 parser: CAdES / PKCS#7 (draft 284), PAdES signed-PDF (draft 285), XAdES / XML-DSig (draft 286)**

**Status notes (drafts 284–286):** Landed as an extension of the existing TLS / X.509 Certificate Parser (the spec's prescribed home — "extend the existing X.509 parser"), so all three signature families reuse the one ASN.1 / X.509 engine the project already ships. **CAdES (284):** detects a PKCS#7 / CMS `ContentInfo` whose `contentType` is `signedData` and renders a summary (embedded-cert + signer counts), every embedded X.509 certificate via the shared cert renderer, and per-`SignerInfo` digest algorithm / signature algorithm / signed `signingTime`; certs are sliced out of `certificates [0]` by re-walking the field at tracked offsets, leaving `asn1Read` unchanged. **PAdES (285):** a signed-PDF file input — `pdfExtractSignatures()` finds each hex-string `/Contents` in the PDF's signature dictionaries, hex-decodes it, keeps the CMS SignedData blobs, and feeds each to the CAdES decoder via the shared `renderCmsInto()` (reusing the draft-283 Factur-X byte-walker). **XAdES (286):** a pasted XML signature — `xadesSignatures()` collects every `<ds:Signature>` via `DOMParser`, and `renderXadesInto()` surfaces the XAdES level (B-B / B-T / B-LT / B-LTA from the qualifying properties present), signature + canonicalization methods, per-Reference digests, `SigningTime`, and each embedded `<ds:X509Certificate>` via the shared cert renderer. No network, no signature-cryptographic verification — the tool reports structure + signer identity, not a validity verdict (per §2.5's "no revocation checking against the network").

**Why:** EU has invested heavily in eIDAS qualified signatures. Free inspector tools are scarce, server-based, or paywalled.

**Spec:**
- **PAdES** (PDF signatures): parse the PDF, extract the `/Sig` dictionary, extract the embedded CMS `Contents` blob, hand off to the CAdES parser.
- **CAdES** (CMS / RFC 5652 + extensions): ASN.1 / DER parser (extend the existing X.509 parser — it already does ASN.1). Surface: signer info, signing certificate (chain if embedded), signing time, signature algorithm, content hash, attribute certificates, timestamp token (RFC 3161) if present, OCSP/CRL revocation info if embedded.
- **XAdES** (XML signatures): `DOMParser`-based. Surface the same fields. XAdES levels (B, T, LT, LTA) are detectable from the embedded properties.
- Output: a structured view of every property; raw ASN.1 / XML toggle for the curious.
- **No revocation checking against the network.** Tool reports what's in the signature; it does not contact OCSP responders or CRL endpoints.

**Dependencies:** none (extends the existing ASN.1 parser, reuses `DOMParser`).

**This is the largest tool in this spec. Plan ~1,500 lines and a multi-week effort.**

### 2.6 EU LOTL trust list viewer — **✅ shipped (draft 17)**

**Status notes (draft 17):** Landed as the `lotl` tool. The parser walks ETSI TS 119 612 XML by `localName` only, so the same code handles every minor revision (v2.1.1, v2.2.1, ...) without per-version branching.

- **LOTL mode** (`TSLType` ends in `EUlistofthelists`): renders the scheme operator + name + territory + issue/next-update dates, then one section per `<OtherTSLPointer>` with the country code (mined from `OtherInformation/SchemeTerritory`) and the `<TSLLocation>` URL.
- **TSL mode** (`TSLType` ends in `schemes/...`): renders the scheme metadata, then one section per `<TrustServiceProvider>` showing the TSP name, followed by each `<TSPService>` with type identifier (e.g. `Svctype/CA/QC`), status (e.g. `Svcstatus/granted`), and status starting time. Embedded `<X509Certificate>` blocks are acknowledged as present with their base64 byte count, but not parsed inline — the TLS Certificate Parser tool handles that if the user wants details.
- **Multi-language `<Name>` blocks**: ETSI lists carry `xml:lang`-tagged `<Name>` children inside `<SchemeName>`, `<SchemeOperatorName>`, `<TSPName>`, etc. The viewer prefers `xml:lang="en"` and falls back to the first child if no English variant is present.
- **No fetching**: the §0.2 / §2.6 "no network" rule is preserved — every parse run appends a footer note pointing the user to `ec.europa.eu` for the official LOTL file. No automatic resolution of `<TSLLocation>` URLs.

UI mirrors the SEPA tool layout (textarea + Parse button). ~190 lines of parser + render JS, ~25 new UI strings × 5 locales (en + fr + de reviewed against standard PKI/eIDAS terminology; zh-CN + hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). 5 SEO prose keys en-only via the existing Phase 7 carve-out.

Three new Playwright cases:

1. LOTL mode — both German and French pointer URLs surface, both country codes appear.
2. TSL mode — Bundesnetzagentur (scheme operator), D-TRUST GmbH (TSP), CA/QC service type, "granted" status, and the embedded X.509 acknowledgement row.
3. Non-trust-list XML (a bare `<Document/>`) gets rejected with a `TrustServiceStatusList` mention in the error.

Full Playwright suite: 230 passed. Tool count 43 → 44; JSON-LD featureList + featureList_count, meta / og / twitter descriptions, and README updated.

**Why:** EU publishes a "List of Trusted Lists" — a master XML pointing to each member state's trust list of qualified certificate authorities. Useful to anyone debugging eIDAS-signed documents. Almost no free tool surfaces this nicely.

**Spec:**
- The user pastes a Trust Service Status List (TSL) XML file or a LOTL XML.
- Parse with `DOMParser`.
- Render: per-country sections, per-service entries (CA name, service type, status, service supply points, qualifiers).
- For each embedded certificate, link to the existing X.509 parser (same site).
- **No fetching.** User downloads the LOTL from the EU Commission's published URL themselves and pastes it. This is a non-negotiable consequence of the "no network" rule. Document the source URL in the tool's help text.

**Dependencies:** none.

**Status notes (draft 18):** Landed as the `ubl` tool. Namespace-blind `DOMParser` walk by `localName` handles every OASIS UBL 2.1 minor revision and the PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 customization without per-version branching. Both root document types are detected: `<Invoice>` and `<CreditNote>` (the credit-note renderer reuses the same line-item path but pulls `CreditNoteLine` / `CreditedQuantity` instead of `InvoiceLine` / `InvoicedQuantity`). Surfaced fields: `CustomizationID`, `ProfileID`, invoice number, issue and due dates, document currency, supplier / customer name (`PartyName/Name` preferred, falls back to `PartyLegalEntity/RegistrationName` as it is in most real PEPPOL invoices), the four `LegalMonetaryTotal` amounts (line-extension, tax-exclusive, tax-inclusive, payable), per-line item (quantity with `unitCode`, unit price, item name, line amount), and per-rate `TaxSubtotal` breakdown (category ID + scheme + percent + taxable amount + tax amount). All currency-tagged amounts render with their `currencyID` (e.g. "119.00 EUR"). No vendored deps — the parser reuses the existing `sepaFirst` / `sepaTextOf` / `sepaAllLocal` helpers as generic namespace-blind XML walkers. ~160 lines of parser + render JS, ~32 new UI strings × 5 locales (en + fr + de reviewed against standard e-invoicing terminology; zh-CN + hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern), 5 SEO prose keys en-only via the existing Phase 7 carve-out. Tool count 44 → 45; JSON-LD `featureList` + `featureList_count`, meta / og / twitter descriptions, and README all updated. Three new Playwright cases (Invoice happy path with line + tax breakdown, CreditNote happy path, non-UBL XML rejected).

**Draft 29 polish:** the inspector now also cross-checks every `Ccy="..."` attribute against the ISO 4217:2015 (Amendment 174) active-currencies set (inline, ≈ 180 codes). Confirmation row uses `intlList()` so the locale-correct joiner ("and" / "und" / "et") is applied; warning row per offending code. Historic codes are deliberately rejected so the cleanup task is visible.

**Draft 27 polish:** the inspector now cross-checks every `<BIC>` and `<BICFI>` element against ISO 9362 (`bicValidate()` from the standalone BIC tool), parallel to the existing IBAN MOD-97 walk. One confirmation row on full success, one warning row per bad BIC on failure. Catches typos that the IBAN check wouldn't surface (e.g. a digit-bearing first segment, lowercase characters, an 11-char BIC missing its branch). 4 new strings × 5 locales; 2 new Playwright cases.

**Draft 28 polish:** the inspector now surfaces an EN 16931 totals consistency check after parsing — ✓ rows when both invariants hold, ✗ rows with computed vs declared values on mismatch. Two invariants: Σ line LineExtensionAmount = declared LineExtensionAmount; TaxExclusive + Σ TaxAmount = TaxInclusive. Integer-cents arithmetic with ±1¢ rounding tolerance.

**Draft 22 polish:** the line-items table now ships with a "Copy as CSV" button. The export rows are emitted alongside the on-screen rendering (same parse, same `<InvoiceLine>` / `<CreditNoteLine>` walk) and the column shape is fixed: `#,Name,Quantity,UnitCode,Price,PriceCurrency,LineAmount,LineCurrency`. Quoting follows RFC 4180 — fields with `,`, `"`, or newline are double-quoted with embedded `"` doubled up; empty cells pass through unquoted. The button label gives the user a `✓ Copied` confirmation for 1.5 s after the click. Four new strings (`ubl.lines.copyCsv` + `copied` + `copyFail` + the existing `lines.heading` styling tweak) × 5 locales. One new Playwright case (asserts the CSV column header and the first line item row byte-for-byte against the test fixture).

**Progress (draft 258): CII XML rendering shipped.** A user can now paste a UN/CEFACT **Cross Industry Invoice** (the XML inside a Factur-X / ZUGFeRD / Order-X hybrid PDF) directly into the inspector and get a structured render. `ublDetectDocType` recognises a `<rsm:CrossIndustryInvoice>` root and dispatches to a sister walker `ublRenderCII()`, which surfaces the same six row groupings as the UBL renderer — document context (`GuidelineSpecifiedDocumentContextParameter`/`ID` ≈ CustomizationID; `BusinessProcessSpecifiedDocumentContextParameter`/`ID` ≈ ProfileID), header (`ExchangedDocument`/`ID` + `TypeCode` + `IssueDateTime`, the `udt:DateTimeString` format-102 `CCYYMMDD` decoded to ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`), parties (`SellerTradeParty`/`BuyerTradeParty` `Name`), currency (`InvoiceCurrencyCode`), the five-figure `SpecifiedTradeSettlementHeaderMonetarySummation` (line / tax-basis / tax / grand / due-payable), and per-line `IncludedSupplyChainTradeLineItem` (LineID, product name, net price, billed quantity + unitCode, line total). The walker reuses the existing namespace-blind `sepaFirst`/`sepaTextOf`/`sepaAllLocal` helpers (localName ignores the `rsm:`/`ram:` prefix) and the `tlsRenderRow` row component, so it inherits the responsive layout. 2 new UI strings (`ubl.row.typeCode` + an extended `err.ublNoRoot` that now names `<CrossIndustryInvoice>`) × 5 locales; 2 new Playwright cases. **Still deferred:** (a) *hybrid PDF/A-3 attachment extraction* — pulling the embedded CII XML out of the carrier PDF needs the same `/EmbeddedFiles` walk that is a §2.5 PAdES dependency; until it lands, the user extracts the XML themselves (or pastes the standalone `factur-x.xml` / `zugferd-invoice.xml`); (b) *CII-native EN 16931 conformance gates* — the ~118 UBL gates are syntax-specific (`cbc:`/`cac:` element names) and do not run on CII, so a parallel `ciiCheck*` family is being built on the `rsm:`/`ram:` syntax; eight landed so far (mandatory-header presence BT-1/2/3/5, draft 259; grand-total arithmetic BR-CO-15 analogue, draft 260; party-name presence BT-27 / BT-44, draft 261; line presence BR-16 + per-line LineTotalAmount BT-131, draft 262; invoice-currency ISO 4217 validity BT-5, draft 263; document-type-code UNCL 1001 validity BT-3, draft 264; line-sum reconciliation Σ per-line BT-131 = header BT-106, BR-CO-10 analogue, draft 265; tax-basis arithmetic BT-109 = BT-106 − BT-107 + BT-108, BR-CO-13 analogue, draft 266 — the 260 / 265 / 266 trio reconciles the CII header monetary chain end to end) and the family will grow toward parity with the UBL set; (c) *profile / conformance-level highlighting* (Minimum / Basic WL / Basic / EN 16931 / Extended for ZUGFeRD; Minimum / Basic WL / Basic / EN 16931 for Factur-X). CSV export of CII line items deferred until requested — the UBL CSV path is `<InvoiceLine>`-shaped and the CII line walk differs.

**Why:** German and French e-invoicing rules mandate one of these formats by 2026–2027. Many devs will be debugging invoice XML for years.

**Spec:**
- **PEPPOL UBL** (`Invoice`/`CreditNote` XML, OASIS UBL 2.1): parse, render header (supplier, customer, totals, currency, due date), line items table, tax breakdown.
- **Factur-X / ZUGFeRD** (hybrid PDF/A-3 with embedded CII XML): extract the embedded XML attachment from the PDF, then parse as CII (UN/CEFACT Cross Industry Invoice). Same renderer otherwise.
- Highlight which profile/conformance level the document claims (Minimum / Basic WL / Basic / EN 16931 / Extended for ZUGFeRD; equivalent for Factur-X).
- Output: structured view + one-click CSV export of line items.

**Dependencies:** none (DOMParser + extending PDF parsing already in §2.5).

### 2.8 EUDI Wallet credential decoder (SD-JWT and ISO mdoc) — **✅ feature-complete: SD-JWT (drafts 20–24) + ISO 18013-5 mdoc (draft 289)**

**Status notes (draft 20):** SD-JWT support landed as an extension of the existing JWT inspector (no new top-level tool, no tool-count change — per the §2.8 spec's "already partly handled by the existing JWT inspector" framing). The JWT input field now detects the SD-JWT `~`-separator and:

- Splits the input into a leading JWT, zero or more disclosures, and an optional trailing key-binding JWT (KB-JWT detected by `dot.dot.dot` shape of the final segment).
- Decodes each disclosure as base64url → UTF-8 → JSON. Recognizes the two disclosure shapes from draft-ietf-oauth-selective-disclosure-jwt §5.2: `[salt, name, value]` for object claims and `[salt, value]` for array elements.
- Computes the SHA-256 digest over the wire-bytes of the disclosure (the literal base64url ASCII bytes, per §5.1.1) — not over the decoded JSON. The `_sd_alg` payload claim is honored (defaults to `sha-256` per §5.1).
- Walks the entire payload tree to collect every `_sd` digest array (top-level and nested under `vc`, `address`, etc.), then per-disclosure shows `✓ referenced in payload _sd` when the computed digest is present and `✗ unreferenced` when it is orphaned.
- Surfaces a present KB-JWT as a separate decoded JSON block (header + payload pretty-printed). Signature verification of the KB-JWT is out of scope for this slice — the existing JWT verify path covers the leading SD-JWT issuer signature, and the KB-JWT verification flow (audience, nonce, holder key resolution) is large enough to warrant its own slice if/when there's demand.

10 new UI strings (`jwt.sdjwt.{heading,colName,colValue,colSalt,colDigest,matched,orphan,arrayElem,kbJwt,parseErr}`) × 5 locales — en/fr/de reviewed against the SD-JWT draft's own vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern. Three new Playwright cases (digest-matched disclosures, orphaned disclosure flagged, KB-JWT surfaced). Full suite: 235 → 238 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change.

**Status notes (draft 21):** Two follow-ups landed.

- **Cleartext-payload reconstruction (SD-JWT draft §7).** After the disclosure table, the inspector now renders a `Disclosed payload (reconstructed)` block: every `_sd` digest that has a matching disclosure is replaced by the disclosed claim name + value; array `{"...": digest}` placeholders are replaced by the disclosed array element; `_sd` and `_sd_alg` are stripped. Digests *without* a matching disclosure are dropped — that is what a verifier sees when the holder withholds a claim, so the rendered output is exactly the JSON the verifier would consume. One new UI string (`jwt.sdjwt.reconstructed`) × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). One new Playwright case (reconstructs two disclosed claims, confirms `_sd` / `_sd_alg` are stripped). Full suite: 238 → 239 passed.
- **`bytesToBase64()` consolidation.** The file used to ship two declarations — the second one (no `urlSafe` parameter) hoisted over the first, silently disabling base64url output for any caller below the duplicate. The redundant second declaration was deleted; sdJwtRender now calls the canonical helper instead of carrying its own inline base64url loop. PBKDF2 base64 output (the only caller of the second form) is unaffected because the first signature treats `urlSafe === undefined` as falsy, returning the same plain base64 the second form produced. Audit + a11y + full Playwright suite all green.

**Status notes (draft 23):** KB-JWT signature verification + `sd_hash` binding check landed.

- When the input is an SD-JWT with a trailing key-binding JWT and the issuer-signed payload declares a `cnf.jwk`, the inspector now imports that JWK via Web Crypto (`sdJwtImportJwk()` maps EC P-256 → ES256, EC P-384 → ES384, OKP Ed25519 → EdDSA, RSA → RS256/RS384/RS512), verifies the KB-JWT signature over `<kb-header>.<kb-payload>`, and renders a `KB-JWT signature ✓ verified with cnf.jwk (ES256)` / `✗ signature does not verify` row. Missing `cnf.jwk` is surfaced as an explicit "no cnf.jwk … cannot verify" row rather than a silent skip.
- The KB-JWT payload's `sd_hash` claim is recomputed at decode time: `base64url(SHA-256(<jwt>~<disc1>~…~<discN>~))` — the literal presentation bytes up to and including the final tilde but excluding the KB-JWT itself, per SD-JWT draft §4.3. Mismatch (substituted disclosures or a swapped KB-JWT) renders an explicit "does not match" row.
- Seven new UI strings (`jwt.sdjwt.{kbSig,kbSigOk,kbSigFail,kbNoCnf,sdHash,sdHashOk,sdHashFail}`) × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against the SD-JWT draft's vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). Two new Playwright cases (ES256 KB-JWT with on-the-fly node-generated P-256 keypair: happy-path verify + tampered-signature rejection). Full suite: 242 → 244 passed.

**Status notes (draft 24):** KB-JWT verifier-side `aud` / `nonce` cross-checks landed. A collapsible `<details>` panel under the JWT input now hosts two optional inputs (`#jwt-kb-aud`, `#jwt-kb-nonce`). When either is filled, sdJwtRender appends an explicit row comparing the value against the KB-JWT payload's `aud` (string or array per RFC 7519) / `nonce` claim — ✓ on match, ✗ with the received value on mismatch, and an explicit "no aud/nonce claim in KB-JWT" row when the claim is absent so a verifier never silently skips a check. Inputs are intentionally not persisted (no localStorage, no URL params) — they are per-decode replay-defense values, not user preferences. 13 new UI strings (`jwt.sdjwt.kbExpect{Summary,AudLabel,AudPh,NonceLabel,NoncePh}` + `jwt.sdjwt.kb{Aud,AudOk,AudFail,AudMissing,Nonce,NonceOk,NonceFail,NonceMissing}`) × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against the SD-JWT draft + RFC 7519 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Three new Playwright cases (aud match, aud mismatch surfaces received value, nonce mismatch surfaces received value). Full suite: 244 → 247 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Status notes (draft 289):** The **ISO mdoc decoder shipped** as a third branch of the JWT / credential inspector (alongside JWT and SD-JWT), so §2.8 is feature-complete. Input is detected as mdoc when it has no JWT/SD-JWT separators and decodes to a CBOR `DeviceResponse` / `Document` / `IssuerSigned`; `mdocRender()` walks docType → per-namespace data elements (tag-24 IssuerSignedItems) → the COSE_Sign1 `issuerAuth` → the tag-24 `MobileSecurityObject` (digest algorithm, validity, value-digest count, device key, COSE alg), and decodes the x5chain issuer certificate via the X.509 parser. Decode-only; no issuer-trust-anchor verification. Reuses the draft-287/288 CBOR decoder and the cert renderer — no new dependency.

**Historical note (the original deferral):**
- **ISO mdoc decoder.** The CBOR-decoder prerequisite **shipped in draft 287** — a hand-rolled RFC 8949 decoder exposed as the `CBOR (hex / base64)` input format on the Format & Convert tool (it also covers COSE / CWT / WebAuthn-attestation decoding generally). Draft 288 then made the decoder credential-aware (recursive tag-24 decode, COSE / CWT tag naming, bignums), so a pasted mdoc CBOR already decodes end to end — COSE_Sign1 `issuerAuth` is labelled and the tag-24 `MobileSecurityObject` decodes inline (docType / namespaces / value digests / validity / device key all visible). What remains is purely cosmetic: a curated mdoc *summary* view (validity / docType as highlighted rows) as an extension of the JWT / SD-JWT credential inspector per the §2.8 framing — a polish slice, not a capability gap.

**Why:** EU Digital Identity Wallet pilots are running now and the regulation forces member states to roll out wallets by 2026. The credential formats — SD-JWT (selective-disclosure JWT) and ISO 18013-5 mdoc (CBOR + COSE-signed) — are new and tooling is sparse.

**Spec:**
- **SD-JWT (`vc+sd-jwt`)**: parse the issuer-signed JWT (already partly handled by the existing JWT inspector), decode the disclosures (Base64URL-encoded JSON arrays), reconstruct the cleartext claim set, surface which claims are disclosed vs blinded.
- **ISO mdoc**: CBOR + COSE_Sign1. Needs a small CBOR decoder (~300 lines, hand-rolled; specifically the deterministic CBOR subset used by ISO 18013-5). Decode the `MobileSecurityObject`, surface namespaces, doctype, digests, validity, device key.
- **No verification against issuer trust anchors.** Tool decodes; does not verify the chain.

**Dependencies:** small hand-rolled CBOR decoder. Worth vendoring as `cbor.min.js` alongside the other deps.

**Risk:** the specs are still moving. Implement against the published ARF v1.x (Architecture and Reference Framework) and label the tool as "early — formats may change."

### 2.9 Argon2id KDF

**Why:** Modern PBKDF2 replacement. BSI, ANSSI, OWASP, and the Password Hashing Competition winners all recommend Argon2id.

**Spec:**
- Inputs: password (text), salt (text or hex), memory cost (KiB), time cost (iterations), parallelism (lanes), output length (bytes), variant (Argon2id default; offer Argon2i and Argon2d).
- Output: hex + PHC-formatted string `$argon2id$v=19$m=...,t=...,p=...$<b64-salt>$<b64-hash>`.
- Report wall-clock derivation time (same UX as the existing PBKDF2 tool).

**Dependencies:** This is the trade-off. Options:
1. **Vendored pure-JS Argon2** (e.g., `argon2-js`, ~30KB, ~10x slower than WASM). Acceptable for parameters typical of interactive logins (memory ~64MB, t=3). Painful for larger parameters.
2. **Vendored WASM Argon2** (`argon2-browser` core ~50KB Wasm). Fast. Requires the page to load WASM, which is a CSP change.

**Recommendation:** ship pure-JS first, label it "slower than native", and revisit WASM only if real users complain.

### 2.10 Ed25519 / X25519 standalone tools — **✅ shipped (draft 4)**

**Why:** Web Crypto now supports Ed25519 and X25519 natively (Safari 17, Chrome 113, Firefox 130). Useful for SSH, age, Signal, Noise, and many EU-origin protocols.

**Status notes (draft 4):** Shipped as two sister tools, `ed25519` and `x25519`. Both use native Web Crypto exclusively — no vendored JS, no WASM. Both detect lack of browser support and show an explicit banner with the minimum browser versions, rather than failing silently. Private keys are surfaced as the 32-byte RFC 8032 §5.1.5 seed (Ed25519) / RFC 7748 scalar (X25519); on import, the tool wraps the seed in the standard PKCS#8 / RFC 8410 envelope that Web Crypto requires. X25519 includes optional HKDF-SHA-256/SHA-512 post-processing with a user-supplied "info" context, matching Noise / X3DH conventions. Functional tests exercise RFC 8032 §7.1 vector 1 (empty message) and vector 2 (single byte `0x72`), a tampered-signature rejection, RFC 7748 §6.1 Alice/Bob shared secret derivation, and a round-trip generate→sign→verify; a11y verified in both themes.

**Spec:**
- **Ed25519:** generate keypair, sign (text or file), verify. Keys in raw 32-byte hex, OpenSSH format, and PEM (PKCS#8 / SPKI).
- **X25519:** generate keypair, compute shared secret given a peer public key. Keys in raw, PEM. Optionally pipe the shared secret through HKDF to produce a symmetric key (offer SHA-256 / SHA-512).

**Dependencies:** none — `window.crypto.subtle` handles both. Gracefully degrade on Safari < 17.

### 2.11 age file encryption

**Why:** Filippo Valsorda's age is a simpler, modern PGP successor — widely adopted in EU privacy circles (used by NixOS deployments, dotfile encryption, password managers like passage).

**Spec:**
- Encrypt to one or more X25519 recipients or scrypt passphrase recipients.
- Decrypt with X25519 identity or passphrase.
- Generate age keypairs (`age-keygen` equivalent).
- Output: armored age format (`-----BEGIN AGE ENCRYPTED FILE-----...`).

**Dependencies:** `age-encryption.js` (or hand-roll against the age v1 spec — it's small, ~600 lines). Vendor as `age.min.js`.

### 2.12 BLAKE2b / BLAKE3 hashes — **✅ feature-complete: BLAKE2b-512 (draft 5) + BLAKE3-256 (draft 290)**

**Why:** Used by WireGuard, IPFS, Zcash, Filecoin, Borg backup, and other EU/global open-source projects. Modern, fast, and not in Web Crypto (which only does SHA-1/SHA-2).

**Status notes (draft 5):** BLAKE2b-512 shipped as an additional algorithm in the existing Hash & Checksum tool — no new tool view, no new top-level entry, just an extra row alongside SHA-1/SHA-256/SHA-384/SHA-512 in the output. Hand-rolled per RFC 7693 §3, 64-bit words emulated as 32-bit pairs (no BigInt to keep the inner loop fast). Verified against RFC 7693 §A.5 (the empty-input vector) and the canonical `"abc"` vector. ~150 lines of crypto code; no new vendored dependency.

**Status notes (draft 290):** BLAKE3-256 shipped as the sixth algorithm row in the same tool, resolving the deferral above. Hand-rolled inline (~110 lines) as the **single-threaded reference construction** — the full Merkle-tree mode (1024-byte chunks, 64-byte blocks, the binary-counter subtree-merge stack, root-output XOF), so it is correct for arbitrary-length text and file inputs, not just single-chunk data. Byte-identical to the multi-threaded SIMD build; it is simply not parallel. BLAKE3 is a pure 32-bit design, so the inner loop is plain `Uint32` arithmetic with no 64-bit-word emulation. No new vendored file (SHA-384 manifest + page-weight budget untouched), no new UI string (algorithm names are not localized), no CSP change. Verified against the official BLAKE3 test vectors (lengths 0 / 1 / 1024 / 1025 / 2048 / 3072, the `i mod 251` pattern) and cross-checked byte-for-byte against the audited `@noble/hashes` implementation across every chunk/block boundary to 256 KiB plus 200 random inputs. 4 Playwright cases cover empty / "abc" / pangram (single-chunk) + a 3072-byte file (the multi-chunk, two-parent-level tree path). The earlier deferral note ("its parallel tree-mode reference implementation is substantially larger and would benefit from a vendored single-file build") proved unnecessary: the *parallel* tree is an optimization, not a correctness requirement, and the sequential walk is small enough to hand-roll like BLAKE2b.

**Spec:**
- Add BLAKE2b (variable output 1–64 bytes, optional key for MAC) and BLAKE3 (variable output, fast on large inputs) to the existing hash & checksum tool.
- Same UI: text or file input, hex output, constant-time comparison.

**Dependencies:** none — both BLAKE2b-512 and BLAKE3-256 are hand-rolled inline (no vendored `blake2b.js` / `blake3.js`). *Possible future extension:* variable output length and the keyed-MAC / derive-key modes are not yet surfaced (the tool fixes BLAKE2b at 512-bit and BLAKE3 at 256-bit, the common defaults); the BLAKE3 XOF would make arbitrary output length trivial to expose if a user asks.

### 2.13 GS1 / EAN / GTIN barcode checksum — **✅ shipped (draft 2)**

**Why:** Boring but universally useful. Validates EAN-8, EAN-13, UPC-A, UPC-E, ITF-14, GTIN-14, SSCC. Mod-10 weighted checksum.

**Status notes (draft 2):** Landed as the `gs1` tool. Detects format by length (8/12/13/14/18), computes mod-10 weighted checksum, looks up the GS1 prefix in a ~130-row inline registry (covers every assigned range as of GS1 General Specifications v23). Functional test uses the real EAN-13 `4006381333931` plus a flipped-digit negative case.

**Spec:**
- Input: a barcode number.
- Output: which format it matches (by length), pass/fail on checksum, and the GS1 prefix decoded (country / registration authority — table ships inline).

**Dependencies:** none. ~80 lines.

---

## 3. Regulator presets — **✅ shipped in PGP key generator (draft 5) + PBKDF2 tool (draft 10); Argon2 deferred with §2.9**

A dropdown in the PGP key generator (and in the PBKDF2 / Argon2 tools) that selects pre-vetted parameters per regulator.

**Status notes (draft 5):** Shipped for the PGP key generator. Presets surface BSI TR-02102-1, ANSSI RGS B1, NIST SP 800-57, CNSA 2.0, and Privacy Guides recommended; each auto-fills algorithm + keysize and links to the regulator's source document in a footnote. The PBKDF2/Argon2 extension is still pending (Argon2 itself is in §2.9 and not yet shipped).

**Status notes (draft 7):** Each preset now carries an `asOf: 'YYYY-MM-DD'` field tracking the most recent re-verification against the upstream document. The preset's help footnote surfaces this date inline (e.g. "parameters verified 2026-05-13") so the user can see at a glance how fresh the recommendation is. `scripts/audit-release.js` parses the literal and warns (does not fail) when any preset's stamp is more than 12 months old — a release can still ship, but the staleness is visible. Verified prefix translated for en/fr/de/zh-CN/hi.

**Status notes (draft 10):** Regulator-preset dropdown extended to the PBKDF2 tool. Four presets ship: BSI TR-02102-1 (1,000,000 iterations, SHA-256), ANSSI RGS B1 (600,000, SHA-256), NIST SP 800-132 / OWASP minimum (600,000, SHA-256 — the OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet floor is the most widely-cited interoperable minimum for SP 800-132 deployments and is what the preset cites), and Privacy Guides recommended (1,000,000, SHA-256). CNSA 2.0 is intentionally absent — KDF parameters fall outside CNSA scope, and the NSA explicitly defers to SP 800-132. Manually editing the iteration count or the PRF resets the dropdown back to "Custom" so the UI never claims a regulator's recommendation once the user has diverged. Each PBKDF2 preset carries the same `asOf: 'YYYY-MM-DD'` stamp + source link + help footnote as the PGP presets. `scripts/audit-release.js` was generalized to scan every `*REGULATOR_PRESETS*` literal (not just one), so the freshness check now covers both blocks (9 entries total) without per-block changes. Six new strings (`pbkdf2.label.regulatorPreset`, `pbkdf2.opt.preset.{custom,bsi,anssi,nistOwasp,privacyGuides}`, plus four `pbkdf2.preset.note.*` keys) translated for en/fr/de/zh-CN/hi (zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review, consistent with the rest of those locales). Three new functional tests (BSI sets iterations + PRF; ANSSI 600k floor; manual edit resets to custom). Full Playwright suite: 209 passed.

| Preset | PGP key | PBKDF2 iters (SHA-256) | Argon2id (m, t, p) | Source URL |
|--------|---------|------------------------|---------------------|------------|
| **BSI TR-02102-1** | RSA 3072+ or Curve25519/Curve448 | ≥ 1,000,000 | m=64MiB, t=2, p=1+ | bsi.bund.de/SharedDocs/Downloads/EN/BSI/Publications/TechGuidelines/TG02102/BSI-TR-02102-1.html |
| **ANSSI RGS B1** | RSA 3072+ or Curve25519 | ≥ 600,000 | m=64MiB, t=3, p=1 | ssi.gouv.fr (RGS B1 annex) |
| **NIST SP 800-57** | RSA 3072+ or P-256/P-384 | ≥ 600,000 (OWASP 2023) | m=19MiB, t=2, p=1 (OWASP minimum) | csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/57/pt1/r5 |
| **CNSA 2.0** | RSA 3072+ or P-384 | n/a (KDFs not in CNSA scope; defer to SP 800-132) | n/a | nsa.gov/Press-Room/Press-Releases/Press-Release-View/Article/3148990 |
| **Privacy Guides** | Curve25519 (`ed25519`/`cv25519`) | ≥ 1,000,000 | m=64MiB, t=3, p=4 | privacyguides.org/en/basics/passwords-overview/ |

Each preset:
- Shows the choices it imposes in the UI before the user confirms.
- Links out to the regulator's actual document (not a summary) so the choice is verifiable.
- Is dated (`as of YYYY-MM-DD`) in the help text. Regulator recommendations drift; the preset must be auditable.
- Is implemented as a JSON blob, not hardcoded logic, so updating a preset is a one-line change.

**Update cadence:** Audit the four sources once per year (set a calendar reminder in `scripts/audit-release.js` so a release surfaces stale presets).

## 4. Distribution, sovereignty, and trust

These changes do not touch the codebase; they touch how the project is published and discovered.

### 4.1 Mirror on Codeberg

Codeberg is an EU-based (Germany), non-profit Forgejo instance. Many EU public-sector and activist users prefer it for jurisdictional reasons.

- Push a mirror to `codeberg.org/<user>/encryptalotta`.
- Add a `git remote` so pushes go to both GitHub and Codeberg.
- Mention both URLs in README and footer.

### 4.2 Reproducible release manifest — **✅ generator shipped (draft 6); signing deferred**

- Each tagged release publishes a `RELEASES.md` line with the SHA-256 of every shipped file (or a single SHA-256 of a deterministic tarball).
- Sign the manifest with a project minisign or sigstore key. Publish the public key in `SECURITY.md` and in the README.
- The build is essentially "copy files + run `build-i18n-variants.js` + run `build-sitemap.js`"; reproducibility is trivial.

**Status notes (draft 6):** `scripts/build-release-manifest.js` shipped. Generates an append-only `RELEASES.md` with the SHA-256 of every shipped file (index.html, the four locale variants, the four vendored .js libraries, all icons, sitemap.xml, robots.txt, _headers, site.webmanifest, plus optional artifacts encryptalotta-portable.html and sbom.json). Each entry records the git commit, tag (if any), working-tree cleanliness flag, and a manifest-fingerprint SHA-256 of all per-file hashes. RELEASES.md itself is *not* committed in this round — maintainers will regenerate at tagged-release time when the working tree is clean and the fingerprint is meaningful. Signing the manifest with minisign/sigstore is still deferred.

### 4.3 Software Bill of Materials (SBOM) — **✅ shipped (draft 6)**

- Publish a CycloneDX 1.5 JSON SBOM listing the vendored dependencies (`openpgp.min.js`, `qrcode.js`, `js-yaml.min.js`, `secrets.min.js`, and any new vendored deps from §2).
- The EU Cyber Resilience Act and NIS2 are pushing public-sector procurement toward requiring SBOMs. Having one available — even for a free static site — is a credibility signal.

**Status notes (draft 6):** `scripts/build-sbom.js` shipped. Output is `sbom.json` at the repo root, CycloneDX 1.5, listing all four vendored libraries with name + version + license + PURL + SHA-256/384/512 hashes + VCS URL + on-disk file path. The serial number is derived from the on-disk dep hashes, so re-running on unchanged inputs produces byte-identical output. The audit script (`scripts/audit-release.js`) cross-checks the SBOM's SHA-384 against the README manifest and the on-disk bytes — if a maintainer updates a vendored library without regenerating the SBOM, audit fails before the commit lands.

### 4.4 Tor onion mirror

- Stand up a v3 onion service mirroring the static files (cheap; the site is static).
- Add the onion address to `SECURITY.md` and the footer.
- This is high signal for the journalist/activist audience the site implicitly serves.

### 4.5 PWA installability and offline-precaching — **✅ shipped (draft 9)**

- Confirm the manifest at `site.webmanifest` declares `display: standalone` and the install icons.
- Add a minimal service worker that precaches the shell. Strict CSP — service worker scope must match.
- This makes the site genuinely "downloadable" on Android and desktop: users in censored or low-bandwidth regions can install once and use forever.

**Status notes (draft 9):** `sw.js` shipped at the repo root. ~60 lines, no dependencies. Precaches a 20-entry shell on install (the four locale roots + `/index.html` + `/privacy.html` + the four vendored libraries + the three favicons + `favicon.ico` + `site.webmanifest`) using `cache.addAll` with `cache: 'reload'` so the SW never installs a stale snapshot mid-flight. Cache-first on `fetch` for same-origin GETs only; cross-origin requests pass straight through (defense in depth — even though the site has no cross-origin scripts). Cache name is versioned (`encryptalotta-shell-v1`); bump at release time to invalidate. `activate` claims clients and drops old caches. Registration in `index.html` is HTTPS-gated and silent on failure (no UI prompt). The document CSP is unchanged — `connect-src 'none'` still applies to the page itself; a path-scoped `_headers` override grants `/sw.js` `connect-src 'self'` so install-time precache fetches resolve. The grant is narrow on purpose: the SW has no logic that would dial out elsewhere. Audit's CSP check still inspects the default (document) rule and continues to enforce `connect-src 'none'` on the page surface.

### 4.6 Single-file portable build — **✅ shipped (draft 6)**

- A script that produces `encryptalotta.html` — the entire site as a single HTML file with all JS/CSS/icons inlined as `data:` URIs.
- Audience: journalists in transit, USB-stick distribution, hostile-network environments.
- Output: hosted as a downloadable on the site itself.

**Status notes (draft 6):** `scripts/build-portable.js` shipped. Output is `encryptalotta-portable.html` at the repo root, ~1.3 MB self-contained. Inlines the four vendored .js libraries and the three favicons (apple-touch + 16x16 + 32x32 as base64 data URIs). Strips OpenGraph / Twitter card / canonical / hreflang metadata and the PWA manifest link (none of those are meaningful from `file://`). Banner timestamp is derived from the newest input mtime so successive builds with unchanged inputs produce byte-identical output (reproducibility-friendly). Verified end-to-end via Playwright smoke test loading from `file://` and exercising hash (BLAKE2b + SHA-256), IBAN MOD-97 validation, and the QR view (which proves the vendored qrcode.js inlined correctly). Audit script verifies no `<script src=>` references remain.

### 4.7 Submission to public-good registries

Targets, in order of fit:

- [Digital Public Goods Alliance registry](https://digitalpublicgoods.net/registry/) — explicit catalog of OSS serving SDGs.
- [Privacy Guides](https://privacyguides.org) tools list.
- [PRISM Break](https://prism-break.org).
- [EFF Surveillance Self-Defense](https://ssd.eff.org) — submit as a tool.
- `Lissy93/awesome-privacy` GitHub list.
- `awesome-selfhosted-non-self-hosted` and `awesome-static-website` lists.
- [Public Sector Tech Watch](https://joinup.ec.europa.eu/collection/public-sector-tech-watch) — EU Commission's OSS catalog.
- Tactical Tech and Access Now resource directories.

## 5. Framing and discoverability

### 5.1 GDPR-by-design page — **✅ shipped English-only (draft 8); localizations deferred**

A `/privacy` page (and translations) that states, in plain language: *no data ever leaves your browser; no cookies; no analytics; no CDN; no fonts loaded from third parties; nothing is stored server-side because there is no server.* This is trivially true for this site and is exactly what EU procurement and compliance teams literally search for. Same content per locale.

**Status notes (draft 8):** Shipped as a standalone static page at `/privacy.html` (English). The page is intentionally minimal: ~200 lines of self-contained HTML + inline CSS, no JavaScript, light/dark via `prefers-color-scheme`, no external assets. Content is structured around what the site does not do, what is stored on the device, what the static host sees, GDPR/CCPA framing, and recommendations for high-risk users — with links out to SECURITY.md / THREAT-MODEL.md / the SBOM / the GitHub repo. Footer link added to the SPA with the i18n key `footer.privacy` translated for all five locales (en/fr/de/zh-CN/hi); the linked page itself is English-only for v1, with localized variants deferred per the §0.6 "translation quality > breadth" rule. Sitemap updated with a single `/privacy.html` entry at priority 0.5 / yearly changefreq.

### 5.2 Security and threat-model documents — **✅ shipped (draft 7)**

- `SECURITY.md` — disclosure policy, contact (PGP-encrypted preferred), supported versions, known-not-applicable threat classes.
- `THREAT-MODEL.md` — what the tool does and does not protect against. Important caveat against overclaiming: *the site cannot protect you from a compromised browser, a compromised device, a malicious browser extension, or a network adversary that can MITM the site's load (mitigated by HTTPS but not eliminated).*

**Status notes (draft 7):** Both files shipped at the repo root. SECURITY.md covers the disclosure email (PGP-preferred), realistic single-maintainer response timelines, what counts as in-scope vs out-of-scope, and the release-verification chain (RELEASES.md, sbom.json, portable build, audit script). THREAT-MODEL.md is structured around four sections: what the site is, what it protects against, what it does not (with explicit mitigations for high-risk users), and recommendations by user type. Both documents avoid the security-marketing register — they are conservative on purpose so users don't decide what to type into the tool based on overclaimed guarantees.

### 5.3 Regional `hreflang` aliases — **✅ shipped for English (draft 8); Spanish/Portuguese/Chinese deferred until those locales ship**

For locales that have meaningful regional splits in date/number conventions, add `hreflang` entries pointing at the same content:

- `en-GB`, `en-IE`, `en-IN`, `en-AU`, `en-NZ`, `en-ZA` → `/`
- `pt-BR` → `/pt-br/`, `pt-PT` → `/pt-pt/`
- `es-ES` → `/es/`, `es-MX` → `/es/`, `es-AR` → `/es/` (until separate variants ship)
- `zh-CN` → `/zh/`, `zh-TW` → `/zh-tw/` (when it ships)

**Status notes (draft 8):** Six regional English aliases (`en-GB`, `en-IE`, `en-AU`, `en-NZ`, `en-ZA`, `en-IN`) added to `<link rel="alternate" hreflang>` in `index.html` and to each `<url>` block in `sitemap.xml` via `scripts/build-sitemap.js`. All six point to the canonical English root. Variants inherit the same hreflang block via `build-i18n-variants.js`. Spanish and Portuguese regional aliases are deferred until the `es` / `pt-BR` / `pt-PT` locales themselves ship (Tier 1 roadmap, §1.2); `zh-TW` deferred per the same logic.

### 5.4 Localized OpenGraph and Twitter cards

`scripts/build-i18n-variants.js` already does this for the five existing locales. Confirm new locales inherit the same treatment, with translated `og:title`, `og:description`, `og:locale`, and `twitter:title`/`twitter:description`.

## 6. Phased rollout

Each phase is independent. Stop after any phase; nothing below requires the next.

### Phase 1 — i18n infrastructure (no new tools yet)

1. ✅ Logical-property CSS refactor for RTL readiness (§1.4) — shipped draft 13. RTL runtime flip + icon mirroring + LTR code-block embedding deferred until the first RTL locale is queued.
2. ✅ `Intl.PluralRules` / `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` / `Intl.ListFormat` integration (§1.5) — three slices shipped (drafts 11, 12, 14). `Intl.NumberFormat` + `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` drive PBKDF2 timing, CIDR address counts, PGP key dates, and the Unix-timestamp relative phrasing. `Intl.PluralRules` powers the regex match-count row. `Intl.ListFormat` powers the BIC multi-tag row. Remaining `.join(', ')` sites are technical lists where the locale's "and" / "und" / "et" final-joiner would feel wrong; the passphrase-strength prose case is deferred until its issue strings are translated.
3. 🟡 Command-palette synonym field added to the `STRINGS` schema (§1.6) — palette + 42 English synonym blocks + 3 localized UI strings × 5 locales shipped draft 15. Locale-specific synonyms (`chiffrer` / `cifrar` / etc.) deferred until §1.3 native reviewers are sourced.
4. ✅ CSV schema extended for plural categories (§1.5) — convention formalized in draft 25. Plural-suffixed keys follow the six CLDR rule names (`.zero|.one|.two|.few|.many|.other`); audit-release.js enforces that every group defines `.other` in en (the universal Intl.PluralRules fallback). Non-en locales may omit rules their grammar does not use. Convention documented inline at the top of `scripts/export-strings-csv.js`.

### Phase 2 — Tier 1 locales

1. Spanish (`es`), Italian (`it`), Polish (`pl`), Portuguese (`pt-BR`).
2. Each goes through all 7 translation passes (§1.3) before shipping.
3. Locale picker UI confirms readability of all option labels in their own scripts.

### Phase 3 — First RTL locale

1. Arabic (`ar`). RTL pass (Pass 5 in §1.3) is mandatory.
2. Validates the §1.4 architectural refactor end-to-end.
3. Hebrew, Persian, Urdu become near-free follow-ups.

### Phase 4 — Quick-win tools

1. ✅ IBAN validator/generator (§2.1) — landed draft 2.
2. ✅ BIC/SWIFT checker (§2.2) — landed draft 2.
3. ✅ EU VAT validator (§2.3) — landed draft 3.
4. ✅ GS1 barcode (§2.13) — landed draft 2.
5. ✅ BLAKE2b (§2.12) — landed draft 5; BLAKE3 deferred (see §2.12 status notes).
6. ✅ Ed25519 / X25519 standalone (§2.10) — landed draft 4.

All ~1-day implementations. Roughly doubles the site's tool count for a small fraction of the effort.

**Progress (draft 2):** IBAN + BIC + GS1 shipped together. Tool count went from 36 → 39. New functional tests in `tests/specs/01-tools.spec.js` (8 cases) and a11y coverage in `tests/specs/05-a11y.spec.js` (3 views × 2 themes = 6 cases). JSON-LD `featureList` updated to 39 entries; `audit-release.js` passes; full Playwright suite passes (170 tests).

**Progress (draft 3):** EU VAT validator shipped. Tool count 39 → 40. 6 new functional tests (real public VATs for DE/FR/IT + NL format-only + ES NIF rejection + DE bad-checksum). 2 new a11y tests (light + dark). JSON-LD `featureList` updated to 40 entries; STRINGS now 1014 keys × 5 locales; audit passes; full new-tool test suite (22 tests including all of IBAN/BIC/GS1/VAT) passes.

**Progress (draft 4):** Ed25519 + X25519 shipped as two sister tools. Tool count 40 → 42. 6 new functional tests using RFC 8032 §7.1 (vectors 1 & 2), RFC 7748 §6.1, plus a tamper-rejection and HKDF derivation. 4 new a11y tests. JSON-LD `featureList` updated to 42 entries; STRINGS now 1071 keys × 5 locales; gzipped page weight 0.33 MB (still well under the 2 MB budget). Audit passes.

**Progress (draft 5):** BLAKE2b-512 added to the existing Hash & Checksum tool (no new view, just an extra algorithm row). Tool count remains 42 because BLAKE2b is integrated into an existing tool. Phase 4 quick-wins now complete except BLAKE3 (deferred). Phase 6 regulator presets shipped in the PGP key generator: dropdown for BSI TR-02102-1, ANSSI RGS B1, NIST SP 800-57, CNSA 2.0, and Privacy Guides recommended; each preset auto-fills algorithm + keysize and surfaces a footnote linking to the source document. Manually editing algorithm/keysize resets the preset to "Custom" so the UI never lies about which regulator's choice is currently selected. en/fr/de UI strings reviewed; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts. 5 new functional tests (2 BLAKE2b RFC 7693 + 3 regulator preset behavior); full suite 206 passed.

**Progress (draft 6):** Phase 5 distribution scripts shipped. Three new generators, all deterministic, all audit-cross-checked:

- `scripts/build-portable.js` → `encryptalotta-portable.html` (~1.3 MB single-file build, vendored .js + favicons inlined, social-card / canonical / PWA metadata stripped). Banner timestamp derived from input mtimes so builds are byte-reproducible. Smoke-tested via Playwright loading from `file://`.
- `scripts/build-sbom.js` → `sbom.json` (CycloneDX 1.5, four library components with SHA-256/384/512 + PURL + license + VCS URL). Deterministic serial number derived from input hashes — same inputs produce byte-identical output.
- `scripts/build-release-manifest.js` → `RELEASES.md` (append-only, one section per snapshot, SHA-256 of every shipped file plus a manifest-fingerprint hash, captures git commit + tag + working-tree cleanliness). Not committed in this round — maintainers will regenerate at tagged-release time when the tree is clean.

`scripts/audit-release.js` extended to validate both artifacts: SBOM hashes are cross-checked against on-disk vendored bytes; the portable build is verified to contain zero `<script src=>` references. Both checks are soft — they only fire if the artifact is present, so contributors aren't forced to regenerate on every commit.

No runtime changes; full Playwright suite still 206 passed.

**Progress (draft 191):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-S-01 / BR-Z-01 / BR-E-01 / BR-AE-01 family per-`<cac:TaxSubtotal>` `<cac:TaxCategory>` block-presence check — paired with draft 190 on the SEPA side, and the container companion to drafts 161 / 189 / 163 (per-TaxCategory ID, Percent, TaxScheme/ID presence). Each category-letter business rule pins `<cac:TaxCategory>` as mandatory under every TaxSubtotal block because TaxCategory is the container the VAT category-code letter (BT-118), the rate (BT-119, draft 189), and the TaxScheme/ID (draft 163) all live under — without it the buyer's AP system cannot route the TaxSubtotal into the correct VAT-reporting bucket. UBL 2.1 declares `<cac:TaxCategory>` at the TaxSubtotal level as schema-mandatory (1..1 child of TaxSubtotal in the UBL 2.1 XSD), but PEPPOL pipelines have been observed accepting TaxSubtotal blocks where only TaxableAmount + TaxAmount are populated and the entire TaxCategory block is stripped (some converters emit the monetary anchors at the upstream summariser stage and append the category container at a later template merge step that may silently drop on a mapping miss), so a TaxSubtotal missing the TaxCategory block is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-*-01 schematron gate but may slip past schema-validation. Distinct from drafts 161 / 189 / 163 which fire on the inner subfields once the container is present — this gate catches the missing-container case before the three downstream gates silently skip. The new `ublCheckTaxSubtotalTaxCategoryPresence()` walks every `<cac:TaxSubtotal>` reachable under the document root, checks for a direct-child `<cac:TaxCategory>` element, and emits one green `✓ Every <cac:TaxSubtotal> declares <cac:TaxCategory> across all N TaxSubtotal block(s) …` row when every block is populated, or one red `✗ TaxSubtotal #i is missing <cac:TaxCategory> — …` row per offending block. With drafts 161 / 189 / 163 / 191 the inspector now exercises the container + the three inner subfields the BR-*-01 / BR-*-05 / BR-CO-04 / BR-CO-08 schematron rules cover. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with one TaxSubtotal carrying a populated TaxCategory → confirmation citing the BR-S-01 family; same fixture with the TaxCategory block stripped → mismatch row citing the missing container). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 190):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook per-`<PmtInf>` `<CtrlSum>` non-empty presence check on pain.001 / pain.008 — paired with draft 191 on the UBL side, and the per-block companion to draft 188's GrpHdr `<CtrlSum>` gate. ISO 20022 declares `<PmtInf>/<CtrlSum>` (ControlSum, DecimalNumber) as schema-optional at 0..1 on pain.001.001.03 and pain.008.001.02, but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both pin CtrlSum as mandatory at the PmtInf level — the per-block control-total receiving banks bind against the sum of per-transaction amounts under each PmtInf group as a per-block integrity gate (distinct from the file-wide GrpHdr/CtrlSum gate covered by draft 188). The per-PmtInf CtrlSum arithmetic invariant (draft 92 — `sepaCheckPmtInfInvariants`) silently exits when the value is empty because Number('') returns NaN and the comparison short-circuits, so a PmtInf without CtrlSum bypasses the per-block arithmetic gate downstream; payments-hub serialisers have been observed emitting empty `<CtrlSum/>` shells inside PmtInf when the upstream per-block summariser fails to compute (typical when one PmtInf block has zero qualifying transactions after a filter and the serialiser ships the element shell anyway); clearing systems (EBA STEP2 / STET / RT1) reject the file at the per-PmtInf schematron gate because the receiving bank cannot reconcile the block against the actual per-block payload sum. Distinct from the per-PmtInf CtrlSum 2-decimal-cap gate (draft 132) and the per-PmtInf CtrlSum arithmetic invariant gate (draft 92): the decimals gate validates lexical precision when the value is present and the arithmetic gate validates the per-block sum invariant when the value is parseable; this gate catches the empty / absent case before either downstream gate silently skips. The new `sepaCheckPmtInfCtrlSumPresence()` walks every `<PmtInf>` block under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, reads each direct-child `<CtrlSum>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ Every PmtInf declares <CtrlSum> with non-empty content across all N PmtInf block(s) …` row when every block is populated, or one red `✗ PmtInf #i is missing <CtrlSum> …` row per offending block. With drafts 188 / 190 the SEPA inspector now covers both file-wide and per-block ControlSum presence — pairing with the existing GrpHdr / PmtInf CtrlSum arithmetic gates (drafts 35 / 92). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical SEPA_PAIN001 with `<CtrlSum>100.00</CtrlSum>` inside the PmtInf block → confirmation citing the EPC SEPA Rulebook mandatory per-block ControlSum and the 1-block count; same fixture with the per-PmtInf `<CtrlSum>` emptied → mismatch row citing the empty per-block element). Canonical SEPA_PAIN001 fixture extended to add `<CtrlSum>100.00</CtrlSum>` inside the PmtInf block — the file previously omitted it and the existing per-PmtInf arithmetic invariant gate silently skipped on the canonical input; the new presence gate now exercises the right-hand side of the per-block reconciliation chain end-to-end. Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 189):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BT-119 per-`<cac:TaxCategory>` `<cbc:Percent>` presence check — paired with draft 188 on the SEPA side, and the per-rate-anchor companion to drafts 161 (TaxCategory/ID presence) and 163 (TaxScheme/ID presence). EN 16931 BT-119 names the VAT category rate (Percent), carried in `<cac:TaxCategory>/<cbc:Percent>`, as the per-rate anchor every category-letter business rule binds against — BR-S-05 (standard) requires Percent > 0; BR-Z-05 (zero-rated), BR-E-05 (exempt), and BR-AE-05 (reverse charge) each require Percent = 0; each rule covers presence + value together, so an absent Percent slot fails the applicable BR-*-05 schematron gate at the buyer's PEPPOL access point regardless of the declared category-code letter. UBL 2.1 declares `<cbc:Percent>` at cac:TaxCategory as schema-optional (TaxCategory.Percent is 0..1), so a category block carrying only `<cbc:ID>` and `<cac:TaxScheme>` passes XML validation but is rejected at the BR-*-05 gate; the downstream Percent-sign non-negativity gate (draft 135 — `ublCheckPercentSign`) only fires when the field is present and silently skips otherwise, and the per-rate rate-times-base check (draft 37 — `ublCheckVatRates`) also silently exits when percentEl is null, so a missing Percent slot lets the rate-product invariant skip downstream. The new `ublCheckTaxCategoryPercentPresence()` walks every `<cac:TaxCategory>` reachable under the document root, reads each direct-child `<cbc:Percent>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ Every <cac:TaxCategory> declares <cbc:Percent> across all N TaxCategory block(s) …` row when every block is populated, or one red `✗ TaxCategory #i is missing <cbc:Percent> — …` row per offending block. With drafts 161 / 163 / 189 the UBL inspector now covers the three TaxCategory subfields the rate-times-base invariant + AP-routing chain depend on (ID, Percent, TaxScheme/ID). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with TaxCategory/Percent=19 → confirmation citing BT-119 / BR-*-05 family; same fixture with the inner Percent stripped → mismatch row citing the missing slot). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 188):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook `<GrpHdr>/<CtrlSum>` non-empty presence check on pain.001 / pain.008 — paired with draft 189 on the UBL side, and the file-wide control-total companion to drafts 182 / 184 / 186 (GrpHdr MsgId / CreDtTm / NbOfTxs presence). ISO 20022 declares `<GrpHdr>/<CtrlSum>` (ControlSum, DecimalNumber) as schema-optional at 0..1 on pain.001.001.03 and pain.008.001.02, but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both pin CtrlSum as mandatory at the GrpHdr level because the receiving bank's reconciliation pipeline binds the declared file-wide total against the sum of per-transaction amounts as a first-line integrity check before the settlement engine values the wire. The GrpHdr/CtrlSum arithmetic invariant (drafts 35 / 92 — `sepaCheckGrpHdrInvariants`) silently exits when the value is empty because Number('') returns NaN and the comparison short-circuits, so a file without CtrlSum bypasses the arithmetic gate downstream; payments-hub serialisers have been observed emitting empty `<CtrlSum/>` shells when the upstream batch total fails to compute (typical when the per-transaction amounts are not summarised before serialisation and the template ships the element unconditionally); without a populated CtrlSum the receiving bank cannot verify the file-wide settlement-amount invariant and either hard-rejects the batch at the EPC SEPA Rulebook gate or — worse — accepts the file and reconciles against the actual payload sum, masking an upstream summariser bug that produces silent settlement-amount drift on the originator side. Distinct from the existing CtrlSum 2-decimal-cap gate (draft 132 — `sepaCheckCtrlSumDecimals`) and the CtrlSum arithmetic invariant gate (drafts 35 / 92): the decimals gate validates lexical precision when the value is present and the arithmetic gate validates the header-vs-payload sum invariant when the value is parseable; this gate catches the empty / absent case before either downstream gate silently skips. The new `sepaCheckCtrlSumPresence()` walks `GrpHdr` on pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads `<CtrlSum>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ GrpHdr declares <CtrlSum> with non-empty content …` row when the value is present, or one red `✗ GrpHdr is missing <CtrlSum> …` row otherwise. Scoped to pain.001 / pain.008 only — camt.053 carries its CtrlSum at a different structural anchor and is intentionally excluded. With drafts 182 / 184 / 186 / 188 the SEPA inspector now exercises non-empty-presence on the four file-wide GrpHdr identifiers / counts / totals (MsgId / CreDtTm / NbOfTxs / CtrlSum) the receiving bank's dedupe / replay-defense / payload-count / settlement-amount gates depend on. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical SEPA_PAIN001 with `<CtrlSum>100.00</CtrlSum>` → confirmation citing the EPC SEPA Rulebook mandatory ControlSum; same fixture with `<CtrlSum></CtrlSum>` → mismatch row citing the empty element). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 187):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-CO-18 / BT-117 per-`<cac:TaxSubtotal>` `<cbc:TaxAmount>` presence check — paired with draft 186 on the SEPA side, and the per-rate tax-amount companion to draft 185's BT-116 TaxableAmount-presence gate. EN 16931 BR-CO-18 names the VAT category tax amount (BT-117) as mandatory on every TaxSubtotal block because BT-117 is the per-rate VAT amount the BR-CO-13 invariant (header BT-110 = Σ per-rate BT-117) sums into the document-level TaxTotal/TaxAmount, and the per-rate figure the rate-times-base check verifies (TaxableAmount BT-116 × Percent = TaxAmount BT-117). UBL 2.1 declares `<cbc:TaxAmount>` as schema-mandatory (1..1 child of TaxSubtotal in the UBL 2.1 XSD), but PEPPOL pipelines have been observed accepting TaxSubtotal blocks where only TaxableAmount + Percent are populated and TaxAmount is stripped (some converters compute the rate-times-base chain upstream and treat the per-rate tax amount as derivable from the base and the percent), so a TaxSubtotal missing BT-117 is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-CO-18 schematron gate but may slip past schema-validation; the downstream per-rate VAT-rate check (draft 37 — `ublCheckVatRates`) silently exits when taxAmtEl is null, and the header BT-110 invariant loses one of its summation inputs. The new `ublCheckTaxSubtotalTaxAmtPresence()` walks every `<cac:TaxSubtotal>` from the document root, reads each direct-child `<cbc:TaxAmount>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ Every <cac:TaxSubtotal> declares <cbc:TaxAmount> across all N TaxSubtotal block(s) …` row when every block is populated, or one red `✗ TaxSubtotal #i is missing <cbc:TaxAmount> — …` row per offending block. Together drafts 185 / 187 cover both inputs to the per-rate rate-times-base check (BT-116 / BT-117) and the two slots the BR-CO-13 invariant sums. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with TaxSubtotal/TaxAmount=19.00 → confirmation citing BR-CO-18 / BT-117; same fixture with the inner TaxSubtotal/TaxAmount stripped → mismatch row citing the missing slot). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 186):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an ISO 20022 `<GrpHdr>/<NbOfTxs>` non-empty presence check on pain.001 / pain.008 / camt.053 — paired with draft 187 on the UBL side, and the file-wide transaction-count companion to drafts 182 / 184 (GrpHdr MsgId / CreDtTm presence). ISO 20022 declares `<GrpHdr>/<NbOfTxs>` (NumberOfTransactions, Max15NumericText) as schema-mandatory (minOccurs=1) on all three message types; NbOfTxs is the file-wide transaction-count anchor the receiving bank uses to verify that the on-the-wire payload count matches what the originator declared in the header, and the downstream NbOfTxs arithmetic-invariant gate (drafts 34 / 91 — `sepaCheckGrpHdrInvariants` / `sepaCheckPmtInfInvariants`) silently exits when the value is empty (Number('') returns NaN and the comparison short-circuits).
