Payments-hub serialisers have been observed emitting empty `<NbOfTxs/>` shells when the upstream batch-counter source returns null (typical when the template ships the element unconditionally but the counter wiring is broken or returns undefined for a zero-transaction edge case); without a populated NbOfTxs the receiving bank cannot verify the file-wide payload-count invariant and either hard-rejects the batch at the GrpHdr schema-validation gate or — worse — accepts the file and reconciles against the actual payload count, masking an upstream batch-counter bug that produces silent transaction-loss on the originator side. Distinct from the NbOfTxs-shape gate (`sepaCheckNbOfTxsShape`) and the NbOfTxs-arithmetic gate (drafts 34 / 91): the shape gate validates digits-only lexical form when the value is present and the arithmetic gate validates the header-vs-payload count invariant when the value is parseable; this gate catches the empty / absent case before either downstream gate silently skips. The new `sepaCheckNbOfTxsPresence()` walks `GrpHdr` on pain.001 / pain.008 / camt.053 wrappers, reads `<NbOfTxs>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ GrpHdr declares <NbOfTxs> with non-empty content …` row when the value is present, or one red `✗ GrpHdr is missing <NbOfTxs> …` row otherwise. With drafts 182 / 184 / 186 the SEPA inspector now exercises non-empty-presence on the three file-wide GrpHdr identifiers / counts (MsgId / CreDtTm / NbOfTxs) the receiving bank's dedupe / replay-defense / payload-count gates depend on. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical SEPA_PAIN001 with `<NbOfTxs>1</NbOfTxs>` → confirmation citing the ISO 20022 mandatory NumberOfTransactions; same fixture with `<NbOfTxs></NbOfTxs>` → mismatch row citing the empty element). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 133):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-DEC tax-monetary 2-decimal-cap check across `<cac:TaxTotal>/<cbc:TaxAmount>`, `<cac:TaxSubtotal>/<cbc:TaxableAmount>`, and `<cac:TaxSubtotal>/<cbc:TaxAmount>` — paired with draft 132 on the SEPA side, and the tax-surface companion to draft 129's LegalMonetaryTotal header gate and draft 131's per-line LineExtensionAmount gate. EN 16931 BR-DEC pins every tax-side monetary amount to a maximum of 2 fractional digits — BR-DEC-13 BT-110 (Invoice total VAT amount on TaxTotal/TaxAmount), BR-DEC-19 BT-116 (TaxSubtotal taxable amount per VAT category), and BR-DEC-20 BT-117 (TaxSubtotal tax amount per VAT category). UBL 2.1 declares each *Amount element as `xsd:decimal` with unbounded fractional precision so a value like `19.000` or `19.0050` is schema-valid; PEPPOL access points reject the document at the BR-DEC schematron gate. The typical real-world failure is an upstream tax engine that emits TaxAmount values at the statutory percentage's full precision (e.g. 19% × 100.00 computed as `19.0000` instead of rounded to `19.00`) or a template that propagates a per-line high-precision unit price into the per-category TaxableAmount without rounding back to the per-currency minor-unit cap. The new `ublCheckTaxAmtDecimals()` walks every `<cac:TaxTotal>` and every nested `<cac:TaxSubtotal>` from the document root, reads each direct-child `<cbc:TaxAmount>` / `<cbc:TaxableAmount>` (trimmed), counts characters after the decimal point on the raw text (trailing zeros included), and emits one green `✓ Every populated TaxTotal / TaxSubtotal monetary amount carries at most 2 fractional digits across all N amount(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ {scope}/{name} value "{v}" declares {dp} fractional digit(s) — …` row per offending value keyed by 1-based TaxTotal / TaxSubtotal index and element local name. With drafts 129 / 131 / 133 the inspector now exercises BR-DEC on every UBL monetary surface — header LegalMonetaryTotal totals, per-line LineExtensionAmount, and tax-side aggregates. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with TaxTotal/TaxAmount=19.00, TaxSubtotal/TaxableAmount=100.00, TaxSubtotal/TaxAmount=19.00 → confirmation citing the 2-decimal cap and 3-amount count; same fixture with the inner TaxSubtotal/TaxAmount flipped to `19.0050` → mismatch row citing the over-precision lexical value and the BR-DEC-20 BT-117 reference). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 216 → 220 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 132):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook 2-decimal-cap check on `<GrpHdr>/<CtrlSum>` and every `<PmtInf>/<CtrlSum>` — paired with draft 133 on the UBL side, and the file-header / per-block surface companion to draft 130's per-transaction InstdAmt / Amt 2dp cap. ISO 20022 declares both control-sum slots as `xs:decimal` with unbounded fractional precision so a value like `100.000` or `100.0050` is schema-valid; both the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks pin the value to a maximum of 2 fractional digits because SEPA is a single-currency scheme (EUR) and EUR's minor unit is the cent. The new `sepaCheckCtrlSumDecimals()` reads `GrpHdr/CtrlSum` and every `<PmtInf>/<CtrlSum>` (trimmed), counts characters after the decimal point on the raw text (trailing zeros included), and emits one green `✓ Every declared CtrlSum fits within the EPC SEPA Rulebook 2-decimal cap across all N control-sum slot(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ {scope} value "{v}" declares {dp} fractional digit(s) — …` row per offending slot keyed by `GrpHdr/CtrlSum` or `PmtInf #i/CtrlSum`. Distinct from the existing GrpHdr / PmtInf CtrlSum arithmetic-invariant gates (drafts 35 / 92) which validate that the declared total equals the sum of per-transaction amounts within ±1¢: those validate the parsed numeric value, this gate validates raw lexical precision before parsing — a sum that arithmetically rounds to the right amount but carries 4 decimal digits in its lexical form passes the arithmetic gates yet is still rejected at the EPC strict-precision character-level reading of the Rulebook. With drafts 130 / 132 in place the SEPA inspector now exercises EPC strict-precision invariants on every monetary surface the file declares — per-transaction amounts and file / block control-totals alike. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical SEPA_PAIN001 with GrpHdr/CtrlSum=100.00 → confirmation citing the 2-decimal cap and 1-slot count; same fixture with GrpHdr/CtrlSum and the matching InstdAmt both flipped to `100.0050` → mismatch row citing the offending GrpHdr/CtrlSum scope and over-precision lexical value). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 216 → 220 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 131):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-DEC line-level 2-decimal-cap check on `<cac:InvoiceLine>/<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` (and the equivalent `<cac:CreditNoteLine>/<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` on credit notes) — paired with draft 130 on the SEPA side, and the line-surface companion to draft 129's header-level BR-DEC family. EN 16931 BR-DEC pins the BT-131 InvoiceLine net amount to a maximum of 2 fractional digits (the line counterpart of BR-DEC-09 / -12 / -14 / -15 / -16 / -17 covered at the LegalMonetaryTotal header level by draft 129). UBL 2.1 declares `<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` as `xsd:decimal` with unbounded fractional precision so a value like `100.0050` or `100.000` is schema-valid; PEPPOL access points reject the document at the BR-DEC schematron gate because the BR-CO-10 invariant (Σ BT-131 = BT-106 LineExtensionAmount on LegalMonetaryTotal) requires both sides of the equality to round to the same per-currency minor-unit cap, and a high-precision line value either inflates the sum past the rounded header total or forces the header to drop sub-cent precision the buyer's AP system can't reconcile back to the supplier's source-system invoice. The typical real-world failure is an upstream ERP computing each line as quantity × unit-price at full commodity / FX-derived precision (4-6 decimals on metals / FX / commodity-traded SKUs) without a per-line round step before writing the LineExtension value. The new `ublCheckLineAmtDecimals()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` and `<cac:CreditNoteLine>` reachable from the document root, reads each line's direct-child `<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` (trimmed), counts characters after the decimal point on the raw text (trailing zeros included, matching EN 16931's strict-precision reading of BR-DEC), and emits one green `✓ Every populated InvoiceLine / CreditNoteLine LineExtensionAmount carries at most 2 fractional digits across all N line(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ Line #i LineExtensionAmount "{v}" declares {dp} fractional digit(s) — …` row per offending line keyed by 1-based line index. Mirror in shape of draft 129 extended to the line surface so both header and line monetary anchors are now covered by BR-DEC gates. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with one InvoiceLine at LineExtensionAmount=100.00 → confirmation citing the BT-131 2-decimal cap and 1-line count; same fixture with the per-line LineExtensionAmount flipped to `100.0050` → mismatch row citing the over-precision lexical value and the BR-DEC BT-131 reference). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 212 → 216 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 130):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook 2-decimal-cap check on per-transaction `<InstdAmt>` / `<Amt>` — paired with draft 131 on the UBL side, and the SEPA-side mirror of draft 129's UBL header-level BR-DEC family. ISO 20022 declares `<InstdAmt>` and `<Amt>` as `xs:decimal` with unbounded fractional precision so a value like `100.000` or `100.0050` is schema-valid; both the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks pin the per-transaction amount to a maximum of 2 fractional digits because SEPA is a single-currency scheme (EUR — draft 128 enforces the EUR-only invariant at the account level) and EUR's minor unit is the cent — two decimals. Clearing systems (EBA STEP2 / STET / RT1) reject files whose amounts declare more than 2 fractional digits because the receiving bank's account-statement renderer and cross-rail settlement accounting both pin the value to two decimals; an over-precision amount is either silently truncated by the receiving bank (the worst outcome — reconciliation drifts by the dropped sub-cent fraction) or hard-rejected by the originator bank's own pre-clearing gate. The canonical real-world failure is an upstream ERP carrying high-precision unit prices (commonly 4-6 decimals on commodity / FX-derived pricing surfaces — metals, energy, FX-tied invoicing) whose print-format default propagates into the pain.001 / pain.008 InstdAmt without rounding back to the EUR minor-unit cap. The new `sepaCheckAmtDecimals()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, reads each transaction's `<InstdAmt>` (pain.001 preferred) or `<Amt>` (pain.008 preferred), counts characters after the decimal point on the raw text (trailing zeros included, matching EPC's strict-precision reading) so `100.000` is flagged even though it numerically rounds to `100.00`, and emits one green `✓ Every transaction amount fits within the EPC SEPA Rulebook 2-decimal cap across all N transaction(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ Transaction #i declares InstdAmt / Amt "{v}" with {dp} fractional digit(s) — …` row per offending transaction keyed by 1-based index. Distinct from `sepaCheckPositiveAmounts` (draft 104) which validates sign on the parsed numeric value: that one validates polarity, this gate validates raw lexical precision before parsing. Mirror in shape of draft 129's `ublCheckLmtDecimals` on the UBL header surface; together drafts 130 / 131 pair the SEPA-side per-transaction cap with the UBL-side per-line cap so the inspector now exercises EN 16931 / EPC strict-precision invariants on every monetary surface that crosses a rail. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical SEPA_PAIN001 with InstdAmt=100.00 → confirmation citing the 2-decimal cap and 1-transaction count; same fixture with InstdAmt flipped to `100.0050` → mismatch row citing the over-precision lexical value and the EPC SEPA single-currency-minor-unit requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 212 → 216 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 129):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-DEC 2-decimal-cap check across every populated `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>` monetary amount — paired with draft 128 on the SEPA side. EN 16931 declares a family of decimal-precision rules (BR-DEC-09 BT-106 LineExtensionAmount, BR-DEC-12 BT-109 TaxExclusiveAmount, BR-DEC-14 BT-112 TaxInclusiveAmount, BR-DEC-15 BT-113 PrepaidAmount, BR-DEC-16 BT-114 PayableRoundingAmount, BR-DEC-17 BT-115 PayableAmount) all of which pin the corresponding LegalMonetaryTotal amount to a maximum of 2 fractional digits. UBL 2.1 declares each *Amount element as `xsd:decimal` with unbounded fractional precision so a value like `119.0050` or `119.0000` is schema-valid; PEPPOL access points reject the document at the BR-DEC schematron gate. The typical real-world failure is an upstream ERP carrying high-precision unit prices (4-6 decimals) whose print-format default propagates into header totals without rounding back to the per-currency minor-unit cap (2 decimals for EUR / USD / GBP). The new `ublCheckLmtDecimals()` walks every direct child of `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>` whose `localName` ends in `Amount`, counts characters after the decimal point on the raw text (trailing zeros included, matching EN 16931's strict-precision reading of BR-DEC), and emits one green `✓ Every populated LegalMonetaryTotal amount carries at most 2 fractional digits across all N amount(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ LegalMonetaryTotal/{name} value "{v}" declares {dp} fractional digit(s) — …` row per offending amount keyed by the element local name. Distinct from the per-amount sign gates (drafts 113-119) which validate sign and arithmetic on the parsed numeric value: this gate validates raw lexical precision before parsing, so it catches over-precision values whose rounded form would still pass the sign/arithmetic gates. Walks future EN-16931 BR-DEC additions without code changes (any new `*Amount` child of LegalMonetaryTotal is automatically covered). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with four amounts at 2 decimals → confirmation citing the 2-decimal cap and 4-amount count; same fixture with PayableAmount flipped to `119.0050` → mismatch row citing the over-precision lexical value and the BR-DEC-17 reference). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 208 → 212 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 128):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook account-level `<Ccy>` = EUR check on `<DbtrAcct>/<Ccy>` and `<CdtrAcct>/<Ccy>` — paired with draft 129 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 declares both as `ActiveOrHistoricCurrencyCode` (any ISO 4217 alpha-3 code is schema-valid), but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks restrict SEPA to EUR; an account-level Ccy other than EUR routes the payment to the cross-border FX gateway (where it is rejected as non-SEPA) instead of the SEPA clearing rails (EBA STEP2 / STET / RT1). The typical real-world failure is a template repurposed across SEPA and SWIFT MT103 flows where the per-account Ccy element was not switched back to EUR even though the amount-level Ccy attribute was. Distinct from `sepaCheckEurOnly` (draft 98, walks every `Ccy="…"` attribute on `<InstdAmt>` / `<Amt>` / `<NtryAmt>` …): that one validates the per-transaction settlement currency; this gate validates the account-of-record currency at the `<DbtrAcct>` / `<CdtrAcct>` element. The new `sepaCheckAcctCcy()` walks every `<DbtrAcct>` and `<CdtrAcct>` under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, reads the optional `<Ccy>` child (case-folded, whitespace-stripped for paste-safety), and emits one green `✓ Every declared account-level <Ccy> is EUR across all N cash account(s) (EPC SEPA Credit Transfer / Direct Debit Rulebook — SEPA is a single-currency scheme)` row when every populated value is EUR, or one red `✗ Cash account <Ccy> "{code}" is not EUR — …` row per offending value. Accounts without a `<Ccy>` child skip silently — the element is optional per the ISO 20022 schema and the receiving bank defaults to EUR for SEPA accounts when absent. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with both DbtrAcct/Ccy and CdtrAcct/Ccy = EUR → confirmation citing the 2-account count; same fixture with CdtrAcct/Ccy flipped to USD → mismatch row citing the offending code and the EPC SEPA single-currency requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 208 → 212 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 127):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-16 at-least-one-line presence check — paired with draft 126 on the SEPA side. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 BR-16 require every Invoice and CreditNote to carry at least one line item; an Invoice without lines has no goods or services to bill for, and the line-total invariants BR-CO-10 (Σ BT-131 = BT-106 line-extension total) and BR-CO-13 (BT-110 = Σ TaxAmount) lose their inputs. UBL 2.1 declares both `<cac:InvoiceLine>` (on Invoice) and `<cac:CreditNoteLine>` (on CreditNote) repeatable at 0..n at the schema level, so a line-less document passes XML validation but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-16 schematron gate. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that emits the document shell from a template before the line-loop has produced any rows, or that suppresses zero-amount lines so a fully-discounted invoice ends up empty. Distinct from the per-line gates (`ublCheckLineQuantity` / `ublCheckItemName` / `ublCheckPriceAmount` / `ublCheckLineAmount` / `ublCheckLineIdUnique`) which only fire when lines are present; this gate catches the degenerate zero-lines case so the downstream gates never silently skip. The new `ublCheckLinePresence()` walks `<cac:InvoiceLine>` (on Invoice roots) or `<cac:CreditNoteLine>` (on CreditNote roots) via `detected.type`, and emits one green `✓ Document carries {n} {field}(s) (EN 16931 BR-16 — an Invoice / Credit Note shall have at least one line)` row when ≥1 line is present, or one red `✗ Document declares zero {field} elements — EN 16931 BR-16 requires every Invoice and Credit Note to carry at least one line item — …` row otherwise. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with one InvoiceLine → confirmation citing BR-16 and the 1-line count; same fixture with the InvoiceLine block stripped → mismatch row citing the BR-16 schematron gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 204 → 208 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 126):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook `<GrpHdr>/<InitgPty>/<Nm>` originator-name presence check — paired with draft 127 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 pain.001 / pain.008 declare `<InitgPty>` as the mandatory party block carried in the group header (the initiating party identification — the originator on whose behalf the file is submitted), but the nested `<Nm>` (Max70Text) is schema-optional at 0..1. The EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both pin the originator name as mandatory because the receiving bank renders it on the beneficiary's account statement and because the originator-identification audit trail back through clearing (EBA STEP2 / STET / RT1) depends on a human-readable label paired with the OrgId/OthrId numeric identifier. A file declaring only `InitgPty/Id` without `InitgPty/Nm` is schema-valid XML but rail-broken; the typical real-world cause is a payments hub passing only the numeric identifier through to GrpHdr without filling in the legal-entity name. Distinct from the party-name character-set check (`sepaCheckPartyNmCharset`, draft 59): that one validates content when the name is present; this gate validates that the name is declared at all. Mirror in shape of drafts 110 (counterparty Nm presence) and 111 (UBL Seller name presence): both enforce a single mandatory-element-presence rule the schema marks optional but the standard/rulebook makes mandatory. The new `sepaCheckInitgPtyNm()` walks `GrpHdr/InitgPty` on pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads `<Nm>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ GrpHdr/InitgPty/Nm "{name}" present (EPC SEPA Rulebook originator-identification requirement)` row when the value is present, or one red `✗ GrpHdr/InitgPty is missing <Nm> — …` row otherwise. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with `InitgPty/Nm=ACME Corp` → confirmation citing the EPC SEPA originator-identification requirement; same fixture with the `<Nm>` replaced by `<Id><OrgId><Othr><Id>ACMECORP01</Id></Othr></OrgId></Id>` → mismatch row citing the missing `<Nm>`). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 204 → 208 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 125):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-11 / BT-55 Buyer country code presence check on `<cac:AccountingCustomerParty>/<cac:Party>/<cac:PostalAddress>/<cac:Country>/<cbc:IdentificationCode>` — paired with draft 124 on the SEPA side, and the buyer-side mirror of draft 123's seller-country (BR-09 / BT-40) gate. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 require every Invoice and CreditNote to declare the buyer's country code; the value is the ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code that establishes which member state's VAT regime governs the buyer side of the supply (intra-EU reverse-charge vs domestic input-VAT vs B2C export) and which national tax authority the BT-48 Buyer VAT identifier (draft 58) is registered with. UBL 2.1 marks both `<cac:PostalAddress>` and `<cac:Country>/<cbc:IdentificationCode>` optional at the schema level, so a customer party carrying only `<cac:PartyName>` and `<cac:PartyLegalEntity>` passes XML validation but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-11 schematron gate. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that fills the legal-entity slot from the customer-master integration but leaves the buyer's postal-address block empty — far more common than the seller-side gap because the supplier's own address is usually mandatory in upstream invoice templates. Together with drafts 111 / 121 (name presence) and 123 / 125 (country presence) the inspector now exercises every EN 16931 schematron mandatory field on both supplier and customer party blocks. The new `ublCheckBuyerCountry()` walks `AccountingCustomerParty/Party/PostalAddress/Country` and reads `<cbc:IdentificationCode>`, emitting one green `✓ Buyer PostalAddress/Country/IdentificationCode "{code}" present (EN 16931 BR-11 / BT-55)` row when the value is present, or one red `✗ AccountingCustomerParty is missing Party/PostalAddress/Country/IdentificationCode — …` row otherwise. The canonical UBL_INVOICE fixture's buyer block was extended with `<cac:PostalAddress><cac:Country><cbc:IdentificationCode>FR</cbc:IdentificationCode></cac:Country></cac:PostalAddress>` to reflect a clean PEPPOL invoice (FR matches the existing buyer name "Beispiel SARL"); the pre-existing buyer-VAT replace anchors are unaffected because they anchor on the trailing `</cac:Party></cac:AccountingCustomerParty>` segment. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with IdentificationCode=FR → confirmation citing BR-11 / BT-55; same fixture with the buyer's PostalAddress block stripped → mismatch row citing the BR-11 schematron gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 200 → 204 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 124):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook 16-character cap per `<PstlAdr>/<PstCd>` — paired with draft 125 on the UBL side, and the postal-code length companion to drafts 120 (AdrLine 70-char) and 122 (TwnNm 35-char); together the three gates cover every length-capped postal-address subfield the EPC narrows. ISO 20022 declares `<PstCd>` as `Max16Text` at the schema level, which already pins the cap; however many real-world generators source the value from CRM postal-code columns that allow up to 35 codepoints (or merge city + ZIP into one field) and rely on downstream validation, so the inspector should surface a value that exceeds 16 chars before the file leaves the originator. The EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks re-affirm the 16-character cap because the value is propagated end-to-end through SEPA rails into the receiving bank's account-statement address renderer at a column width pinned to the legacy SWIFT MT103 / MT104 postal-code limit. An over-cap PstCd is either silently truncated by the receiving bank (the statement reads a half-truncated ZIP that doesn't match the contract on file — the worst outcome because the payment settles but reconciliation breaks on ZIP match) or hard-rejected by the originator bank's own pre-clearing gate. The canonical real-world failure is a CRM column carrying ZIP + city ("10115 Berlin Mitte") or a long alphanumeric routing code packed into the postal-code slot. The new `sepaCheckPstCdLength()` walks every `<PstlAdr>` reachable under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, reads `<PstCd>` (trimmed), measures codepoint length via `[...v].length`, and emits one green `✓ Every populated PstCd fits within the EPC SEPA Rulebook 16-character cap across all N non-empty postal code(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ PostalAddress #i PstCd "{value}" exceeds the 16-character cap ({len}/16 characters) — …` row per offending value keyed by 1-based PostalAddress index. Empty / absent PstCd slots skip silently. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with a Cdtr PstlAdr/PstCd=10115 → confirmation citing the 16-char cap; same fixture with a 19-char "10115 Berlin Mitte" → mismatch row citing the offending PostalAddress and the over-cap length). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 200 → 204 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 123):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-09 / BT-40 Seller country code presence check on `<cac:AccountingSupplierParty>/<cac:Party>/<cac:PostalAddress>/<cac:Country>/<cbc:IdentificationCode>` — paired with draft 122 on the SEPA side. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 require every Invoice and CreditNote to declare the seller's country code; the value is the ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code that establishes which member state's VAT regime governs the supply (intra-EU vs export vs domestic) and which national tax authority the BT-31 Seller VAT identifier (draft 57) is registered with. UBL 2.1 marks both `<cac:PostalAddress>` and `<cac:Country>/<cbc:IdentificationCode>` optional at the schema level, so a supplier party carrying only `<cac:PartyName>` and `<cac:PartyLegalEntity>` passes XML validation but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-09 schematron gate. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that fills the legal-entity and tax-scheme slots from the company-registry integration but leaves the postal-address block empty because the address source is wired to a separate CRM that's optional in the data model. Distinct from the existing `ublCheckCountryCode()` (validity of any declared codes against ISO 3166-1 alpha-2): that one only fires when a code is present and only validates the value; this gate validates that the seller's code is declared at all. Mirror in shape of draft 111 (Seller name presence): both gate EN 16931 schematron mandatory fields on the supplier party. The new `ublCheckSellerCountry()` walks `AccountingSupplierParty/Party/PostalAddress/Country` and reads `<cbc:IdentificationCode>`, emitting one green `✓ Seller PostalAddress/Country/IdentificationCode "{code}" present (EN 16931 BR-09 / BT-40)` row when the value is present, or one red `✗ AccountingSupplierParty is missing Party/PostalAddress/Country/IdentificationCode — …` row otherwise. The canonical UBL_INVOICE fixture's supplier block was extended with `<cac:PostalAddress><cac:Country><cbc:IdentificationCode>DE</cbc:IdentificationCode></cac:Country></cac:PostalAddress>` to reflect a clean PEPPOL invoice; the two pre-existing country-code-validity tests were simplified to anchor on the canonical fixture (DE) and to flip the code to UK via a narrower replace anchor. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with IdentificationCode=DE → confirmation citing BR-09 / BT-40; same fixture with the PostalAddress block stripped → mismatch row citing the BR-09 schematron gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 196 → 200 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 122):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook 35-character cap per `<PstlAdr>/<TwnNm>` — paired with draft 123 on the UBL side, and the city-slot length companion to draft 120's per-AdrLine 70-char cap. ISO 20022 declares `<TwnNm>` as `Max35Text` at the schema level, which already pins the cap; however many real-world generators source the value from CRM city columns that allow up to 140 codepoints (matching the widely used ISO 20022 Max140Text caps across pain.* free-text identifiers) and rely on downstream validation, so the inspector should surface a value that exceeds 35 chars before the file leaves the originator. The EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks re-affirm the 35-character cap because the value is propagated end-to-end through SEPA rails into the receiving bank's account-statement address renderer at a column width pinned to the legacy SWIFT MT103 / MT104 35-char town/city limit. A 140-character TwnNm is either silently truncated by the receiving bank (the statement reads a half-truncated city that doesn't match the contract on file — the worst outcome because the payment settles but reconciliation breaks on city match) or hard-rejected by the originator bank's own pre-clearing gate. The canonical real-world failure is a CRM column carrying city + administrative-division ("Frankfurt am Main, Hessen, Greater Rhine-Main Area") or a hyphenated multi-locality name packed into one free-text field. Parallel in shape to draft 120 (AdrLine length): the postal-address character-set gate (sepaCheckTwnNmCharset, draft 63) already validates content; this gate validates the city slot's length at its own narrower cap. The new `sepaCheckTwnNmLength()` walks every `<PstlAdr>` reachable under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, reads `<TwnNm>` (trimmed), measures codepoint length via `[...v].length`, and emits one green `✓ Every populated TwnNm fits within the EPC SEPA Rulebook 35-character cap across all N non-empty town/city name(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ PostalAddress #i TwnNm "{value}" exceeds the 35-character cap ({len}/35 characters) — …` row per offending value keyed by 1-based PostalAddress index. Empty / absent TwnNm slots skip silently. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with a Cdtr PstlAdr/TwnNm=Berlin → confirmation citing the 35-char cap; same fixture with a 49-char "Frankfurt am Main Hessen Greater Rhine-Main Area" → mismatch row citing the offending PostalAddress and the over-cap length). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 196 → 200 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 121):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-07 / BT-44 Buyer name presence check on `<cac:AccountingCustomerParty>/<cac:Party>/<cac:PartyLegalEntity>/<cbc:RegistrationName>` — paired with draft 120 on the SEPA side, and the buyer-side mirror of draft 111's seller-name (BR-08 / BT-27) gate. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 require every Invoice and CreditNote to declare the buyer's legal-entity registration name; the value is the name the supplier prints on the sales ledger and the name AP / VAT systems pair with the BT-48 Buyer VAT identifier (draft 58) for tax reporting. UBL 2.1 marks both `<cac:PartyLegalEntity>` and `<cbc:RegistrationName>` optional at the schema level, so a customer party carrying only `<cac:PartyName>/<cbc:Name>` (the trading name, BT-45) passes XML validation but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-07 schematron gate. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that fills the trading-name slot from a CRM column but leaves the legal-entity block empty because the company-registry integration is wired to a separate, sometimes-stale data source. Distinct from draft 58's BT-48 Buyer VAT identifier check: that validates the identifier value; this validates the name the identifier is registered against. The new `ublCheckBuyerName()` walks `AccountingCustomerParty/Party/PartyLegalEntity` and reads `<cbc:RegistrationName>`, emitting one green `✓ Buyer PartyLegalEntity/RegistrationName "{name}" present (EN 16931 BR-07 / BT-44)` row when the value is present, or one red `✗ AccountingCustomerParty is missing Party/PartyLegalEntity/RegistrationName — …` row otherwise. Together with draft 111 the inspector now exercises both EN 16931 schematron party-name presence rules. The canonical UBL_INVOICE fixture's customer block was extended with `<cac:PartyLegalEntity><cbc:RegistrationName>Beispiel SARL</cbc:RegistrationName></cac:PartyLegalEntity>` to reflect a clean PEPPOL invoice; the buyer-VAT tests' replace anchors were updated to key off the new line. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with RegistrationName=Beispiel SARL → confirmation citing BR-07 / BT-44; same fixture with the PartyLegalEntity block stripped → mismatch row citing the BR-07 schematron gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 192 → 196 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 120):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook 70-character cap per `<PstlAdr>/<AdrLine>` — paired with draft 121 on the UBL side, and the length companion to draft 118's 2-line cardinality cap (mirror of how draft 114 was the length companion to draft 110's name presence). ISO 20022 declares each `<AdrLine>` as `Max70Text` at the schema level, which already pins the cap; however many real-world generators source the value from CRM address columns that allow up to 140 codepoints (matching the widely used ISO 20022 Max140Text caps across pain.* free-text identifiers), and the per-line value is not gated by draft 118's 2-line cardinality cap. The EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks re-affirm the 70-character cap because the value is propagated end-to-end through SEPA rails into the receiving bank's account-statement address renderer, at a column width pinned to the legacy SWIFT MT103 / MT104 70-char address-line limit. A 140-character AdrLine is either silently truncated by the receiving bank (the statement reads a half-truncated address that doesn't match the contract on file — the worst outcome because the payment settles but reconciliation breaks) or hard-rejected by the originator bank's own pre-clearing gate. The canonical real-world failure is a CRM column carrying street + number + apartment + building-name + neighborhood as a single free-text line that exceeds 70 chars. Parallel in shape to draft 114 (counterparty Nm length): the cardinality companion (draft 118) already gates the line count; this gate gates per-line length, so the inspector now exercises both cardinality and length on the EPC postal-address attribute surface. The new `sepaCheckAdrLineLength()` walks every `<PstlAdr>` reachable under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, iterates non-empty `<AdrLine>` children, measures codepoint length via `[...v].length`, and emits one green `✓ Every populated AdrLine fits within the EPC SEPA Rulebook 70-character cap across all N non-empty line(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ PostalAddress #i line j "{value}" exceeds the 70-character cap ({len}/70 characters) — …` row per offending line keyed by 1-based PostalAddress index and line position. Empty / absent AdrLine entries skip silently. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with a Cdtr PstlAdr carrying two clean AdrLine entries → confirmation citing the 70-char cap; same fixture with a 76-char street + apartment + building + neighborhood line injected → mismatch row citing the offending PostalAddress / line index and the over-cap length). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 192 → 196 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 119):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-CO-15 / BT-112 file-wide `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>/<cbc:TaxInclusiveAmount>` non-negative gate — paired with draft 118 on the SEPA side. Completes the file-wide signed-monetary-anchor family alongside drafts 113 (BT-110 file-wide VAT), 115 (BT-106 file-wide line totals), 117 (BT-109 file-wide net), and 45 (BR-CO-16 arithmetic). EN 16931 BT-112 (Invoice total amount with VAT) is the gross figure carried by UBL as the `<cbc:TaxInclusiveAmount>` direct child of `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>`. BR-CO-15 pins BT-112 = BT-109 + Σ BT-110, and both inputs narrow to non-negative decimals, so BT-112 inherits the non-negativity invariant. UBL declares `<cbc:TaxInclusiveAmount>` as `xsd:decimal` which permits negative values, so a generator that bypasses BR-CO-15 and writes BT-112 directly (e.g. summary-only invoice templates that derive the figure from an external accounting system) can write a signed value that's schema-valid but rail-broken — the negative BT-112 propagates into the BR-CO-16 chain (PayableAmount = TaxInclusiveAmount − PrepaidAmount + PayableRounding) and the buyer's AP system reads the invoice as a credit. The new `ublCheckLegalMonTotalTaxInclSign()` reads `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>/<cbc:TaxInclusiveAmount>` via `sepaFirstChildLocal`, parses to `Number`, and emits one green `✓ LegalMonetaryTotal/TaxInclusiveAmount "{v}" is non-negative (EN 16931 BT-112)` row when the value is ≥ 0, or one red `✗ LegalMonetaryTotal/TaxInclusiveAmount declares a negative value "{v}" — …` row otherwise. With drafts 113 / 115 / 117 / 119 the inspector now exercises every document-level monetary anchor BR-CO-15 / BR-CO-16 totals chains depend on. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with TaxInclusiveAmount=119.00 → confirmation citing BT-112; same fixture with the value flipped to -119.00 → mismatch row citing BR-CO-15). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 188 → 192 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 118):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook 2-`<AdrLine>` cardinality cap per `<PstlAdr>` — paired with draft 119 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 declares `<PstlAdr>/<AdrLine>` with cardinality `0..7` (up to seven free-text address lines per party address); the EPC SCT and SDD Rulebook Implementation Guidelines narrow the maximum cardinality to 2 lines per address because SEPA party-address rendering downstream (account statement, regulator reporting feeds, AML screening) is pinned to a two-line layout matching the legacy SWIFT MT103 / MT104 address columns. A party block declaring three or more AdrLine elements is schema-valid plain XML but rail-broken: the receiving bank silently drops the third and subsequent lines (the customer sees a half-truncated address that doesn't match the contract on file) or the originator bank's pre-clearing gate hard-rejects the file. The canonical real-world failure is a CRM exporting the address in line-per-field shape (street, number, ZIP, city, country) without re-packing it into the SEPA two-line format. The new `sepaCheckAdrLineCount()` walks every `<PstlAdr>` reachable under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, counts non-empty `<AdrLine>` children, and emits one green `✓ Every PostalAddress fits within the EPC SEPA Rulebook 2-AdrLine cap …` row when every populated address fits within 2 lines, or one red `✗ PostalAddress #i declares {count} <AdrLine> elements — …` row per offending PostalAddress keyed by a 1-based index across the document. Addresses missing AdrLine entirely (legitimately omitted for in-scheme SEPA-zone counterparties where the bank uses the IBAN-linked address) skip silently. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with a Cdtr PstlAdr carrying two AdrLine elements → confirmation citing the 2-line cap; same fixture with a third AdrLine injected → mismatch row citing the offending PostalAddress and the over-cap count). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 188 → 192 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 117):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-CO-13 / BT-109 file-wide `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>/<cbc:TaxExclusiveAmount>` non-negative gate — paired with draft 116 on the SEPA side, and the third member of the document-level signed monetary anchor family after drafts 113 (BT-110 file-wide VAT) and 115 (BT-106 file-wide line totals). EN 16931 BT-109 (Invoice total amount without VAT) is the headline net figure on every invoice / credit note. BR-CO-13 composes BT-109 = BT-106 (line totals) + BT-99 (charges) − BT-92 (allowances), and each input narrows to a non-negative decimal, so BT-109 inherits the non-negativity invariant. UBL declares `<cbc:TaxExclusiveAmount>` as `xsd:decimal` which permits negative values; a generator that bypasses the per-line / allowance / charge composition and writes BT-109 directly (sometimes seen with summary-only invoice templates that derive the figure from an external accounting system) can write a signed value that's schema-valid but rail-broken — the negative BT-109 propagates into the BR-CO-15 chain (TaxInclusiveAmount = TaxExclusiveAmount + Σ TaxAmount) and the buyer's AP system reads the invoice as a credit. The new `ublCheckLegalMonTotalTaxExclSign()` reads `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>/<cbc:TaxExclusiveAmount>` via `sepaFirstChildLocal` (so nested values are not picked up), parses to `Number`, and emits one green `✓ LegalMonetaryTotal/TaxExclusiveAmount "{v}" is non-negative (EN 16931 BT-109)` row when the value is ≥ 0, or one red `✗ LegalMonetaryTotal/TaxExclusiveAmount declares a negative value "{v}" — …` row otherwise. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with TaxExclusiveAmount=100.00 → confirmation citing BT-109; same fixture with the value flipped to -100.00 → mismatch row citing BR-CO-13). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 184 → 188 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 116):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook single-`<Ustrd>`-per-`<RmtInf>` cardinality cap — paired with draft 117 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 declares `<RmtInf>/<Ustrd>` as `0..n` — multiple unstructured remittance lines per transaction are schema-valid. The EPC SCT (pain.001) and SDD (pain.008) Rulebook Implementation Guidelines narrow the maximum cardinality to a single occurrence per `<RmtInf>`: only one `<Ustrd>` line is allowed (the 140-character cap on individual line content is already gated by draft 87's Ustrd-length check). Clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) reject files whose RmtInf carries two or more Ustrd elements because the receiving bank's downstream account-statement renderer surfaces only one narrative line; any additional Ustrd elements are silently dropped before the customer sees them, so the rulebook forbids the shape upfront. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that concatenates a multi-line invoice description by writing each line as its own `<Ustrd>` element, intending the bank to render them as a block — schema-valid but rail-broken. Distinct from draft 108's Strd / Ustrd XOR gate: that validates which carrier is used; this gate validates the cardinality within the Ustrd carrier. The new `sepaCheckRmtInfUstrdCount()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, counts non-empty `<Ustrd>` children inside each `<RmtInf>`, and emits one green `✓ Every transaction's RmtInf carries at most one <Ustrd> element across all N populated RmtInf block(s) …` row when every populated RmtInf carries ≤ 1 Ustrd, or one red `✗ Transaction #i RmtInf carries {count} <Ustrd> elements — …` row per offending transaction keyed by the 1-based index. Empty / absent RmtInf blocks skip silently. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with single Ustrd → confirmation citing the EPC narrowing; same fixture with a second `<Ustrd>Line 2 description</Ustrd>` injected alongside the canonical one → mismatch row citing transaction #1 and the 2-element count). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 184 → 188 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 115):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-CO-10 / BT-106 file-wide `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>/<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` non-negative gate — paired with draft 114 on the SEPA side, and the file-wide counterpart to draft 107's per-line BT-131 gate. EN 16931 BT-106 (Sum of Invoice line net amounts) is carried by UBL as the `<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` that appears as a direct child of `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>` at the document root — distinct from the per-line `<cac:InvoiceLine>/<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` (BT-131) nested below. BR-CO-10 pins the file-wide value to Σ BT-131, and BT-131 is itself non-negative (draft 107), so BT-106 as the sum must be non-negative too; UBL declares `<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` as `xsd:decimal` which permits negative values, so a generator that bypasses the per-line totals and writes the file-wide figure directly (sometimes seen with summary-only invoice templates that derive BT-106 from an external accounting system rather than summing the lines) can write a signed value that's schema-valid but rail-broken — the negative BT-106 propagates into the BR-CO-10 totals chain and the buyer's AP system reads the invoice as a credit. Parallel in shape to draft 113 (file-wide BT-110 sign): both validate document-level signed monetary anchors. The new `ublCheckLegalMonTotalLineSign()` reads `<cac:LegalMonetaryTotal>/<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` via `sepaFirstChildLocal` (so nested per-subtotal / per-line values are not picked up), parses to `Number`, and emits one green `✓ LegalMonetaryTotal/LineExtensionAmount "{v}" is non-negative (EN 16931 BT-106)` row when the value is ≥ 0, or one red `✗ LegalMonetaryTotal/LineExtensionAmount declares a negative value "{v}" — …` row otherwise. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with file-wide LineExtensionAmount=100.00 → confirmation citing BT-106; same fixture with the file-wide value flipped to -100.00 (per-line value left positive so only BT-106 trips) → mismatch row citing BR-CO-10). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 180 → 184 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 114):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook counterparty-name 70-character length cap on every `<CdtTrfTxInf>/<Cdtr>/<Nm>` (pain.001 SCT, AT-03 Name of the Beneficiary) and `<DrctDbtTxInf>/<Dbtr>/<Nm>` (pain.008 SDD, AT-23 Name of the Debtor) — paired with draft 115 on the UBL side, and the length counterpart to draft 110's name-presence gate. ISO 20022 declares the slot as `Max140Text` — Unicode strings up to 140 codepoints pass the schema; the EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks narrow the allowed length to 70 characters because the value is propagated end-to-end through SEPA rails into the receiving bank's account-statement renderer, at a column width pinned to the legacy SWIFT MT103 / MT104 70-char narrative limit. A 140-character name that passes ISO 20022 schema validation is either silently truncated by the receiving bank (the statement reads a half-truncated company name the customer can't reconcile — the worst outcome because the payment settles but reconciliation breaks) or hard-rejected by the originator bank's own pre-clearing gate. The canonical real-world failure is a CRM column carrying legal name plus department / branch suffix ("ACME Widgets GmbH / Hamburg Branch — Procurement Department") that exceeds 70 chars but sits well below the 140-char ISO 20022 cap. Parallel in shape to drafts 80-90 (length checks for free-text identifiers); together with draft 110 (presence) the inspector now exercises both presence and length on the EPC counterparty-name attribute surface. The new `sepaCheckCounterpartyNameLength()` walks every transaction in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, reads the counterparty's `<Nm>` (trimmed), measures codepoint length via `[...nm].length`, and emits one green `✓ Every {party}/Nm value fits within the EPC SEPA Rulebook 70-character cap across all N transaction(s) …` row when every value fits, or one red `✗ Transaction #i {party}/Nm "{nm}" exceeds the 70-character cap ({len}/70) — …` row per offending value. Transactions that omit `<Nm>` skip silently (caught by draft 110's presence gate). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with Cdtr/Nm=Supplier GmbH (13 chars) → confirmation citing the 70-char cap; same fixture with a 76-char legal-name-plus-department-plus-branch suffix → mismatch row citing transaction #1 and the over-cap value). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 180 → 184 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 113):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-CO-13 / BR-DEC-13 / BT-110 file-wide `<cac:TaxTotal>/<cbc:TaxAmount>` non-negative gate — paired with draft 112 on the SEPA side. EN 16931 BT-110 (Invoice total VAT amount) is carried by UBL as the `<cbc:TaxAmount>` that appears as a direct child of `<cac:TaxTotal>` at the document root — distinct from the per-subtotal `<cac:TaxSubtotal>/<cbc:TaxAmount>` (BT-117) nested below. BR-CO-13 / BR-DEC-13 pin BT-110 to a non-negative decimal: an invoice's file-wide VAT total can be zero (zero-rated supply, exempt invoice, intra-EU reverse charge) but never negative — even a CreditNote carries a positive VAT total because the document-type code already conveys the credit direction (UNCL 1001 = 381 / 396 / 532, draft 47). UBL declares `<cbc:TaxAmount>` as `xsd:decimal` which permits negative values; EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 narrow the allowed set to non-negative decimals because a negative file-wide VAT silently inverts the sign of the buyer's input-VAT entry and breaks the per-period VAT reporting chain. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that reuses the same invoice shape for charges and refunds and flips the sign on every monetary slot rather than switching the document-type code. Distinct from drafts 103 (PriceAmount sign), 107 (LineExtensionAmount sign), and 28 (VAT totals arithmetic): those validate inputs / arithmetic; this gate validates the document-level BT-110 sign so the math means what the BR-CO rules say it means. The new `ublCheckTaxTotalSign()` walks every `<cac:TaxTotal>` reachable from the root, reads its direct-child `<cbc:TaxAmount>` (via `sepaFirstChildLocal` so the nested TaxSubtotal/TaxAmount values are not picked up), parses to `Number`, and emits one green `✓ Every TaxTotal declares a non-negative TaxAmount across all N TaxTotal block(s) (EN 16931 BR-CO-13 / BT-110)` row when every parsed value is ≥ 0 or one red `✗ TaxTotal #i declares a negative TaxAmount "{v}" — …` row per offending TaxTotal. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with TaxTotal/TaxAmount=19.00 → confirmation citing BT-110; same fixture with the file-wide value flipped to -19.00 (per-subtotal value left positive so only BT-110 trips) → mismatch row citing TaxTotal #1 and BR-CO-13). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 176 → 180 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 112):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook IBAN-only account-identification gate on every `<DbtrAcct>` and `<CdtrAcct>` — paired with draft 113 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 declares the CashAccount38 identification slot as a choice between `<IBAN>` and `<Othr>` (the BBAN / domestic account number / proprietary identifier alternative); both options pass the XML schema. The EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks narrow the allowed set to a single value — IBAN-only — for every account block carried in a SEPA pain.001 / pain.008 file. SEPA's interbank-settlement design is pinned to IBAN routing; an `<Othr>`-identified account is schema-valid plain XML but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) cannot route the leg because the BIC of the holding bank can't be derived from a non-IBAN identifier, and the file is rejected before any rail-level processing fires. The canonical real-world failure is a template repurposed from a non-SEPA domestic-rail flow (BACS, NACHA, FedWire) where account identification used a BBAN / proprietary number; the account slot wasn't migrated to IBAN when the file moved onto SEPA rails. The new `sepaCheckIbanOnly()` walks every `<DbtrAcct>` and `<CdtrAcct>` reachable under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, drills into `<Id>`, and tests for `<IBAN>` vs `<Othr>` presence; emits one green `✓ Every DbtrAcct / CdtrAcct identifies the account by <IBAN> across all N account block(s) …` row when every account block identifies by IBAN, or one red `✗ {role} #i identifies the account via <Othr> or with no <IBAN> child — …` row per offending block keyed by the 1-based index and the role (DbtrAcct / CdtrAcct). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with both DbtrAcct + CdtrAcct identified by IBAN → confirmation citing the EPC narrowing; same fixture with the DbtrAcct IBAN replaced by a `<Othr><Id>0532013000</Id></Othr>` block → mismatch row citing DbtrAcct #1 and the rail-broken Othr alternative). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 176 → 180 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 111):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-08 / BT-27 (Seller name) presence check on `<cac:AccountingSupplierParty>/<cac:Party>/<cac:PartyLegalEntity>/<cbc:RegistrationName>` — paired with draft 110 on the SEPA side. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 require every Invoice and CreditNote to declare the seller's legal-entity registration name; the value is the name printed on the buyer's purchase ledger and the name AP / VAT systems pair with the BT-31 Seller VAT identifier (draft 57) for tax reporting. UBL 2.1 marks both `<cac:PartyLegalEntity>` and `<cbc:RegistrationName>` optional at the schema level, so a supplier party carrying only `<cac:PartyName>/<cbc:Name>` (the trading name, BT-28) passes XML validation but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-08 schematron gate. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that fills the trading-name slot from a CRM column but leaves the legal-entity block empty because the company-registry integration is wired to a separate, sometimes-stale data source. Distinct from draft 57's BT-31 Seller VAT identifier check: that validates the identifier value; this validates the name the identifier is registered against. The new `ublCheckSellerName()` walks `AccountingSupplierParty/Party/PartyLegalEntity` and reads `<cbc:RegistrationName>`, emitting one green `✓ Seller PartyLegalEntity/RegistrationName "{name}" present (EN 16931 BR-08 / BT-27)` row when the value is present, or one red `✗ AccountingSupplierParty is missing Party/PartyLegalEntity/RegistrationName — …` row otherwise. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with RegistrationName=ACME Widgets GmbH → confirmation citing BR-08 / BT-27; same fixture with the PartyLegalEntity block stripped → mismatch row citing the BR-08 schematron gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 172 → 176 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 110):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook counterparty legal-name presence check on every `<CdtTrfTxInf>/<Cdtr>/<Nm>` (pain.001 SCT, AT-03 Name of the Beneficiary) and `<DrctDbtTxInf>/<Dbtr>/<Nm>` (pain.008 SDD, AT-23 Name of the Debtor) — paired with draft 111 on the UBL side. Both the EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks pin the counterparty's legal name as a hard-required attribute: the value is what the receiving bank prints on the customer's statement (the canonical "who paid me / who am I paying" signal the rulebooks treat as user-facing rail metadata) and is the field anti-money-laundering and sanctions-screening systems key off (a missing name silently skips the screening lookup). ISO 20022 declares `<Cdtr>/<Nm>` and `<Dbtr>/<Nm>` as `Max140Text` and — in older pain.* versions — optional at the schema level, so a party block reduced to `<Cdtr/>` with only nested `<CdtrAcct>` passes XML validation but is rejected by SEPA clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) at the rulebook gate. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that defaults the party block to an account-only structure when the source database's name column is null (the value defaults to NULL when a contact was deleted but the open receivable / payable wasn't reassigned). The new `sepaCheckCounterpartyName()` walks every transaction in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper (selecting `<Cdtr>` for SCT or `<Dbtr>` for SDD by `detected.type`), reads the counterparty's `<Nm>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ Every transaction declares a {party}/Nm across all N transaction(s) (EPC SEPA Rulebook AT-03/AT-23 — counterparty legal name)` row when every transaction declares a non-empty name, or one red `✗ Transaction #i is missing {party}/Nm — …` row per offending transaction keyed by the 1-based index. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with Cdtr/Nm=Supplier GmbH → confirmation citing AT-03 and the 1-transaction count; same fixture with the Nm element stripped → mismatch row citing transaction #1 and the EPC SEPA rulebook gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 172 → 176 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 109):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BT-1 (Invoice number) 35-character length check on the root `<cbc:ID>` — paired with draft 108 on the SEPA side, and the third sibling in the downstream-rail-caps-the-value-the-UBL-field-carries family after drafts 91 (BuyerReference / BT-10) and 97 (OrderReference/ID / BT-13). UBL declares `<cbc:ID>` at the Invoice / CreditNote root as plain Identifier text with no schema-level length cap, and EN 16931 itself imposes none either; however the value is routinely copied verbatim into the SEPA `<EndToEndId>` (ISO 20022 Max35Text, 35-codepoint cap) by downstream pain.001 generators when the PEPPOL invoice is settled over SEPA rails — BT-1 is the canonical reconciliation key the creditor's bank-statement matching pairs against the open receivable, more universally than the optional BT-10 / BT-13 fields the prior gates covered. An invoice number longer than 35 characters forces the SEPA generator either to truncate (silent data loss across the reconciliation key — the worst outcome, because the invoice still settles but the creditor's AP / ERP matching breaks) or to fail the file at the Max35Text schema-validation gate. Schema-valid UBL, rail-broken downstream. The new `ublCheckInvoiceIdLength()` reads `<cbc:ID>` as a direct child of the document root (via `sepaFirstChildLocal` so the nested per-line and per-tax-category `<cbc:ID>` elements are not picked up), measures codepoint length via `[...id].length`, and emits one green `✓ Invoice/CreditNote ID (EN 16931 BT-1) fits within the 35-character SEPA EndToEndId cap across 1 reference(s) (≤35 characters)` row when the value fits, or one red `✗ Invoice/CreditNote ID "{id}" exceeds the 35-character cap ({n}/35 characters) — …` row otherwise. Skips silently when the optional root `<cbc:ID>` is absent. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `INV-2024-042` (12 chars) → confirmation citing the 35-char SEPA EndToEndId cap; same fixture with a 43-char `INV-2024-Subscription-Long-Customer-Ref-042` → mismatch row citing the over-cap and the SEPA-side truncation failure mode). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 168 → 172 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 108):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook `<RmtInf>` Strd / Ustrd mutual-exclusivity gate — paired with draft 109 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 declares `<RmtInf>` as a free container that may carry zero or more `<Ustrd>` elements and / or one `<Strd>` block (with CdtrRefInf) side-by-side; the EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks narrow the allowed set to exactly one form per occurrence — unstructured `<Ustrd>` OR structured `<Strd>`, never both. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that interpolates a human-readable description into `<Ustrd>` (for the buyer's accounting team) while a separate workflow attaches `<Strd>/<CdtrRefInf>/<Ref>` (the reconciliation key the creditor's bank-statement matching keys off) — both populated in the same `<RmtInf>`. Clearing systems reject at the rail-level rulebook gate because downstream reconciliation can't tell which carrier holds the canonical reference; the SCT Rulebook requires the originator to commit to one or the other so the creditor's pain.001-generator-fed AP system can route the reference deterministically. Distinct from drafts 94 (CdtrRefInf/Ref ISO 11649 MOD-97 check) and 67 (Ustrd EPC charset): both validate what is in each carrier; this gate validates which carrier may be used. The new `sepaCheckRmtInfXor()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, locates each transaction's `<RmtInf>`, tests whether both `<Strd>` and a non-empty `<Ustrd>` are present, and emits one green `✓ Every transaction's RmtInf carries at most one of <Strd> or <Ustrd> across all N populated RmtInf block(s) …` row when every populated RmtInf is single-form, or one red `✗ Transaction #i RmtInf populates both <Ustrd> and <Strd> — …` row per offending transaction keyed by the 1-based index. Empty or absent RmtInf blocks skip silently. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SCT / SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with Ustrd-only RmtInf → confirmation citing the EPC narrowing; same fixture with `<Strd><CdtrRefInf><Ref>INV-2024-001</Ref></CdtrRefInf></Strd>` injected alongside the existing Ustrd → mismatch row citing transaction #1 and the rail-level rulebook gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 168 → 172 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 107):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-26 / BR-CN-26 line `<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` non-negative gate — paired with draft 106 on the SEPA side. BR-26 (Invoices) and BR-CN-26 (CreditNotes) both require the per-line net amount (BT-131) to be non-negative. UBL declares the slot as `xsd:decimal` which permits negative values; EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 narrow the allowed set to non-negative decimals because a negative line amount silently inverts the file-wide LegalMonetaryTotal sum (drafts 28 / 45), produces a payable amount the buyer reads as a credit when the supplier meant a charge, and short-circuits the AllowanceCharge / CreditNote flow EN 16931 prescribes for genuine reductions. Distinct from draft 103 (PriceAmount sign): that gate validates the unit-price input; this gate validates the computed line-amount output, so a negative quantity × positive price (or vice versa) is also caught. The new `ublCheckLineAmountSign()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` / `<cac:CreditNoteLine>`, reads `<cbc:LineExtensionAmount>` (as `Number`), and emits one green `✓ Every line declares a non-negative LineExtensionAmount across all N line(s) (EN 16931 BR-26 / BR-CN-26 / BT-131)` row when every parsed value is ≥ 0, or one red `✗ Line #i declares a negative LineExtensionAmount "{v}" — …` row per offending line. Together with draft 103 the two line-level sign-invariant gates close out the renderer's totals-check dependencies. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE with LineExtensionAmount=100.00 → confirmation citing BR-26 / BT-131; same fixture with LineExtensionAmount=-100.00 → mismatch row citing line #1 and the BR-26 / BR-CN-26 prohibition). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 164 → 168 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 106):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an ISO 20022 `<NbOfTxs>` `Max15NumericText` shape check — paired with draft 107 on the UBL side, and orthogonal to drafts 35 + 92's value-parity invariants. ISO 20022 declares `<NbOfTxs>` (at both `GrpHdr` and per-PmtInf level) as `Max15NumericText`, a strict regex of 1-15 ASCII digits with no decimal point, sign, exponent, separators, or whitespace. The value-parity gates from drafts 35 and 92 exercise the arithmetic relation (declared count == actual transaction count) but route the comparison through `Number()`, which silently accepts `"1.0"`, `"01"`, `" 1 "`, scientific notation, and other shapes the schema rejects; this gate catches the shape itself, so a `<NbOfTxs>1.0</NbOfTxs>` (JSON-number-serialised) or `<NbOfTxs>1,000</NbOfTxs>` (locale-formatted) declaration is flagged even when the numeric value happens to equal the actual transaction count. Clearing systems reject both at the xs:Max15NumericText schema gate before any rail-level processing fires. The new `sepaCheckNbOfTxsShape()` walks `GrpHdr/NbOfTxs` and every `PmtInf/NbOfTxs`, tests each non-empty value against `^\d{1,15}$`, and emits one green `✓ NbOfTxs values all match the ISO 20022 Max15NumericText shape …` row when every value passes or one red `✗ {scope} declares "{v}" — …` row per offending occurrence (scope names the schema anchor: `GrpHdr/NbOfTxs` or `PmtInf #i/NbOfTxs`). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `NbOfTxs=1` → confirmation citing the Max15NumericText shape; same fixture with `NbOfTxs=1.0` → mismatch row citing GrpHdr/NbOfTxs and the Max15NumericText violation). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 164 → 168 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 105):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BT-157 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 GS1 GTIN check on `<cac:Item>/<cac:StandardItemIdentification>/<cbc:ID>` when `@schemeID="0160"` — paired with draft 104 on the SEPA side. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 narrows the EN 16931 BT-157 standard item identifier to a small set of scheme IDs from the PEPPOL Code List `ICD`; the most common is `"0160"` (GS1 Global Trade Item Number — GTIN-8 / GTIN-12 / GTIN-13 / GTIN-14). When schemeID=0160 is declared the value MUST satisfy the GS1 MOD-10 check digit on top of being a valid GTIN length; a bad-checksum GTIN is plain digit text that passes the XML schema but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BT-157 schematron gate, and downstream catalog / inventory systems silently fail to match the line to a master-data record. The new `ublCheckGtin()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` / `<cac:CreditNoteLine>`, drills into `<cac:Item>/<cac:StandardItemIdentification>/<cbc:ID>` and its `@schemeID` attribute, narrows to lines where schemeID equals exactly `"0160"`, and routes the value through the existing `gs1Validate()` helper that powers the §2.13 GS1 / EAN / GTIN tool — single-sourced. Lines whose StandardItemIdentification declares any other schemeID (`"0088"` GLN, `"0093"` ASN, or a non-PEPPOL internal scheme) are silently skipped, as are lines that omit StandardItemIdentification entirely (BT-157 is optional per EN 16931). Emits one green `✓ StandardItemIdentification GTIN(s) valid (GS1 MOD-10) across all N line(s) declaring schemeID="0160" …` row when every shaped value passes or one red `✗ StandardItemIdentification "{v}" with schemeID="0160" failed the GS1 GTIN MOD-10 check — …` row per offending value. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / GS1 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `StandardItemIdentification schemeID="0160">5901234123457` (canonical EAN-13 with passing MOD-10) injected → confirmation citing BT-157; same fixture with the final digit flipped to `5901234123458` → mismatch row citing the failed GS1 MOD-10 check). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 160 → 164 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 104):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook strictly-positive amount enforcement on every transaction-level `<InstdAmt>` / `<Amt>` — paired with draft 105 on the UBL side. Both the EPC SCT and SDD Rulebooks pin per-transaction amounts to a strictly positive decimal: zero-amount and negative-amount transactions are rejected at the rail-level amount gate (a negative would mean a refund / reverse — the EPC mechanism for those is a separate pain message, not a sign-flipped entry). ISO 20022 declares the amount slots as `xs:decimal` with no positivity constraint, so a `0.00` entry (commonly produced by a batch generator that forgets to exclude prelaunch / setup records) or a negative entry (commonly produced by a refund script that misuses the credit-transfer flow) is schema-valid plain text but rail-broken. The new `sepaCheckPositiveAmounts()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads each transaction's `<InstdAmt>` (pain.001 preferred) or `<Amt>` (pain.008 preferred), parses to `Number`, and emits one green `✓ Every transaction declares a positive InstdAmt / Amt across all N transaction(s) …` row when every value is > 0, or one red `✗ Transaction #i declares InstdAmt / Amt "{v}" — …` row per offending transaction. Skips transactions that lack a parseable amount (caught by upstream schema validation; CtrlSum invariants from drafts 35, 92 already exercise the arithmetic relation). camt.053 is intentionally out of scope — statement entries can legitimately be debits (negative from the holder's perspective) or zero (reservation entries). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 with InstdAmt=100.00 → confirmation citing the strictly-positive narrowing; same fixture with InstdAmt=0.00 → mismatch row citing transaction #1 and the EPC SEPA Rulebook requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 160 → 164 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 103):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-27 / BT-146 `<cac:Price>/<cbc:PriceAmount>` non-negative gate — paired with draft 102 on the SEPA side. UBL declares `<cbc:PriceAmount>` as `xsd:decimal` which permits negative values; EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 narrow the allowed set to non-negative decimals because a negative unit price silently inverts BR-CO-04 (LineExtensionAmount = Quantity × Price), turning a positive-quantity line into a negative line amount the buyer reads as a credit. The canonical real-world failure is a template that reuses the same line shape for charges and discounts and forgets to switch the discount to `<cac:AllowanceCharge>` (the EN-16931-correct mechanism for line-level discounts). Distinct from drafts 36 (line-amount arithmetic) and 45 (PayableAmount): those validate that the math holds; this gate validates the input sign so the math means what the BR rules say it means. The new `ublCheckPriceAmount()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` / `<cac:CreditNoteLine>` (selected via `detected.type`), reads `<cac:Price>/<cbc:PriceAmount>` (as `Number`), and emits one green `✓ Every line declares a non-negative Price/PriceAmount across all N line(s) (EN 16931 BR-27 / BT-146)` row when every parsed value is ≥ 0, or one red `✗ Line #i declares a negative Price/PriceAmount "{v}" — …` row per offending line. Skips lines that don't carry a Price / PriceAmount — those are caught by upstream presence gates. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE single-line fixture with PriceAmount=50.00 → confirmation citing BR-27 / BT-146; same fixture with PriceAmount=-50.00 → mismatch row citing line #1 and the BR-27 prohibition). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 156 → 160 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 102):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SDD Rulebook `<CdtrSchmeId>/<Id>/<PrvtId>/<Othr>/<SchmeNm>/<Prtry>` literal-"SEPA" enforcement — paired with draft 103 on the UBL side and pairing with draft 100's CID MOD-97-10 check at the same anchor. ISO 20022 declares `<Prtry>` as `Max35Text`, so any value passes the schema; the EPC SDD Rulebook narrows the allowed set to the literal string `SEPA` so the debtor's bank can route the mandate authorisation against the SEPA scheme rather than a domestic / non-SEPA scheme. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that defaults the scheme name to a domestic identifier ("SDD", a country-specific scheme code, or the creditor's internal scheme label) when the underlying flow is meant to be SEPA — the file balances at the schema level and at the CID MOD-97 gate (draft 100 passes), but the debtor's bank routes the mandate authorisation to a scheme the creditor isn't actually registered under, and the collection fails. The new `sepaCheckCdtrSchmePrtry()` walks every `<CdtrSchmeId>` reachable under the pain.008 wrapper, drills into `<Id>/<PrvtId>/<Othr>/<SchmeNm>/<Prtry>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ … declares the literal "SEPA" across all N CdtrSchmeId entry(ies) …` row when every value is exactly "SEPA", or one red `✗ … declares "{actual}" — …` row per offending value. pain.008-only — pain.001 credit transfers don't carry CdtrSchmeId. With drafts 100 + 102 in place both gates at the `<CdtrSchmeId>` anchor — the identifier (MOD-97-10) and the scheme it is declared under (literal "SEPA") — are validated. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (pain.008 fixture with `CdtrSchmeId` carrying SchmeNm/Prtry="SEPA" → confirmation citing the EPC SDD narrowing; same fixture with SchmeNm/Prtry="SDD" → mismatch row citing the offending value). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 156 → 160 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 101):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an EN 16931 BR-25 / BT-153 `<cac:Item>/<cbc:Name>` presence check — paired with draft 100 on the SEPA side. Every InvoiceLine / CreditNoteLine MUST carry an Item name — the human-readable description the buyer reads on the invoice to identify what was billed. UBL 2.1 marks `<cbc:Name>` optional at the schema level, so a line missing the slot passes XML validation but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-25 schematron gate; downstream AP / ERP systems either reject the line or render an empty description (the buyer sees a phantom charge they can't associate with a deliverable, which is the canonical dispute-trigger pattern).
