The typical real-world failure is a generator that keys the description off an internal SKU column that is sometimes empty (service-line lookups where the item master returns null). Parallel in shape to draft 99's line-quantity presence gate; together drafts 99 + 101 cover the two BR-rules that must hold on every line for the renderer to produce a non-degenerate output. Distinct from the line-amount arithmetic check (draft 36) and from the line-ID uniqueness check (draft 48): this gate validates the description so downstream renderers have something to display. The new `ublCheckItemName()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` / `<cac:CreditNoteLine>` (selected via `detected.type`), reads `<cac:Item>/<cbc:Name>` (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ Every line declares an Item/Name across all N line(s) (EN 16931 BR-25 / BT-153)` row when every line carries a value, or one red `✗ Line #i is missing an Item/Name — …` row per offending line. Skips silently when no lines are present (the file is degenerate at the BR-16 gate, caught upstream). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE single-line fixture → confirmation citing BR-25 / BT-153; same fixture with the `<cbc:Name>Widget Pro</cbc:Name>` stripped → mismatch row citing line #1 and the BR-25 requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 152 → 156 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 100):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a SEPA Creditor Identifier (CID) MOD-97-10 check on `<CdtrSchmeId>/<Id>/<PrvtId>/<Othr>/<Id>` — paired with draft 101 on the UBL side. The CID is the EPC SDD Rulebook's identifier that authenticates the creditor on every SEPA direct debit; it carries the legal anchor the debtor's bank uses to validate the mandate authorisation against the creditor identity. Format: 2-char ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country code + 2 check digits + 3-char Creditor Business Code (defaults to "ZZZ"; other 3-char alphanumeric values identify sub-creditors within the same legal entity) + 1-28 char national identifier. The check digits are computed by stripping the business code from positions 4-6 and running the standard IBAN-like MOD-97-10 walk on the remainder: rearrange so country + check digits go to the end, convert letters A-Z to 10-35, MOD 97 must equal 1. A bad-checksum CID is schema-valid plain text but rejected at the EPC SDD rulebook gate — the debtor's bank can't anchor the mandate authorisation against the creditor identity, and the collection fails before any rail-level processing fires. The typical real-world failure is a generator that interpolates the creditor's legal name into the national-identifier slot without recomputing the check digits. The new `sepaCheckCid()` walks every `<CdtrSchmeId>` reachable under the pain.008 wrapper, drills into `<Id>/<PrvtId>/<Othr>/<Id>` (trimmed + uppercased), gates against the `^[A-Z]{2}\d{2}[A-Z0-9]{3}[A-Z0-9]{1,28}$` shape, and routes matching values through the existing `ibanMod97()` helper (algorithm single-sourced with drafts 1, 94, 95). Emits one green `✓ SEPA Creditor Identifier(s) valid (MOD-97-10) across all N CID(s) …` row when every shaped value passes or one red `✗ SEPA Creditor Identifier "{cid}" failed MOD-97-10 check — …` row per offending value. pain.008-only — pain.001 credit transfers don't carry CIDs. Marks the 100th paired SEPA+UBL gate landed on this branch of the spec — alongside drafts 94/95 (ISO 11649) the inspector now exercises every MOD-97-10-based identifier on the SEPA + UBL surface (IBAN, RF Creditor Reference, SEPA CID). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SDD Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (pain.008 fixture with the canonical EPC SDD example `DE98ZZZ09999999999` injected → confirmation citing the MOD-97-10 check; same fixture with the final digit flipped to `DE98ZZZ09999999998` → mismatch row citing the failed MOD-97 check). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 152 → 156 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 99):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a line-level `<cbc:InvoicedQuantity>` / `<cbc:CreditedQuantity>` presence check — paired with draft 98 on the SEPA side. EN 16931 BR-21 (Invoices) and BR-CN-2 (CreditNotes) require every line to declare a quantity; EN 16931 BR-CO-04 (LineExtensionAmount = Quantity × Price) depends on that value, so a line missing the quantity slot has no derivable basis for its declared amount. UBL 2.1 marks both elements optional at the schema level, so a line missing the quantity passes XML validation but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-21 / BR-CN-2 schematron gate; downstream AP / ERP systems either reject the line or default it to quantity 1 (silent data fabrication — the worst outcome, because the line amount silently reinterprets as a unit price). The typical real-world failure is a template that uses an Item without a quantity column (free-text "service" lines) or a generator that suppresses the element when the value is zero. Distinct from the line-amount arithmetic check (draft 36) which assumes quantity is declared and validates the math: this gate validates presence so the math gate can fire downstream. The new `ublCheckLineQuantity()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` / `<cac:CreditNoteLine>` (selected via `detected.type`), reads the corresponding quantity element (trimmed), and emits one green `✓ Every line declares a InvoicedQuantity across all N line(s) (EN 16931 BR-21 / BR-CN-2 / BR-CO-04)` row when every line carries a value, or one red `✗ Line #i is missing a InvoicedQuantity — …` row per offending line keyed by the 1-based line index. Skips silently when no lines are present — the file is degenerate at the BR-16 (at least one line) gate, caught upstream. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL_INVOICE single-line fixture → confirmation citing BR-21 / BR-CN-2 / BR-CO-04; same fixture with the `InvoicedQuantity` element stripped → mismatch row citing line #1 and the BR-21 requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 148 → 152 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 98):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an EPC SEPA Rulebook EUR-only currency enforcement — paired with draft 99 on the UBL side. The ISO 4217 active-codes cross-check from draft 29 verifies every `Ccy` attribute against the active ISO 4217 set; this gate narrows the allowed set further to a single value — `EUR` — for pain.001 and pain.008 instruction files. The EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both pin the only valid currency for SEPA-zone transactions to EUR; a pain.001 carrying `Ccy="USD"` (or any other ISO 4217 code) is schema-valid plain XML, passes the ISO 4217 active-codes gate (USD is current), but is rejected by SEPA clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) at the rail-level currency gate as non-SEPA. The typical real-world failure is a template repurposed across SEPA and cross-border wire-transfer flows where the Ccy attribute was not switched back to EUR before sending. The new `sepaCheckEurOnly()` walks every element under the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, collects each `@Ccy` attribute (uppercased + deduped), and emits one green `✓ All @Ccy attributes declare EUR …` row when every value is EUR, or one red `✗ Non-EUR currency code @Ccy="{code}" declared — …` row per offending value. camt.053 (bank-to-customer statement) is intentionally skipped — statement-side messages can legitimately mix currencies for accounts that hold non-EUR sub-balances. Distinct from drafts 27 (BIC format cross-check) and 29 (ISO 4217 active codes): both validate identifiers / codes against an external standard; this check applies the SEPA-zone-specific narrowing on top. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA Rulebook vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `Ccy="EUR"` → confirmation citing the EPC SEPA EUR-only restriction; same fixture with `Ccy="USD"` injected → mismatch row citing the SEPA-zone non-SEPA failure mode). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 148 → 152 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 97):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:OrderReference/cbc:ID` (EN 16931 BT-13 Purchase order reference) 35-character length check — paired with draft 96 on the SEPA side, and the parallel sibling to draft 91's BuyerReference length gate. UBL declares `cac:OrderReference/cbc:ID` at the document root as plain Identifier text with no schema-level length cap, and EN 16931 itself imposes none either; however the value is routinely copied verbatim into the SEPA `<EndToEndId>` (ISO 20022 Max35Text, 35-codepoint cap) by downstream pain.001 generators when the PEPPOL invoice is settled over SEPA rails — alongside or in place of BuyerReference (BT-10), depending on which the supplier's settlement template keys off. An OrderReference/ID longer than 35 characters forces the SEPA generator either to truncate (silent data loss across the reconciliation key — the worst outcome, because the invoice still settles but the buyer's AP / ERP matching breaks) or to fail the file at the Max35Text schema-validation gate. Schema-valid UBL, rail-broken downstream — the same "downstream-rail caps the value the UBL field carries" pattern as drafts 89 (PaymentMandate/ID) and 91 (BuyerReference). The new `ublCheckOrderRefLength()` reads `<cac:OrderReference>/<cbc:ID>` from the root, measures via `[...ref].length`, and emits one green `✓ OrderReference/ID values (EN 16931 BT-13) all fit within the 35-character SEPA EndToEndId cap across 1 reference(s) (each ≤35 characters)` row when the value fits, or one red `✗ OrderReference/ID "{ref}" exceeds the 35-character cap ({n}/35 characters) — …` row otherwise. Skips silently when the optional OrderReference/ID is absent — draft 79's BuyerReference-OR-OrderReference presence gate already covers the absence case. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `OrderReference/ID=PO-2024-042` (11 chars) injected → confirmation citing the 35-char SEPA EndToEndId cap; same fixture with a 42-char `PO-2024-Subscription-Long-Customer-Ref-042` → mismatch row citing the 42/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 144 → 148 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 96):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a per-transaction `<DrctDbtTx>/<MndtRltdInf>/<DtOfSgntr>` xs:date format check — paired with draft 97 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 declares the mandate date-of-signature on every pain.008 direct-debit transaction as xs:date, the same canonical YYYY-MM-DD form already gated on ReqdExctnDt / ReqdColltnDt (draft 76) and CreDtTm (draft 74). The EPC SDD Rulebook elevates DtOfSgntr to a hard-reject field: the value is the legal anchor for the mandate's validity (it is what the debtor's bank checks against the mandate registry to authorise the collection), and clearing systems reject pain.008 files at the xs:date schema gate before any rail-level check fires. The typical real-world failure is a CRM exporting US-style MM/DD/YYYY (`03/15/2024`) or slash-separated `2024/03/15` into the slot — both schema-valid plain text, both rail-broken. pain.008-only — pain.001 credit transfers don't carry mandates. The new `sepaCheckDtOfSgntr()` walks every `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.008 wrappers, drills into `<DrctDbtTx>/<MndtRltdInf>/<DtOfSgntr>` (trimmed), and reuses the existing `SEPA_DATE_RE` regex + month/day sanity gate from draft 76. Emits one green `✓ MndtRltdInf/DtOfSgntr values all match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form across all N direct-debit transaction(s) …` row when every value is clean or one red `✗ MndtRltdInf/DtOfSgntr "{v}" does not match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form …` row per offending value. With this draft the SEPA inspector's xs:date / xs:dateTime gates cover every date-shaped element in pain.001 / pain.008 (CreDtTm, ReqdExctnDt, ReqdColltnDt, DtOfSgntr) and the pain.008-specific MndtRltdInf surface joins MndtId charset + length (drafts 55 / 88) for full schema-level coverage. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SDD / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (pain.008 fixture with `MndtRltdInf` extended to carry `DtOfSgntr=2024-03-15` → confirmation citing the 1-transaction count; same fixture with `03/15/2024` (US-style) → mismatch row citing the canonical xs:date form). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 144 → 148 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 95):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:PaymentMeans/cbc:PaymentID` ISO 11649 MOD-97-10 check — paired with draft 94 on the SEPA side. EN 16931 BT-83 (Remittance information) is carried by UBL as `cbc:PaymentID`; when the value is shaped like an ISO 11649 Structured Creditor Reference (`RF` + 2 check digits + 1-21 alphanumeric chars, total length 5-25 — the canonical form for EU-area structured remittance) it MUST satisfy the same MOD-97-10 checksum that powers IBAN. A PEPPOL invoice pasted with a typo'd RF reference passes the EN 16931 / PEPPOL schematron (UBL declares `cbc:PaymentID` as plain Identifier text) but breaks the supplier's downstream reconciliation when the SEPA pain.001 generator copies the value into `<RmtInf>/<Strd>/<CdtrRefInf>/<Ref>` and the creditor's bank-statement matching silently fails to pair the payment to the open receivable. Parallel in shape to draft 36's UBL IBAN MOD-97 cross-check and draft 94's SEPA RF check (same MOD-97 walk, just at a different anchor). The new `ublCheckPaymentIdRf()` walks every `<cac:PaymentMeans>/<cbc:PaymentID>`, trims and uppercases, gates against the `^RF\d{2}[A-Z0-9]{1,21}$` shape, and routes matching values through the existing `ibanMod97()` helper. Emits one green `✓ PaymentID Structured Creditor Reference(s) valid (ISO 11649 MOD-97-10) across all N reference(s)` row when every shaped value passes or one red `✗ PaymentID "{ref}" failed ISO 11649 MOD-97-10 check …` row per offending value. Non-RF-shaped PaymentIDs (free-text, internal scheme references) are silently skipped — they are not the wrong kind of identifier, just a different one. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / ISO 11649 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `PaymentID=RF18539007547034` (Wikipedia ISO 11649 canonical example, MOD-97-10 passes) → confirmation citing BT-83; same fixture with the final digit flipped to `RF18539007547035` → mismatch row citing the failed MOD-97 check). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 140 → 144 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 94):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an ISO 11649 Structured Creditor Reference MOD-97-10 check on `<RmtInf>/<Strd>/<CdtrRefInf>/<Ref>` — paired with draft 95 on the UBL side. The EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook's structured remittance information slot carries the creditor's reconciliation key; when the value is shaped like an ISO 11649 reference (`RF` + 2 check digits + 1-21 alphanumeric chars, total length 5-25) it MUST satisfy the same MOD-97-10 checksum that powers IBAN — rearrange so `RF` + check digits go at the end, convert letters A-Z to 10-35, run MOD 97, result must equal 1. ISO 20022 declares `<Ref>` as `Max35Text`, so a bad-checksum RF reference is schema-valid but rail-broken: the creditor's downstream reconciliation system silently fails to match the payment to the open receivable (a common cause is a CRM exporting the reference with a typo'd digit). The new `sepaCheckCdtrRefRf()` walks every `<CdtrRefInf>` reachable in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper, trims + uppercases each `<Ref>`, gates against the `^RF\d{2}[A-Z0-9]{1,21}$` shape, and routes matching values through the existing `ibanMod97()` helper (reused so the algorithm is single-sourced with the IBAN MOD-97 walk from draft 1). Emits one green `✓ Structured Creditor Reference(s) valid (ISO 11649 MOD-97-10) across all N reference(s)` row when every shaped value passes or one red `✗ Structured Creditor Reference "{ref}" failed ISO 11649 MOD-97-10 check …` row per offending value. Non-RF-shaped references (free-text, scheme-specific identifiers) are silently skipped. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 11649 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (pain.001 fixture with `<Strd>/<CdtrRefInf>/<Tp>/<CdOrPrtry>/<Cd>=SCOR` + `<Ref>=RF18539007547034` (Wikipedia canonical example) → confirmation row; same fixture with final digit flipped to `RF18539007547035` → mismatch row citing the failed MOD-97 check). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 140 → 144 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 93):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `<cbc:TaxPointDate>` ≤ `<cbc:IssueDate>` temporal-ordering check — paired with draft 92 on the SEPA side, and the second UBL temporal-ordering gate after draft 39's DueDate ≥ IssueDate. EN 16931 BT-7 (Value-added tax point date) records when VAT becomes accountable on the supply; BT-2 (Invoice issue date) records when the invoice was issued. Across every EU VAT regime the tax point precedes (or equals) issue — period-end VAT reporting bins the supply by tax point, and a tax-point date later than the issue date implies the supplier is declaring VAT for a reporting period that has not yet started (data-entry error, future-dating fraud pattern, or a template field-swap where the issue and tax-point columns were transposed). UBL declares both as plain xs:date so the inversion passes schema; the form check (draft 75 `ublCheckDateFormats`) already validates xs:date shape, this gate validates ordering. The new `ublCheckTaxPointDateOrder()` reads both root-level dates, parses via `Date.parse()`, and emits one green `✓ TaxPointDate {tpd} is on or before IssueDate {issue} (EN 16931 BT-7 / BT-2 — VAT cannot become accountable after the invoice is issued)` row when `TaxPointDate ≤ IssueDate`, or one red `✗ TaxPointDate {tpd} is later than IssueDate {issue} — …a tax point after the issue date implies the supplier is declaring VAT for a period that has not yet started …` row otherwise. Skips silently when either date is missing or unparseable — those degenerate cases are caught by the existing form gate upstream. Mirror of draft 39's DueDate ≥ IssueDate. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `TaxPointDate=2024-03-15` (before the 2024-04-01 issue date) injected → confirmation citing the BT-7 / BT-2 ordering; same fixture with `TaxPointDate=2024-05-15` → mismatch row citing both dates). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 136 → 140 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 92):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains per-PmtInf `<NbOfTxs>` + `<CtrlSum>` invariants — paired with draft 93 on the UBL side, and the per-block extension of the document-level invariants from draft 35. ISO 20022 declares both `<PmtInf>/<NbOfTxs>` and `<PmtInf>/<CtrlSum>` as optional but when present they MUST equal the count / sum of the `CdtTrfTxInf` / `DrctDbtTxInf` children of that block — the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and Direct Debit Rulebooks both treat per-block totals as hard reject reasons at the per-PmtInf schematron gate. The canonical real-world failure is a batch generator that refreshes the group-header pair correctly (so draft 35's gate passes) but forgets to refresh the per-block pair after a transaction is added or removed; the file balances at the document level and fails at the per-PmtInf gate downstream. Distinct from draft 35: that check validates document-wide totals against the full transaction set, this check validates per-block totals against per-block transactions. The new `sepaCheckPmtInfInvariants()` walks every `<PmtInf>` block in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, counts its `CdtTrfTxInf` + `DrctDbtTxInf` children, sums their `InstdAmt` / `Amt` in integer cents (±1¢ tolerance per the ISO 20022 IG rounding policy), and emits two independent gates per block: NbOfTxs parity (a single green `✓ PmtInf/NbOfTxs values match the per-block transaction count across all N PmtInf block(s)` row across all matching blocks or one red `✗ PmtInf #i NbOfTxs mismatch: block declares X but contains Y transaction(s) — …` row per offending block) and CtrlSum parity (parallel green/red rows). Blocks that omit either header element skip that gate silently. Parallel in shape to `sepaCheckGrpHdrInvariants`. 4 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `<PmtInf>` injected with `NbOfTxs=1` + `CtrlSum=100.00` (matching the single 100.00 EUR transaction) → both confirmation rows; same fixture with `NbOfTxs=2` + `CtrlSum=200.00` → both mismatch rows citing PmtInf #1, the declared values, and the actual values). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 136 → 140 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 91):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cbc:BuyerReference` (EN 16931 BT-10 Buyer reference) 35-character length check — paired with draft 90 on the SEPA side. UBL declares `cbc:BuyerReference` at the document root as plain text with no schema-level length cap, and EN 16931 itself imposes none either; however the value is routinely copied verbatim into the SEPA `<EndToEndId>` (ISO 20022 Max35Text, 35-codepoint cap) by downstream pain.001 generators when the PEPPOL invoice is settled over SEPA rails — the buyer reference IS the reconciliation key the supplier needs to surface on the bank statement. A BuyerReference longer than 35 characters forces the SEPA generator either to truncate (silent data loss across the reconciliation key, the worst outcome — the invoice still settles but the buyer's downstream matching against AP / ERP records breaks) or to fail the file at the Max35Text schema-validation gate. Schema-valid UBL, rail-broken downstream — the canonical pattern this gate catches. Parallel in shape to draft 89's `ublCheckMandateIdLength()` (same "downstream SEPA rail caps the value the UBL field carries" pattern); pairs cleanly with draft 79's BuyerReference-OR-OrderReference presence gate (presence is one rule, length is the orthogonal rule). The new `ublCheckBuyerRefLength()` reads the root `<cbc:BuyerReference>` (trimmed), measures via `[...ref].length`, and emits one green `✓ BuyerReference values (EN 16931 BT-10) all fit within the 35-character SEPA EndToEndId cap across 1 reference(s) (each ≤35 characters)` row when the value fits, or one red `✗ BuyerReference "{ref}" exceeds the 35-character cap ({n}/35 characters) — EN 16931 BT-10 is routinely copied verbatim into SEPA EndToEndId (ISO 20022 Max35Text); a longer value forces downstream SEPA generators to truncate (silent data loss across the reconciliation key) or to fail the file at the schema-validation gate` row otherwise. Skips silently when the optional BuyerReference is absent — draft 79's presence gate already covers the absence case. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `BuyerReference=PO-2024-042` (11 chars) injected → confirmation citing the 35-char SEPA EndToEndId cap; same fixture with a 42-char `PO-2024-Subscription-Long-Customer-Ref-042` → mismatch row citing the 42/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 132 → 134 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 90):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a per-PmtInf `<PmtTpInf>/<InstrPrty>` ISO 20022 Priority2Code enum check — paired with draft 91 on the UBL side, and the sixth PmtInf-level scalar enum gate after PmtMtd (draft 40), ChrgBr (draft 42), SvcLvl (draft 44), SeqTp (draft 46), and CtgyPurp/Cd (draft 72). ISO 20022 declares `InstrPrty` as `Priority2Code`, a closed two-value enumeration — `HIGH` (high priority) and `NORM` (normal priority). Anything else (the canonical mistake is a template repurposed from a non-SEPA wire-instruction flow that carried `URGT`, `URGP`, or lowercase `high`) is schema-invalid; clearing systems reject the file at the xs:schema gate before any rail-level check fires. The new `sepaCheckInstrPrty()` walks every `<PmtInf>` block in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads `PmtTpInf/InstrPrty` (case-folded and whitespace-stripped for paste-safety), and emits one green `✓ PmtTpInf/InstrPrty values match ISO 20022 Priority2Code (HIGH or NORM) across all N PmtInf block(s)` confirmation row across all matching blocks, or one red `✗ PmtTpInf/InstrPrty mismatch: PmtInf #i declares X but ISO 20022 Priority2Code only allows HIGH or NORM — clearing systems reject the file at the schema-validation gate` row per offending block keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index. `InstrPrty` is optional per the schema, so blocks that omit `PmtTpInf` or `InstrPrty` skip silently — parallel to the PmtMtd / ChrgBr / SvcLvl / SeqTp / CtgyPurp checks. With this draft the six scalar PmtInf-level ISO 20022 / EPC rulebook enum gates are complete. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `InstrPrty=NORM` injected → confirmation citing the Priority2Code allowed set; same fixture with `InstrPrty=URGT` → mismatch row citing PmtInf #1 and Priority2Code). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 132 → 134 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 89):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:PaymentMeans/cac:PaymentMandate/cbc:ID` (EN 16931 BT-89 Mandate reference identifier) 35-character length check — paired with draft 88 on the SEPA side. EN 16931 BT-89 is the UBL-side analogue of SEPA SDD's `<MndtId>` (ISO 20022 Max35Text); a UBL invoice that triggers a SEPA direct debit downstream MUST carry a mandate reference within the 35-character cap, because the value is what the creditor's pain.008 generator copies verbatim into `<MndtId>` at file-build time. A value over the cap is schema-valid UBL but rail-broken — the SDD instruction built from it fails the EPC SDD Rulebook MndtId Max35Text gate downstream, and the typical real-world cause mirrors the SEPA side (a debtor name folded into the mandate reference pushing the value past 35 chars). The new `ublCheckMandateIdLength()` walks every `<cac:PaymentMeans>` block, drills into the optional `<cac:PaymentMandate>/<cbc:ID>`, measures codepoint length via `[...id].length`, and emits one green `✓ PaymentMeans/PaymentMandate/ID values (EN 16931 BT-89 Mandate reference) all fit within the 35-character cap across N mandate(s) (each ≤35 characters)` row when every value fits or one red `✗ PaymentMeans/PaymentMandate/ID "{id}" exceeds the 35-character cap ({n}/35 characters) — EN 16931 BT-89 mirrors SEPA SDD MndtId (ISO 20022 Max35Text) …` row per offending value. Skips silently when no PaymentMandate block carries an ID — most invoices (credit-transfer-only) legitimately omit it. With drafts 88 + 89 the UBL inspector now mirrors SEPA's MndtId length gate at the field that feeds it. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with a PaymentMeans/PaymentMandate carrying `ID=MANDATE-2024-001` (16 chars) → confirmation citing the 1-mandate count; same fixture with a 49-char `MNDT-Subscriber-Long-Company-Name-GmbH-Co-KG-2024` → mismatch row citing the 49/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 128 → 132 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 88):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a per-transaction pain.008 `DrctDbtTx/MndtRltdInf/MndtId` ISO 20022 Max35Text length check — paired with draft 89 on the UBL side, and the fifth (and final) in the identifier-length series alongside drafts 80 (MsgId), 82 (EndToEndId), 84 (InstrId), and 86 (PmtInfId). ISO 20022 declares `MndtId` as `Max35Text`, a 35-codepoint schema cap; draft 55 covers the orthogonal EPC SDD Rulebook character-set rule on the same field but does not validate length. pain.008-only — pain.001 credit transfers don't carry mandate references. The canonical real-world failure is a debtor name folded into the mandate reference (e.g. `MNDT-Subscriber-Long-Company-Name-GmbH-Co-KG-2024` busts the cap; the originator typically writes the mandate reference to be human-readable so the cap is easy to overrun). Schema-invalid for ISO 20022 (clearing systems reject the file at the xs:Max35Text gate before any rail-level check fires). The new `sepaCheckMndtIdLength()` walks every `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.008 wrappers, drills into `<DrctDbtTx>/<MndtRltdInf>/<MndtId>` (trimmed), measures via `[...id].length`, and emits one green `✓ MndtId values fit within ISO 20022 Max35Text across all N direct-debit transaction(s) (each ≤35 characters)` row when every value fits or one red `✗ MndtId "{id}" exceeds ISO 20022 Max35Text ({n}/35 characters) — clearing systems reject the file at the schema-validation gate …` row per offending value. With drafts 80 / 82 / 84 / 86 / 88 the five free-text identifier surfaces ISO 20022 caps to Max35Text now all carry both schema-level gates (charset + length); the SEPA inspector's identifier-length series is complete. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SDD vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (pain.008 fixture with `MndtId=MANDATE-2024-001` (16 chars) → confirmation citing the 1-transaction count; same fixture with the same 49-char debtor-name mandate reference → mismatch row citing the 49/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 128 → 132 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 87):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:BillingReference/cac:InvoiceDocumentReference/cbc:IssueDate` xs:date format check — paired with draft 86 on the SEPA side, and the fourth distinct xs:date anchor in EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 after the root-level dates (draft 75), Delivery/ActualDeliveryDate (draft 81), and PaymentMeans/PaymentDueDate (draft 85). EN 16931 BT-26 (Preceding Invoice issue date) carries this anchor on credit notes (pointing at the invoice being credited) and on correction invoices (UNCL 1001 code 384 — pointing at the invoice being corrected). A document may reference multiple preceding invoices via repeated `<cac:BillingReference>` blocks. The new `ublCheckBillingRefDate()` walks every `<cac:BillingReference>`, drills into the nested `<cac:InvoiceDocumentReference>/<cbc:IssueDate>`, runs the same `UBL_DATE_RE` regex + month/day sanity gate that drafts 75 / 81 / 85 already use, and emits one green `✓ BillingReference/InvoiceDocumentReference/IssueDate values all match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form across N preceding-invoice reference(s) …` row when every value is clean or one red `✗ BillingReference/InvoiceDocumentReference/IssueDate "{v}" does not match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form …` row per offending value. Skips silently when no BillingReference carries an IssueDate — the field is optional per UBL 2.1 and stand-alone invoices legitimately omit it. With drafts 75 / 81 / 85 / 87 the inspector now exercises every xs:date anchor EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 declare on the UBL document family. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / xs:date vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with a BillingReference carrying `IssueDate=2024-03-15` injected → confirmation citing the 1-reference count; same fixture with `03/15/2024` (US-style MM/DD/YYYY) injected → mismatch row citing the canonical xs:date form). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 124 → 128 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 86):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a per-PmtInf `<PmtInfId>` ISO 20022 Max35Text length check — paired with draft 87 on the UBL side, and the fourth in the identifier-length series alongside drafts 80 (MsgId), 82 (EndToEndId), and 84 (InstrId). ISO 20022 declares `PmtInfId` as `Max35Text`, a 35-codepoint schema cap; draft 56 covers the orthogonal EPC SEPA character-set rule on the same field but does not validate length. PmtInfId is the per-payment-information-block identifier the originator uses to key per-batch reconciliation; the canonical real-world failure is a generator that interpolates a long internal key into the identifier (`BATCH-{originator-name}-{YYYY-MM-DD}-{seq}` busts the cap when the originator name alone runs long, e.g. a multi-word legal entity name). Schema-invalid for ISO 20022 (clearing systems reject the file at the xs:Max35Text gate before any rail-level check fires). The new `sepaCheckPmtInfIdLength()` walks every `<PmtInf>` block in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads each `<PmtInfId>` (trimmed), measures via `[...id].length`, and emits one green `✓ PmtInfId values fit within ISO 20022 Max35Text across all N PmtInf block(s) (each ≤35 characters)` row when every value fits or one red `✗ PmtInfId "{id}" exceeds ISO 20022 Max35Text ({n}/35 characters) — clearing systems reject the file at the schema-validation gate …` row per offending value. With drafts 80 / 82 / 84 / 86 the four free-text identifier surfaces ISO 20022 caps to Max35Text now all carry both schema-level gates (charset + length); pairs with the four xs:date gates on the UBL side (drafts 75 / 81 / 85 / 87) to close out schema-level form validation across the two document families. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `PmtInfId=PAY-001` (7 chars) → confirmation citing the 1-block count; same fixture with a 41-char per-batch identifier injected → mismatch row citing the 41/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 124 → 128 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 85):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:PaymentMeans/cbc:PaymentDueDate` xs:date format check — paired with draft 84 on the SEPA side. EN 16931 BT-9 (Payment due date) is carried at two distinct UBL anchors: the document root `cbc:DueDate` (covered for form by draft 75) and on each `cac:PaymentMeans` block as `cbc:PaymentDueDate`. Both shapes are plain `xs:date`. Multi-method invoices (e.g. BACS + card + standing order) carry one PaymentMeans block per method, and each may declare its own PaymentDueDate when settlement timings differ across rails — none of those values are exercised by the existing root-date gate. Same canonical failure modes as draft 75 — US-style `04/01/2024`, slash-separated `2024/05/01`, datetime spilled into the date slot — all schema-valid plain text but rejected by PEPPOL access points at the xs:date schematron gate. The new `ublCheckPaymentDueDate()` walks every `<cac:PaymentMeans>` block, reads each `<cbc:PaymentDueDate>` (trimmed), runs the same `UBL_DATE_RE` regex + month/day sanity gate that drafts 75 and 81 already use, and emits one green `✓ PaymentMeans/PaymentDueDate values all match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form across N PaymentMeans block(s) (EN 16931 BT-9 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row when every value is clean or one red `✗ PaymentMeans/PaymentDueDate "{v}" does not match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form …` row per offending value. Skips silently when no PaymentMeans block carries a PaymentDueDate — BT-9 may live solely at the root, in which case draft 75 already covers it. Together with drafts 75 (root-level dates + InvoicePeriod bounds) and 81 (Delivery/ActualDeliveryDate) this closes out the four xs:date surfaces in EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / xs:date vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with a PaymentMeans block carrying `PaymentDueDate=2024-05-01` injected → confirmation citing the 1-block count; same fixture with `05/01/2024` (US-style MM/DD/YYYY) → mismatch row citing the canonical xs:date form). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 120 → 124 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 84):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a per-transaction `PmtId/InstrId` ISO 20022 Max35Text length check — paired with draft 85 on the UBL side, and the third in the document-/transaction-level identifier-length series alongside drafts 80 (MsgId) and 82 (EndToEndId). ISO 20022 declares `InstrId` as `Max35Text`, a 35-codepoint schema cap; draft 53 covers the orthogonal EPC SEPA character-set rule on the same field but does not validate length. InstrId is the originator-side instruction key the EPC SCT/SDD Rulebooks describe as a free-text identifier set by the initiating party for its own bookkeeping — it is commonly populated from internal batch / sequence references that may exceed 35 chars when stitched together from multiple sub-keys (e.g. `BATCH-{YYYY-Qn}-SEQ-{nnnnnn}-SUBKEY-{ref}` busts the cap with a few moderately-long sub-references). Schema-invalid for ISO 20022 (clearing systems reject the file at the xs:Max35Text gate before any rail-level check fires). The new `sepaCheckInstrIdLength()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, measures each `PmtId/InstrId` via `[...id].length`, and emits one green `✓ InstrId values fit within ISO 20022 Max35Text across all N transaction(s) (each ≤35 characters)` row when every value fits or one red `✗ InstrId "{id}" exceeds ISO 20022 Max35Text ({n}/35 characters) — clearing systems reject the file at the schema-validation gate …` row per offending value. Together with drafts 53 (charset) this closes both schema-level constraints on the originator-side instruction identifier; with drafts 80 (MsgId) and 82 (EndToEndId) the three free-text identifier surfaces ISO 20022 caps to Max35Text now all carry both schema-level gates. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `InstrId=INSTR-000001` (12 chars) injected → confirmation citing the 1-transaction count; same fixture with a 41-char internal batch key injected → mismatch row citing the 41/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 120 → 124 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 83):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:Delivery/cac:DeliveryLocation/cac:Address/cac:Country/cbc:IdentificationCode` ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 check — paired with draft 82 on the SEPA side. EN 16931 BT-80 (Deliver-to country code) names this element; PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 narrows the value to a current ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code. UBL 2.1 declares `cbc:IdentificationCode` as plain text with no enumeration, so the same failure modes that drove drafts 68 (party-address country, BT-40 / BT-55) and 71 (item country of origin, BT-159) apply at the delivery-location layer too — `UK` (not an ISO code — Great Britain is `GB`), `EN`, `XX`, or trailing-whitespace artefacts pass the schema but break PEPPOL routing. Distinct from drafts 68 and 71 in scope: those gates cover the supplier/customer postal-address country and the per-line item country of origin; this gate covers the deliver-to country, which is the field a buyer's customs broker and the Intrastat statistical reporting both key off when an invoice declares delivery to a different jurisdiction than the buyer's billing address. The new `ublCheckDeliveryCountry()` drills into `cac:Delivery/cac:DeliveryLocation/cac:Address/cac:Country/cbc:IdentificationCode`, trims, and validates against the shared `ISO_3166_1_ALPHA2` set (data single-sourced across drafts 68 / 69 / 70 / 71 / 83). Emits one green `✓ Delivery country code … {code} is a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code (EN 16931 BT-80 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row on match, or one red `✗ Delivery country code … "{code}" is not a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code …` row otherwise. Skips silently when no Delivery / DeliveryLocation / Address / Country / IdentificationCode chain is present — BT-80 is optional per PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 and domestic invoices without a separate delivery address legitimately omit it. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with the full Delivery / DeliveryLocation chain and `IdentificationCode=DE` injected → confirmation citing BT-80; same chain with `UK` → mismatch row citing `GB` as the canonical alternative). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 116 → 120 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 82):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a per-transaction `PmtId/EndToEndId` ISO 20022 Max35Text length check — paired with draft 83 on the UBL side, and the transaction-level extension of draft 80's `GrpHdr/MsgId` length gate. ISO 20022 declares `EndToEndId` as `Max35Text`, a 35-codepoint schema cap; draft 51 covers the orthogonal EPC SEPA character-set rule on the same field but does not validate length. The canonical real-world failure is a CRM-supplied invoice / customer reference that exceeds 35 chars — e.g. `Customer-{UUID}` whose 36-char UUID alone busts the cap, or `INV-{YYYY-MM-DD}-{customer-name}` where a long company name pushes the value past the limit. Schema-invalid for ISO 20022 (clearing systems reject the file at the xs:Max35Text gate before any rail-level check fires), but commonly missed because the originator-side application never warned about the length. The new `sepaCheckE2eIdLength()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, measures each `PmtId/EndToEndId` value via `[...id].length` (so surrogate pairs in pasted Unicode aren't double-counted), and emits one green `✓ EndToEndId values fit within ISO 20022 Max35Text across all N transaction(s) (each ≤35 characters)` row when every value fits or one red `✗ EndToEndId "{id}" exceeds ISO 20022 Max35Text ({n}/35 characters) — clearing systems reject the file at the schema-validation gate …` row per offending value. Transactions without an EndToEndId skip silently. Together with drafts 51 (charset) and 43 (uniqueness) this closes the three schema-level constraints on the transaction-level reconciliation identifier. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `EndToEndId=E2E-001` (7 chars) → confirmation citing the 1-transaction count; same fixture with a 40-char CRM-style reference injected → mismatch row citing the 40/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 116 → 120 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 81):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:Delivery/cbc:ActualDeliveryDate` (EN 16931 BT-72) xs:date format check — paired with draft 80 on the SEPA side. UBL 2.1 declares the field as plain `xs:date`, which requires the canonical `YYYY-MM-DD` form; draft 75 covered the root-level date surface (IssueDate / DueDate / TaxPointDate) plus the InvoicePeriod bounds, and this gate extends the same regex (`^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$` with month 01-12 / day 01-31 sanity) to the Delivery block, which sits one level deeper at `cac:Delivery`. The canonical real-world failure modes mirror draft 75 but at the delivery-date layer: US-style `04/15/2024`, slash-separated `2024/04/15`, or a datetime spilled into the date slot (`2024-04-15T00:00:00`) — all schema-valid plain text but rejected by PEPPOL access points at the xs:date schematron gate. The new `ublCheckDeliveryDate()` reads `cac:Delivery/cbc:ActualDeliveryDate` (trimmed), validates against the existing `UBL_DATE_RE`, and emits one green `✓ Delivery/ActualDeliveryDate {v} matches the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form (EN 16931 BT-72 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row on match or a red `✗ Delivery/ActualDeliveryDate "{v}" does not match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form …` row otherwise. Skips silently when no Delivery block (or no ActualDeliveryDate child) is present — BT-72 is optional per PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 and over-the-counter / digital-only supplies legitimately omit it. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / xs:date vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `Delivery/ActualDeliveryDate=2024-04-15` injected → confirmation citing BT-72; same fixture with `04/15/2024` (US-style MM/DD/YYYY) injected → mismatch row citing the canonical xs:date form). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 112 → 116 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 80):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `GrpHdr/MsgId` ISO 20022 Max35Text length check — paired with draft 81 on the UBL side, and complementary to draft 49's character-set check on the same field. ISO 20022 declares `MsgId` as `Max35Text`, a 35-codepoint schema cap; draft 49 covers the orthogonal EPC SEPA character-set rule on the same field but does not validate length. The canonical real-world failure is a generator that uses a full UUID (36 chars — exactly one over the cap) for the document identifier — schema-valid bytes for a CRM that issues UUIDs by default, but schema-invalid for ISO 20022 (clearing systems reject the file at the xs:Max35Text gate before any rail-level check fires). The new `sepaCheckMsgIdLength()` reads `GrpHdr/MsgId` on pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope), measures codepoint length via `[...msgId].length` so surrogate pairs in pasted Unicode aren't double-counted, and emits one green `✓ GrpHdr/MsgId {msgId} fits within ISO 20022 Max35Text ({n}/35 characters)` row when within the cap or a red `✗ GrpHdr/MsgId "{msgId}" exceeds ISO 20022 Max35Text ({n}/35 characters) — clearing systems reject the file at the schema-validation gate …` row otherwise. Together with draft 49 (charset) this closes both schema-level constraints on the document-level identifier. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `MsgId=MSG-2024-001` (12 chars) → confirmation citing the 12/35 ratio; same fixture with a 36-char UUID injected → mismatch row citing the 36/35 over-cap). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 112 → 116 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 79):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cbc:BuyerReference` (BT-10) / `cac:OrderReference/cbc:ID` (BT-13) presence check (paired with draft 78's SEPA temporal-ordering gate). PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 PEPPOL-EN16931-R003 requires a PEPPOL invoice to declare either the buyer reference or the purchase-order reference — the two fields are alternates and at least one MUST be present so the buyer's accounts-payable team can match the invoice against an internal reference. UBL 2.1 marks both as optional at the schema level so an invoice missing both is schema-valid plain XML but PEPPOL access points reject at the PEPPOL-EN16931-R003 schematron gate; France's Chorus Pro public-sector portal additionally pins BuyerReference for B2G flows (the "Service Code" the central government uses to route the invoice to the right department). The new `ublCheckBuyerOrOrderRef()` reads both fields and emits one green confirmation row when at least one is present (specialised string by which field is declared — BuyerReference only, OrderReference only, or both), or one red `✗ Neither BuyerReference (BT-10) nor OrderReference/ID (BT-13) is declared — PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 PEPPOL-EN16931-R003 …` row when both are missing. Invoice-only — credit notes are exempt from PEPPOL-EN16931-R003. 4 new strings × 5 locales for the four surfacing modes (both / buyer-only / order-only / missing) — en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern. Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with BuyerReference=PO-2024-042 injected → confirmation citing BT-10; canonical fixture (neither declared) → mismatch row citing PEPPOL-EN16931-R003). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 108 → 112 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 78):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a temporal-ordering check between `GrpHdr/CreDtTm` (file creation timestamp, draft 74) and the per-PmtInf `ReqdExctnDt` / `ReqdColltnDt` (settlement date, draft 76) — paired with draft 79 on the UBL side. ISO 20022 doesn't forbid the inversion at the schema level, but clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) reject any pain.001 / pain.008 whose requested execution / collection date precedes the file creation timestamp because the settlement engine reads the gap as a back-dated instruction. The canonical real-world cause is a template repurposed from a prior cycle where `CreDtTm` was refreshed to today but `ReqdExctnDt` wasn't bumped forward, leaving last week's settlement date in place. Parallel in shape to draft 39's UBL DueDate ≥ IssueDate gate. The new `sepaCheckCreDtTmReqdDtOrder()` reads the date prefix of `GrpHdr/CreDtTm` (the `YYYY-MM-DD` prefix is stable across all valid ISO 20022 ISODateTime shapes — both the format and the xs:date pattern are pre-validated by drafts 74 and 76), walks every `<PmtInf>`, picks the right field by `detected.type`, unwraps the optional `<Dt>` child, parses both as Dates, and compares. Emits one green `✓ {field} is on or after GrpHdr/CreDtTm {cre} across all N PmtInf block(s) …` row across all matching blocks, or one red `✗ {field} ordering mismatch: PmtInf #i declares X which precedes GrpHdr/CreDtTm Y …` row per offending block keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index. With drafts 74 / 76 / 78 the SEPA inspector now covers both format (xs:dateTime + xs:date) and semantic ordering (CreDtTm ≤ settlement date) for every date-bearing surface in pain.001 / pain.008. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with CreDtTm=2024-01-15 / ReqdExctnDt=2024-01-20 → confirmation citing the ordering; same fixture with `ReqdExctnDt=2024-01-10` → mismatch row citing the back-dated case and both timestamps). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 108 → 112 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 77):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cbc:EndpointID` presence check on both `AccountingSupplierParty/Party` and `AccountingCustomerParty/Party` (paired with draft 76's SEPA `ReqdExctnDt` / `ReqdColltnDt` xs:date gate). EN 16931 BT-34 (Seller electronic address) / BT-49 (Buyer electronic address) and PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 BR-62 / BR-63 require every PEPPOL invoice to declare an electronic address for both parties via `cbc:EndpointID`; the address is what PEPPOL access points key off when routing the document through the SML / SMP discovery layer. A missing supplier or customer EndpointID means the document has no routing destination, and access points reject at the schematron gate before any content-level check fires. UBL 2.1 marks the element optional at the schema level so this is exactly the kind of presence gate this inspector is designed to surface; the canonical real-world cause is a UBL template repurposed from a non-PEPPOL flow where routing was handled out-of-band, leaving the original endpoint-free template in place when the invoice moved onto PEPPOL rails. The new `ublCheckEndpointIds()` reads `cbc:EndpointID` under each of `AccountingSupplierParty/Party` and `AccountingCustomerParty/Party`, and emits one green `✓ Both supplier and customer Party/EndpointID are declared (EN 16931 BT-34 / BT-49 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 BR-62 / BR-63)` row when both are present, or one red `✗ {party} Party/EndpointID is missing — PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 BR-62 / BR-63 requires …` row per missing party citing the specific rule. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with EndpointID injected on both parties → confirmation; same fixture with EndpointID injected on only the customer → mismatch row citing BR-62 for the supplier). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 104 → 108 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 76):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a strict xs:date format check on the per-PmtInf `ReqdExctnDt` (pain.001 requested execution date) / `ReqdColltnDt` (pain.008 requested collection date) — paired with draft 77 on the UBL side and a direct extension of draft 74's `GrpHdr/CreDtTm` ISODateTime gate from the header to the per-PmtInf settlement-date surface. ISO 20022 declares both as plain xs:date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) in pain.*.03-.08, and pain.001.001.09+ additionally wraps `ReqdExctnDt` in a `<Dt>` child (`<ReqdExctnDt><Dt>YYYY-MM-DD</Dt></ReqdExctnDt>`) — both shapes must hold the xs:date form. The canonical real-world failure modes mirror draft 75's UBL xs:date gate but at the settlement-date layer: US-style `04/15/2024`, slash-separated `2024/04/15`, or a datetime spilled into the date slot (`2024-04-15T00:00:00`) — all schema-valid plain text but rejected at clearing-system entry. The new `sepaCheckReqdDt()` walks every `<PmtInf>`, picks the right field (`ReqdExctnDt` for pain.001, `ReqdColltnDt` for pain.008) by `detected.type`, unwraps the optional `<Dt>` child, and validates against the same `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$` regex from draft 75 plus month/day sanity (01-12 / 01-31). Emits one green `✓ {field} matches the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form across all N PmtInf block(s) …` row across all matching blocks, or one red `✗ {field} mismatch: PmtInf #i declares "X" but ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA Rulebook require the canonical xs:date form …` row per offending block keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index. Together with draft 74 (CreDtTm xs:dateTime, header-level) and draft 75 (UBL xs:date, root + period), this closes out the date-format attack surface across both pain.* and UBL document families. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA / xs:date vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `ReqdExctnDt=2024-01-20` → confirmation citing all PmtInf blocks; same fixture with `01/20/2024` (US-style MM/DD/YYYY) → mismatch row citing the canonical xs:date form). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 104 → 108 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 75):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a strict xs:date format check on the root-level date fields and the `InvoicePeriod` bounds (paired with draft 74's SEPA `CreDtTm` xs:dateTime gate). The existing `ublCheckDates()` (draft 39, BT-9 ≥ BT-2 temporal sanity) and `ublCheckInvoicePeriod()` (draft 47-era, EN 16931 BR-29) compare values via `Date.parse()`, which is lenient and accepts `04/01/2024`, `2024/04/01`, `2024-04-01T08:00:00`, and timestamp-with-time variants — schema-valid in some downstream parsers but rejected by PEPPOL access points at the xs:date schematron gate. The new `ublCheckDateFormats()` strictly validates every date-bearing surface — root `cbc:IssueDate` (BT-2), `cbc:DueDate` (BT-9), `cbc:TaxPointDate` (BT-7), plus the `cac:InvoicePeriod` `cbc:StartDate` / `cbc:EndDate` (BT-73 / BT-74) — against a single `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$` regex with an additional sanity gate on month (01-12) and day (01-31) so `2024-13-01` doesn't slip through. Emits one green `✓ Date fields (IssueDate / DueDate / TaxPointDate / InvoicePeriod bounds) all match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form across all N field(s) (EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ {field} "X" does not match the xs:date YYYY-MM-DD form …` row per offending value naming both the field and the canonical form. Pairs naturally with the existing temporal checks: this gate covers form (xs:date well-formedness), those cover ordering (DueDate ≥ IssueDate, EndDate ≥ StartDate) and presence — together they close out the EN 16931 / PEPPOL date-surface attack on the UBL inspector. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / xs:date vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL fixture with IssueDate=2024-04-01 / DueDate=2024-05-01 → confirmation citing all date fields; same fixture with IssueDate=04/01/2024 (US-style MM/DD/YYYY) injected → mismatch row citing the canonical xs:date form). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 100 → 104 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 74):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a strict ISO 20022 ISODateTime (xs:dateTime) format check on `GrpHdr/CreDtTm` (paired with draft 75 on the UBL side). The existing header rendering surfaces the raw `CreDtTm` value alongside the MsgId and InitgPty but does not validate its shape; ISO 20022 declares the field as ISODateTime, which requires the canonical `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS[.fff][Z|±HH:MM]` form — T separator, hours/minutes/seconds, optional fractional seconds, optional explicit timezone designator. The canonical real-world failure modes are (a) a space separator (`2024-04-01 12:30:45` — common when the value is exported from a spreadsheet column that strips control characters), (b) a date-only value (`2024-04-01` — pasted from a different field), (c) US-style ordering (`04/01/2024 12:30:45`), and (d) bare timestamp text without separators (`20240401T123045`). All four pass downstream string consumers but break ISO 20022 schema validation at clearing-system entry — EBA STEP2, STET, and RT1 all reject the file at the xs:dateTime gate before the rail-level checks even begin. The new `sepaCheckCreDtTm()` reads `GrpHdr/CreDtTm` on pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope), tests it against the `SEPA_DATETIME_RE` regex, and emits one green `✓ GrpHdr/CreDtTm X matches ISO 20022 ISODateTime …` row on match or a red `✗ GrpHdr/CreDtTm "X" is not a valid ISO 20022 ISODateTime …` row otherwise. Header-only — per-PmtInf CreDtTm doesn't exist in pain.001 / pain.008. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / xs:dateTime vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical pain.001 fixture with `CreDtTm=2024-01-15T10:30:00` → confirmation citing the ISO 20022 form; same fixture with `2024-01-15 10:30:00` (space separator) → mismatch row citing the T separator + explicit timezone requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 100 → 104 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 73):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:FinancialInstitutionBranch/cbc:ID` BIC format check (EN 16931 BT-86 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0; paired with draft 72's SEPA `CtgyPurp/Cd` gate). UBL 2.1 declares the supplier's BIC under `cac:PaymentMeans/cac:PayeeFinancialAccount/cac:FinancialInstitutionBranch/cbc:ID` (or the legacy `cac:FinancialInstitution/cbc:ID` shape) as plain Identifier text — any 8 / 11 / random-length string passes the schema, but PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 and ISO 9362 narrow the value to 8 or 11 alphanumeric characters with a fixed shape (4 bank, 2 country, 2 location, optional 3 branch). The canonical real-world failure modes are length errors (9 or 10 chars from a paste that lost or gained a character — `COBADEFF1` instead of `COBADEFFXXX`), case-folded values that hit the bank-code or location prefix, or a BBAN / routing number folded into the BIC slot by a template that mixes the two fields. Parallel in shape to the existing SEPA BIC cross-check (draft 27) and the UBL IBAN MOD-97 cross-check (draft 36): a new `ublCollectBics()` walks every `<cac:FinancialInstitutionBranch>` and `<cac:FinancialInstitution>` under PaymentMeans, dedupes + uppercases the nested `<cbc:ID>` value, and `ublCheckBics()` runs each through the same `bicValidate()` that powers the standalone BIC tool. Emits one green `✓ Payment-service-provider BICs (FinancialInstitutionBranch/ID) are all valid ISO 9362 across all N BIC(s) (EN 16931 BT-86 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ FinancialInstitutionBranch/ID "X" is not a valid ISO 9362 BIC …` row per offending value. Skips silently when no FinancialInstitutionBranch/ID is present — bank-coordinate-free invoices (PaymentMeansCode 48 / 54 / 55, card-payment routes) legitimately omit it. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / ISO 9362 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `COBADEFFXXX` (Commerzbank, ISO 9362-valid) injected → confirmation citing BT-86; same fixture with `COBADEFF1` (9-char, wrong length) → mismatch row citing the ISO 9362 shape). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 96 → 100 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 72):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `PmtTpInf/CtgyPurp/Cd` validation against the ISO 20022 ExternalCategoryPurpose1Code set (paired with draft 73 on the UBL side). The optional `<CtgyPurp>/<Cd>` element under `<PmtInf>/<PmtTpInf>` classifies the high-level purpose of every transaction in the block — salary (`SALA`), supplier payment (`SUPP`), tax (`TAXS`), VAT (`VATX`), pension (`PENS`), and so on across ~34 canonical codes. ISO 20022 declares the field as `ExternalCategoryPurpose1Code` (open enumeration governed by the ISO 20022 ExternalCodeSets list, refreshed quarterly), so a typo against the closest valid code (`SALR` for `SALA`, `SUPPL` for `SUPP`) is schema-valid plain text but breaks downstream automation — clearing systems and regulator-side reporting (wage-statement matching, VAT-payment routing, social-security reconciliation) key off these codes, and a silent typo means a payment marked `SALR` won't show up in the wage-payment register the receiving country's tax authority pulls from. The new `sepaCheckCtgyPurp()` walks every `<PmtInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads each block's `PmtTpInf/CtgyPurp/Cd` (case-folded + whitespace-stripped for paste-safety), and validates against a newly inlined `EXTERNAL_CATEGORY_PURPOSE` set (34 codes, ISO 20022 ExternalCodeSets 2024-Q4). Emits one green `✓ CtgyPurp/Cd is a valid ISO 20022 ExternalCategoryPurpose1Code across all N PmtInf block(s) …` row across all matching blocks, or one red `✗ CtgyPurp/Cd mismatch: PmtInf #i declares X but ISO 20022 ExternalCategoryPurpose1Code allows only the canonical set …` row per offending block keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index. Parallel in shape to `sepaCheckSvcLvl` (draft 44) and `sepaCheckSeqTp` (draft 46): per-PmtInf gate, optional element so absent blocks skip silently. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `CtgyPurp/Cd=SALA` → confirmation citing the canonical set; same fixture with `CtgyPurp/Cd=SALR` → mismatch row citing the typo and the canonical codes). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 96 → 100 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 71):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:Item/cac:OriginCountry/cbc:IdentificationCode` ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 check (paired with draft 70 on the SEPA side; both reuse the inlined 249-entry `ISO_3166_1_ALPHA2` set first shipped with draft 68). EN 16931 BT-159 (Item country of origin) names this element for cross-border supplies, customs clearance, and Intrastat statistical reporting; PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 access points reject any value that is not a current ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code at the schematron gate. UBL 2.1 declares `cbc:IdentificationCode` as plain text with no enumeration, so the same failure modes as draft 68's party-level check apply at the line/item level — `UK` (not an ISO code — Great Britain is `GB`), `EN`, `XX`, or trailing-whitespace artefacts pass the schema but break PEPPOL routing. Distinct from draft 68 in scope: that gate covers the supplier and customer postal-address country codes (BT-40 / BT-55); this gate covers per-line item country of origin (BT-159), which is the field a buyer's customs broker reads when an invoice crosses a border. The new `ublCheckOriginCountry()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` and `<cac:CreditNoteLine>`, drills into `cac:Item/cac:OriginCountry/cbc:IdentificationCode`, trims + uppercases, and validates against the shared set. Emits one green `✓ Item country-of-origin codes (Item/OriginCountry/IdentificationCode) are all valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 across all N line(s) (EN 16931 BT-159 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ Item/OriginCountry/IdentificationCode "X" is not a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code …` row per offending code. Skips silently when no item declares `cac:OriginCountry` — BT-159 is optional per PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 and domestic-only supplies legitimately omit it. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `Item/OriginCountry/IdentificationCode=DE` injected → confirmation citing BT-159; same fixture with `UK` injected → mismatch row citing `GB` as the canonical alternative). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 92 → 96 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 70):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `Dbtr/Cdtr CtryOfRes` ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 check (parallel to the UBL Item OriginCountry/IdentificationCode check shipped alongside as draft 71; both reuse the inlined 249-entry `ISO_3166_1_ALPHA2` set first shipped with draft 68 + draft 69). ISO 20022 declares the optional `<CtryOfRes>` element (PartyIdentification245.CtryOfRes) under Dbtr / Cdtr as type CountryCode with the pattern `[A-Z]{2}`, so any two uppercase letters pass the schema; the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks narrow the value to a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code. Same canonical failure as draft 69's `PstlAdr/Ctry` gate (`UK` for `GB`), but at a different ISO 20022 anchor — `CtryOfRes` is the party's country of residence rather than the country of the postal address, so a natural-person debtor resident in Germany but using a UK correspondence address has two distinct country-code surfaces the EPC rulebooks pin. The new `sepaCheckCtryOfRes()` walks every `<CtryOfRes>` reachable in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope), trims + uppercases, and validates against the shared set. Emits one green `✓ Country of residence codes (Dbtr/Cdtr CtryOfRes) are all valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 across all N code(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ CtryOfRes "X" is not a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code …` row per offending value. Together with draft 69's `PstlAdr/Ctry` gate, this covers the two distinct ISO 20022 country-code surfaces an originator declares in a pain.001 / pain.008 file. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `Dbtr/CtryOfRes=DE` → confirmation; same fixture with `UK` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and `GB` as the canonical alternative). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 92 → 96 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 69):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `PstlAdr/Ctry` ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 check (parallel to the UBL Country/IdentificationCode check shipped alongside as draft 68). ISO 20022 declares `Ctry` as type CountryCode with the pattern `[A-Z]{2}`, so any two uppercase letters pass the schema; the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks narrow the value to a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code. The canonical real-world failure is `UK` (not an ISO code — Great Britain is `GB`) folded into a Dbtr or Cdtr postal address from a template repurposed across SEPA and non-SEPA flows; rail-level processors (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) reject the value at the country-code gate so the inspector should surface it before the file leaves the originator. The new `sepaCheckCtry()` walks every `<Ctry>` reachable in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope), trims + uppercases, and validates against a newly inlined `ISO_3166_1_ALPHA2` set (249 codes, 2025 list — shared with draft 68's UBL counterpart so the data is single-sourced). Emits one green `✓ Country codes (PstlAdr/Ctry) are all valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 across all N code(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ PstlAdr/Ctry "X" is not a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code …` row per offending value. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `Dbtr/PstlAdr/Ctry=DE` → confirmation; same fixture with `UK` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and `GB` as the canonical alternative). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 88 → 92 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 68):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `cac:PostalAddress/cac:Country/cbc:IdentificationCode` ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 check (paired with draft 69 on the SEPA side; both share an inlined 249-entry `ISO_3166_1_ALPHA2` set). EN 16931 BT-40 (supplier country) / BT-55 (customer country) and PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 BR-08 / BR-55 require each party's postal address to carry a country code that is a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code; UBL 2.1 declares `cbc:IdentificationCode` as plain text with no enumeration, so `UK` (not an ISO code — Great Britain is `GB`), `EN`, `XX`, or trailing-whitespace artefacts pass the schema but are rejected by PEPPOL access points at the schematron gate. The new `ublCheckCountryCode()` walks `AccountingSupplierParty` and `AccountingCustomerParty`, drills into `Party/PostalAddress/Country/IdentificationCode`, trims + uppercases, and validates against the shared set. Emits one green `✓ Party country codes (PostalAddress/Country/IdentificationCode) are all valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 across all N party address(es) (EN 16931 BT-40 / BT-55 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ Country/IdentificationCode "X" is not a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code …` row per offending code. Skips silently when neither party declares a `PostalAddress/Country/IdentificationCode` — UBL 2.1 marks the wrapping elements optional, and PEPPOL BR-08 / BR-55 absence is caught by upstream schema validation. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with supplier `Country/IdentificationCode=DE` injected → confirmation citing BT-40 / BT-55; same fixture with `UK` injected → mismatch row citing `GB` as the canonical alternative). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 88 → 92 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 67):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check (drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 / 61 / 63 / 65) from identifiers, Ustrd, party Nm, AdrLine, and TwnNm to the postal-code field (`PstlAdr/PstCd`) — the last of the three free-text postal-address surfaces the EPC rulebooks pin to the basic SEPA character set. ISO 20022 declares PstCd as Max16Text so any Unicode codepoints up to 16 chars pass the schema, but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both require the basic SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space) to hold across every free-text field, postal codes included. Most country postal codes are ASCII digit / letter+digit combinations (UK: `SW1A 1AA`, NL: `1234 AB`, CA: `K1A 0B1`, DE: `10115`) that pass trivially, but rare cases carry non-ASCII characters via paste — typically when a postcode is folded into the address line of a template that mixes form fields, or when a downstream system substitutes a `§` for a non-printable separator. The new `sepaCheckPstCdCharset()` walks every `<PstCd>` reachable anywhere in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope), trims, tests against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49, and emits one green `✓ Postal codes (PstlAdr/PstCd) use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N code(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ PstlAdr/PstCd "X" contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value. With drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 / 61 / 63 / 65 / 67 the EPC character-set check now covers MsgId, EndToEndId + InstrId, MndtId, PmtInfId, Ustrd, party Nm, AdrLine, TwnNm, and PstCd — nine free-text surfaces in pain.001 / pain.008, closing out every free-text field the EPC rulebook pins to the basic SEPA character set. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `Dbtr/PstlAdr/PstCd=10115` → confirmation; same fixture with `10115§` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 84 → 88 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 66):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `TaxCurrencyCode` (BT-6) check against the ISO 4217 active-currencies set — the optional element at the root of the UBL document that names the VAT-accounting currency when an invoice declares VAT in a different currency than its document currency (e.g. a SEK invoice declaring EUR-denominated VAT for a cross-border supply where VAT must be reported in the destination-state currency). UBL 2.1 declares `cbc:TaxCurrencyCode` as an open ISO-4217 enumeration so any text passes the schema, including typos (`ERU` for `EUR`) and historical codes (`ITL` Italian lira, `DEM` German mark, `FRF` French franc) — PEPPOL access points reject anything outside the current ISO 4217 active set at the schematron gate. Parallel to draft 31's `@currencyID` check but scoped to the root element only; surfaces a small but distinct gate because BT-6 is the field that governs the per-rate TaxAmountCurrency split when present, and an invalid code silently breaks the multi-currency reconciliation that BT-6 is supposed to enable. Distinct from the DocumentCurrencyCode consistency check (draft 64, which validates that every `@currencyID` matches BT-5): that gate covers intra-document agreement; this gate covers the BT-6 element's own ISO 4217 validity. The new `ublCheckTaxCurrencyCode()` reads `cbc:TaxCurrencyCode` at the root (trimmed, uppercased), looks it up in the existing inline `ISO_4217_ACTIVE` set (≈ 180 codes, same source as drafts 29 / 31), and emits one green `✓ TaxCurrencyCode X is a current ISO 4217 active code (EN 16931 BT-6 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row on a hit, or one red `✗ TaxCurrencyCode X is not active per ISO 4217 — EN 16931 BT-6 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 requires a current ISO 4217 code …` row on a miss. Skips silently when the element is absent — BT-6 is optional per PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 and most single-currency invoices don't declare it. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL fixture with `TaxCurrencyCode=EUR` injected at the root → confirmation citing BT-6; same fixture with `TaxCurrencyCode=ITL` (historical Italian lira) → mismatch row citing the schematron gate). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 84 → 88 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 65):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check (drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 / 61 / 63) from identifiers, Ustrd, party Nm, and AdrLine to town-name fields (`PstlAdr/TwnNm`). ISO 20022 declares TwnNm as Max35Text so any Unicode codepoints up to 35 chars pass the schema, but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both pin the basic SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space) across every free-text field, town names included. Common European town names with accented characters (`München`, `Köln`, `Düsseldorf`, `Genève`, `Zürich`, `Linköping`) are schema-valid but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) either strip the accented characters (so the counterparty bank shows a mangled town on the rail statement) or hard-reject at the rail-level character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckTwnNmCharset()` walks every `<TwnNm>` reachable anywhere in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope), trims, tests against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49, and emits one green `✓ Town names (PstlAdr/TwnNm) use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N town(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ PstlAdr/TwnNm "X" contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value. Naturally pairs with the AdrLine check from draft 63 to cover both common postal-address free-text surfaces.
