With drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 / 61 / 63 / 65 the EPC character-set check now covers MsgId, EndToEndId + InstrId, MndtId, PmtInfId, Ustrd, party Nm, AdrLine, and TwnNm — eight free-text surfaces in pain.001 / pain.008 that the EPC rulebook pins to the basic SEPA character set. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `Dbtr/PstlAdr/TwnNm=Berlin` → confirmation; same fixture with `München` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 80 → 84 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 64):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `DocumentCurrencyCode` (BT-5) consistency check against every `@currencyID` attribute in the document (EN 16931 BR-CO-04). UBL 2.1 declares `@currencyID` on every `*Amount` element as an open ISO-4217 enumeration, so an invoice can technically carry mixed currencies (e.g. line amounts in EUR and the tax breakdown in USD) and still pass the schema — but PEPPOL access points reject the document at the BR-CO-04 schematron gate. The canonical real-world cause is a template repurposed across multi-currency flows where the bulk replace missed one `currencyID`, or a copy-paste of a tax-breakdown block from a different invoice. Distinct from the ISO 4217 validity check (draft 31): that catches typos (`ERU` for `EUR`) and historical codes (`ITL`, `DEM`); this catches intra-document inconsistency (every code is a valid current ISO 4217 code but they don't all match BT-5). The new `ublCheckCurrencyConsistency()` reads the root `<cbc:DocumentCurrencyCode>`, walks every `@currencyID` attribute reachable in the DOM (the same collector used by `ublCheckCurrencies()`), and emits one green `✓ All monetary @currencyID attributes match the document currency X (EN 16931 BR-CO-04 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row on full agreement, or one red `✗ Monetary @currencyID X does not match the document currency Y — EN 16931 BR-CO-04 …` row per offending code. Skips silently when BT-5 itself is absent — the existing header rendering already surfaces that, and a real access point's schema-validation step catches the BR-04 absence upstream. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (canonical UBL fixture declaring EUR throughout → confirmation citing BR-CO-04; same fixture with the line-extension `@currencyID` flipped to USD → mismatch row citing both codes and BR-CO-04). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 80 → 84 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 63):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check (drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 / 61) from identifiers, the Ustrd remittance field, and party-name (Nm) fields to postal-address lines (`PstlAdr/AdrLine`) — the third-most-common entry point for non-Latin characters into a SEPA file after Ustrd (draft 59) and party Nm (draft 61). ISO 20022 declares `AdrLine` as `Max70Text` so any Unicode codepoints up to 70 chars pass the schema, but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both pin the basic SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space) across every free-text field, addresses included. A street name like `Königsallee 12` or `Côte d'Azur` folded into a `Dbtr/PstlAdr/AdrLine` or `Cdtr/PstlAdr/AdrLine` is schema-valid but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) either strip the accented characters (so the counterparty bank shows a mangled address on the rail statement) or hard-reject at the rail-level character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckAdrLineCharset()` walks every `<AdrLine>` reachable anywhere in the pain.001 / pain.008 wrapper (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope), trims, tests against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49, and emits one green `✓ Postal address lines (PstlAdr/AdrLine) use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N line(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ PstlAdr/AdrLine "X" contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value (the offender quoted inline). With drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 / 61 / 63 the EPC character-set check now covers MsgId (document-level), EndToEndId + InstrId (transaction-level identifiers), MndtId (pain.008 mandate reference), PmtInfId (per-block payment-information identifier), Ustrd (per-transaction unstructured remittance), party Nm (file / block / transaction level), and AdrLine (postal-address lines) — every free-text surface in pain.001 / pain.008 that the EPC rulebook pins to the basic SEPA character set. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `Dbtr/PstlAdr/AdrLine = "123 Main Street"` + `"Berlin 10115"` → confirmation citing the 2-line count; same fixture with `Königsallee 12` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 76 → 80 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 62):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `PaymentMeansCode` UNCL 4461 subset check, narrowing the existing UBL 2.1 schema (which accepts any UNCL 4461 code as plain text) to the EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 allowed subset: `1` (Instrument not defined), `10` (In cash), `20` (Cheque), `30` (Credit transfer), `42` (Payment to bank account), `48` (Bank card), `49` (Direct debit), `57` (Standing agreement), `58` (SEPA credit transfer), `59` (SEPA direct debit), `97` (Clearing between partners). PEPPOL access points reject anything outside this subset at the schematron gate; the canonical real-world failure mode is a template repurposed across payment instruments (`70` "Bankers draft", or `2` "Automated clearing house credit" pasted into a SEPA invoice) where the arithmetic still balances against itself, the code is plain text the schema doesn't constrain, but the document is rail-broken at the access-point level. The new `ublCheckPaymentMeansCode()` walks every `<cac:PaymentMeans>` reachable from the root, reads each `<cbc:PaymentMeansCode>` (trimmed), dedupes, and emits a single ✓ row across all matching codes via `intlList()` (locale joiner applies — `30 und 58` in de, `30 et 58` in fr) or one ✗ row per offending code citing the full allowed set. Skips silently when no PaymentMeans block is present — the field is technically mandatory under BR-49 but a real access point's schema-validation step catches absence upstream; this check is about value correctness, not presence. Distinct from the UNCL 1001 document-type-code check (draft 47, validates per-document type code) and the UNCL 5305 VAT category-code check (draft 41, validates per-line category letter): those validate other UNCL code lists; this validates the payment-instrument code list. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / UNCL 4461 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `<cbc:PaymentMeansCode>30</cbc:PaymentMeansCode>` injected → confirmation row citing UNCL 4461; same fixture with `70` → mismatch row citing the EN 16931 / PEPPOL allowed set). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 76 → 80 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 61):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check (drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59) from identifiers and the Ustrd remittance field to party-name fields — the second-most-common entry point for non-Latin characters into a SEPA file. Walks every `Nm` reachable from these anchors in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope): `GrpHdr/InitgPty/Nm` (file-level initiating party), per-PmtInf `Dbtr/Nm` (debtor on credit transfers) and `Cdtr/Nm` (creditor on direct debits), plus per-transaction `Cdtr/Nm` (beneficiary on credit transfers) and `Dbtr/Nm` (payer on direct debits). ISO 20022 declares `Nm` as `Max70Text` — any Unicode codepoints up to 70 chars pass the schema — but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebooks both pin the basic SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space) across every free-text field, names included. A `Müller`, `Görtz`, or `Bénéteau` folded into a `Dbtr/Nm` or `Cdtr/Nm` is schema-valid but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) either strip the accented characters (so the beneficiary's bank shows a mangled name on the rail statement, making the payment hard to identify) or hard-reject at the rail-level character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckPartyNmCharset()` collects every `Nm` from the five anchor positions, tests each against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49, and emits one green `✓ Party name (InitgPty/Cdtr/Dbtr Nm) values use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N name(s) …` row when every name is clean, or one red `✗ Party name "X" contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value (the offender quoted inline so the maintainer can search the file for the source record). Anchors that omit `Nm` skip silently — the existing renderers already surface absence, and the inspector doesn't add noise for partially-populated blocks. With drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 / 61 the EPC character-set check now covers MsgId (document-level), EndToEndId + InstrId (transaction-level identifiers), MndtId (pain.008 mandate reference), PmtInfId (per-block payment-information identifier), Ustrd (per-transaction unstructured remittance), and InitgPty + Dbtr + Cdtr Nm (party names at file, block, and transaction levels) — every free-text field reachable in pain.001 / pain.008 that the EPC rulebook pins to the basic SEPA character set. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `InitgPty/Nm=ACME Corp` plus ASCII debtor/creditor names → confirmation row; same fixture with the initiating-party name rewritten to `Müller GmbH` → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 72 → 76 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 60):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains an `InvoicePeriod` start ≤ end sanity check, the fourth file-wide accounting/structural invariant on the UBL side alongside the line-sum + tax-inclusive invariants from draft 28, the PayableAmount invariant from draft 45, and the line-ID uniqueness gate from draft 48. EN 16931 BR-29 / BR-CO-19 require that when `<cac:InvoicePeriod>` is present at the document level (BT-73 StartDate, BT-74 EndDate — the billing period the invoice covers, used for subscriptions, utilities, telecoms, and any recurring supply), the StartDate must be on or before the EndDate. UBL 2.1 declares both as plain `xs:date`, so an inverted period passes the schema but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BR-29 schematron gate; the typical real-world cause is a template repurposed across billing cycles where the date swap was missed (so a March invoice carries Feb's End and Mar's Start, or vice-versa). The new `ublCheckInvoicePeriod()` walks the root for `cac:InvoicePeriod`, reads `cbc:StartDate` and `cbc:EndDate` (trimmed, `Date.parse`-resolved so half-completed inputs degrade silently rather than firing false negatives), and emits one green `✓ InvoicePeriod EndDate Y is on or after StartDate X (EN 16931 BR-29 / BR-CO-19)` row when the order is correct, or one red `✗ InvoicePeriod EndDate Y precedes StartDate X — EN 16931 BR-29 / BR-CO-19 requires StartDate ≤ EndDate, PEPPOL access points reject at the schematron gate` row otherwise. Skips silently when the element is absent or either bound is missing/unparseable — those degenerate cases are caught by the schema-validation step a real implementation would run upstream, and `InvoicePeriod` itself is optional per PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0. Distinct from the IssueDate / DueDate check (the existing `ublCheckDates()`): that check covers the invoice's own issuance and payment timeline (BT-2 / BT-9); this check covers the supply period the invoice represents (BT-73 / BT-74). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed UBL fixture with `2024-03-01 → 2024-03-31` injected → confirmation row citing BR-29 / BR-CO-19; same fixture with the dates swapped → mismatch row citing the precedence violation). Audit OK. Full SEPA+UBL suite: 72 → 76 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 59):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check (drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56) from free-text identifiers to the per-transaction `<RmtInf>/<Ustrd>` unstructured remittance information field — the highest-frequency real-world rail-rejection cause in the SEPA flow. ISO 20022 declares `Ustrd` as `Max140Text`, so any Unicode string up to 140 codepoints passes the schema; the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook both require the SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space) to hold across every free-text field, and `Ustrd` is the field most likely to inherit user-facing or invoice-derived text (German umlauts, French accents, em-dashes, smart quotes) the originator's accounting system never sanitised. A `Ustrd` carrying characters outside the allowed set is schema-valid but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) either strip the offending characters before forwarding (which destroys the remittance reference the beneficiary uses to reconcile the payment against their AR ledger) or hard-reject the file at the rail-level character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckUstrdCharset()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope for this gate), reads every `<Ustrd>` child under `<RmtInf>` (whitespace-trimmed; multiple `<Ustrd>` elements per `<RmtInf>` are allowed by the ISO 20022 schema and all are checked), tests each against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49, and emits one green `✓ RmtInf/Ustrd values use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N unstructured remittance entry(ies) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ RmtInf/Ustrd "X" contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value (the offender quoted inline so the maintainer can search the file for the source record). Transactions without an `<Ustrd>` skip silently — `<RmtInf>` is optional in ISO 20022 (some flows rely entirely on `<EndToEndId>` for reconciliation) and the inspector doesn't add noise for the degenerate case. With drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 / 59 the EPC character-set check now covers MsgId (document-level), EndToEndId + InstrId (transaction-level identifiers in both pain.001 and pain.008), MndtId (pain.008-specific mandate reference), PmtInfId (per-block payment-information identifier), and Ustrd (per-transaction unstructured remittance information) — the full free-text surface in pain.001 / pain.008. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `<Ustrd>Invoice 2024-001</Ustrd>` → confirmation citing the 1-entry count; same fixture with `Rechnung Müller 2024` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 306 → 308 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 58):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a Buyer VAT identifier check, the symmetric counterpart to the Seller VAT identifier check shipped in draft 57. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 invoices declare the buyer's VAT number via `AccountingCustomerParty/Party/PartyTaxScheme/CompanyID`, paired with `TaxScheme/cbc:ID="VAT"`. EN 16931 BT-48 names this as the Buyer VAT identifier and BR-CO-09 makes it mandatory for intra-EU reverse-charge supplies (the AE category from draft 41); the value MUST start with a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country code prefix followed by the member-state VAT format. UBL 2.1 declares `cbc:CompanyID` as plain identifier text so a value without the country prefix, or with the wrong remainder shape, passes the schema but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BT-48 schematron gate — the typical real-world cause is a template carrying the buyer's national tax registration number (e.g. a French SIREN or a German Steuernummer) where the file generator should have substituted the EU VAT identifier instead. The new `ublCheckBuyerVat()` walks `AccountingCustomerParty/Party/PartyTaxScheme`, picks the block whose nested `TaxScheme/cbc:ID = VAT`, reads `cbc:CompanyID` (whitespace-trimmed), and routes the value through the same `vatValidate()` the standalone EU VAT tool (§2.3) ships — same per-country format regex + checksum logic, no duplication, locale-correct error path. Emits one green `✓ Buyer VAT identifier X valid per ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 + EU VAT format (EN 16931 BT-48 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row when `vatValidate()` accepts the value (format pass or checksum pass), or one red `✗ Buyer VAT identifier X invalid — PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 BT-48 requires a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country prefix + EU VAT format` row otherwise. Skips silently when no PartyTaxScheme with TaxScheme/ID=VAT is present — the existing customer header already surfaces what's declared, and BR-CO-09 absence (when applicable to the document's tax categories) would be caught by a real access point's schema-validation step upstream. Distinct from the Seller VAT identifier check (draft 57): same shape, mirrored anchor element (AccountingCustomerParty vs AccountingSupplierParty), different BT field (BT-48 vs BT-31). The two checks together cover both party-level VAT identifiers on the UBL side. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `FR40303265045` (the canonical FR example, passes the `(12 + 3*(SIREN%97))%97` checksum) injected into the customer's PartyTaxScheme → confirmation row citing ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 + EU VAT format; same fixture with `987654321` (no country prefix) → mismatch row citing PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / BT-48). Audit OK. Full suite: 304 → 306 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 57):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a Seller VAT identifier check. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 invoices declare the supplier VAT number via `AccountingSupplierParty/Party/PartyTaxScheme/CompanyID`, paired with `TaxScheme/cbc:ID="VAT"`. EN 16931 BT-31 names this as the Seller VAT identifier and BR-CO-09 makes it mandatory for any invoice carrying standard-rated lines; the value MUST start with a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country code prefix followed by the member-state VAT format (e.g. `DE123456789`, `FR12345678901`). UBL 2.1 declares `cbc:CompanyID` as plain identifier text so a value without the country prefix, or with the wrong remainder shape, passes the schema but is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the BT-31 schematron gate — the typical real-world cause is a non-EU UBL template repurposed for PEPPOL where the supplier ID was carried unchanged from a flow that didn't enforce the EU format. The new `ublCheckSellerVat()` walks `AccountingSupplierParty/Party/PartyTaxScheme`, picks the block whose nested `TaxScheme/cbc:ID = VAT`, reads `cbc:CompanyID` (whitespace-trimmed), and routes the value through the same `vatValidate()` the standalone EU VAT tool (§2.3) ships — same per-country format regex + checksum logic (ISO 7064 MOD 11,10 for DE, weighted MOD 11 for the Scandinavian formats, MOD 97 for BE, etc.), no duplication, locale-correct error path. Emits one green `✓ Seller VAT identifier X valid per ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 + EU VAT format (EN 16931 BT-31 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0)` row when `vatValidate()` accepts the value (format pass or checksum pass), or one red `✗ Seller VAT identifier X invalid — PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 BT-31 requires a valid ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country prefix + EU VAT format` row otherwise. Skips silently when no PartyTaxScheme with TaxScheme/ID=VAT is present — the existing supplier header already surfaces what's declared, and a real access point's schema-validation step would catch BR-CO-09 absence upstream. Distinct from the IBAN cross-check (draft 36, which validates Payee/Payer account numbers) and the TaxScheme/ID=VAT enforcement (draft 54, which validates the scheme designator on tax categories): those gates cover the financial-account identifier and the per-category tax scheme; this gate covers the party-level seller identifier. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / PEPPOL vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with `DE136695976` injected into PartyTaxScheme → confirmation row citing ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 + EU VAT format; same fixture with `123456789` (no country prefix) → mismatch row citing PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / BT-31). Audit OK. Full suite: 304 → 306 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 56):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check from drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 to the per-PmtInf `<PmtInfId>` identifier — the fifth and final free-text identifier the EPC rulebooks pin to the SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space). ISO 20022 declares `PmtInfId` as `Max35Text`, so any Unicode string up to 35 codepoints passes the schema; the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook both require the SEPA character set to hold across every free-text identifier in the file. The canonical real-world failure mode is a batch generator interpolating a company name or local reference (`Übermäßig GmbH`, `Müller & Cie`) into the block identifier — schema-valid `Max35Text`, rail-broken at the EBA STEP2 / STET / RT1 character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckPmtInfIdCharset()` walks every `<PmtInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope for this gate), reads each block's `<PmtInfId>` (whitespace-trimmed), tests against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49 (`^[A-Za-z0-9/\-?:().,'+ ]+$`), and emits one green `✓ PmtInfId values use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N PmtInf block(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ PmtInfId X contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value (keyed by the bad identifier so the maintainer can locate the source block). Blocks without a `PmtInfId` skip silently — the existing PmtInf header rendering surfaces what's declared, and the inspector doesn't add noise for the degenerate case. With drafts 49 / 51 / 53 / 55 / 56 the SEPA character-set check covers MsgId (document-level), EndToEndId + InstrId (transaction-level credit-transfer + direct-debit), MndtId (pain.008 mandate reference), and PmtInfId (per-block payment-information identifier) — the full free-text identifier surface in pain.001 / pain.008. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `PmtInfId=PAY-001` → confirmation citing the 1-block count; same fixture with `PAY-Übermäßig` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 304 → 306 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 55):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC character-set check (drafts 49 / 51 / 53) to the pain.008 per-transaction mandate identifier. The EPC SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook requires the SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space) to hold across every free-text identifier in pain.008 — and `<DrctDbtTx>/<MndtRltdInf>/<MndtId>` is the mandate reference the creditor and debtor agreed on at mandate-signing time, used as the per-transaction reconciliation key on the SDD side. ISO 20022 declares MndtId as Max35Text so accented characters (the canonical real-world cause: a debtor's name like `Müller` folded into the mandate reference by the originator's CRM) pass the schema; clearing systems either strip the offending characters before forwarding (destroying the mandate reference) or hard-reject the file at the character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckMndtIdCharset()` walks every `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.008 wrappers (pain.001 skips silently — credit transfers don't carry mandates), reads each transaction's `<DrctDbtTx>/<MndtRltdInf>/<MndtId>` (whitespace-trimmed), tests it against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49, and emits one green `✓ MndtId values use only EPC SDD Rulebook allowed characters across all N direct-debit transaction(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ MndtId X contains characters outside the EPC SDD Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value (keyed by the bad identifier so the maintainer can locate the source record). Transactions without an MndtId skip silently — MndtId is only declared on direct debits, and the per-transaction renderer from draft 22 already surfaces what's declared. With this draft the SEPA character-set checks cover MsgId (document-level), EndToEndId + InstrId (transaction-level identifiers in both pain.001 and pain.008), and MndtId (pain.008-specific mandate reference). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SDD / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.008 with `MndtId=MNDT-2024-001` → confirmation citing the 1-direct-debit count; same fixture with `MNDT-Müller-001` → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SDD character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 302 → 304 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 54):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `TaxScheme/ID = VAT` enforcement check. UBL 2.1 allows `cac:TaxScheme/cbc:ID` to carry any UNCL 5153 tax-scheme code — GST (Australia), HST (Canada), VAT (EU), and dozens more — but PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 narrows the value to "VAT" only across every `TaxCategory` reachable from the root: header-side `TaxSubtotal/TaxCategory/TaxScheme` and item-level `ClassifiedTaxCategory/TaxScheme` alike. The canonical real-world failure mode is a template repurposed from a non-EU UBL flow — a New Zealand or Singapore UBL invoice template ships with `TaxScheme/ID=GST` and an engineer ports the layout to PEPPOL without updating the scheme code; the schema passes (UBL 2.1 doesn't constrain the code) but PEPPOL access points reject the document at the schematron gate. The new `ublCheckTaxScheme()` walks every `<cac:TaxScheme>` reachable from the root, reads each `<cbc:ID>` (uppercased and deduped), and emits one green `✓ TaxScheme/ID = VAT across every TaxCategory (PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / UNCL 5153)` row when every code matches, or one red `✗ TaxScheme/ID X is not VAT — PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 narrows the UNCL 5153 tax scheme code to VAT only` row per offending code. TaxCategories without a TaxScheme child skip silently — the schema allows the absence and the existing per-rate breakdown surfaces what's declared. Distinct from the VAT category-letter check (draft 41, which validates the `TaxCategory/ID` letter against the EN 16931 BR-S/Z/E/AE/K/G/O/L/M set) and from the VAT-rate / totals invariants (drafts 28 / 37, which validate arithmetic against numeric fields): those checks operate inside the tax breakdown; this check validates the tax-scheme designator itself, which selects which rule family applies to all the values inside. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / UNCL 5153 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (UBL fixture with TaxScheme/ID=VAT → confirmation row citing PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / UNCL 5153; same fixture with TaxScheme/ID=GST → mismatch row citing the PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 restriction). Audit OK. Full suite: 300 → 302 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 53):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check from drafts 49 / 51 to the per-transaction `<PmtId>/<InstrId>` originator-side instruction identifier — the third character-set gate, completing the trio of free-text identifiers the EPC rulebooks pin to the SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space). The EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and Direct Debit Rulebooks describe `InstrId` as a free-text key set by the initiating party for its own bookkeeping; in practice it is commonly populated from internal sequence keys or batch references that may carry punctuation or whitespace outside the allowed set. A bad `InstrId` is `Max35Text`-valid but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) either strip the offending characters before forwarding (destroying the originator-side instruction key) or hard-reject the file at the character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckInstrIdCharset()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads each transaction's `<PmtId>/<InstrId>` (whitespace-trimmed), tests it against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49 (`^[A-Za-z0-9/\-?:().,'+ ]+$`), and emits one green `✓ InstrId values use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N transaction(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ InstrId X contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value (keyed by the bad identifier so the maintainer can locate the source record). Transactions without an `InstrId` skip silently — `InstrId` is optional per the ISO 20022 schema (only `EndToEndId` is mandatory inside `PmtId`), and the existing per-transaction renderer surfaces what's declared. With this draft the three character-set gates collectively cover MsgId (document-level), EndToEndId (transaction-level reconciliation key), and InstrId (transaction-level originator-side key). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `<InstrId>INSTR-001</InstrId>` injected → confirmation citing the 1-transaction count; same fixture with `INSTR-Übergabe` → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 300 → 302 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 52):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `ProfileID` process-conformance check, the second profile-level gate on the UBL side after the `CustomizationID` gate from draft 50. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 defines a single process URN — `urn:fdc:peppol.eu:2017:poacc:billing:01:1.0` ("Billing 01: Invoicing") — and conforming invoices and credit notes declare it at the document root via `<cbc:ProfileID>`. A schema-valid UBL 2.1 document carrying any other process URN is rejected by PEPPOL access points at the routing layer, because `ProfileID` selects the SML (Service Metadata Locator) / SMP (Service Metadata Publisher) service-metadata record the receiver advertises; a mismatch sends the document to a routing dead-end. Parallel in shape to draft 50: `CustomizationID` names the document profile (which schema-rule set the document claims compliance with), `ProfileID` names the transaction process (which business interaction the document participates in). Both gates must pass for a PEPPOL access point to forward the document. The new `ublCheckProfileId()` reads the root `<cbc:ProfileID>` (trimmed), compares it to the canonical PEPPOL string stored in an inline `UBL_PEPPOL_PROFILE_ID` constant, and emits one green `✓ ProfileID declares the PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 process (…)` row on exact match or one red `✗ ProfileID X does not match the PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 process (Y) — PEPPOL access points route documents by ProfileID, mismatches dead-end at the SML/SMP lookup` row otherwise, citing both the declared and expected URNs inline so the maintainer doesn't have to look up the PEPPOL spec. Missing element skips silently — the existing `ProfileID` rendering already surfaces absence, and a PEPPOL access point's schema-validation step would catch it upstream. Distinct from `CustomizationID` (which validates schema-rule conformance) and from the per-document UNCL 1001 code check (draft 47): those validate codes carried inside the document; this check validates the process URN the document declares participation in. With drafts 50 + 52 in place, both PEPPOL profile-level gates are wired. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed UBL fixture with the canonical PEPPOL process URN → confirmation row; same fixture with the URN rewritten to `urn:fdc:peppol.eu:2017:poacc:billing:99:9.9` → mismatch row citing both the actual and expected values plus the SML/SMP routing failure mode). Audit OK. Full suite: 298 → 300 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 51):** §2.4 SEPA inspector extends the EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check from draft 49 to the per-transaction `EndToEndId` reconciliation key — the second character-set gate, complementing the document-level `MsgId` gate. The EPC SEPA Credit Transfer and Direct Debit Rulebooks both require the SEPA character set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space) to hold across every free-text identifier in the file; `MsgId` is only one entry point, and transaction-level `EndToEndId` values are commonly populated from customer references, invoice numbers, or CRM identifiers that may carry accented characters the originator never sanitised. A `EndToEndId` carrying characters outside the allowed set is `Max35Text`-valid but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) either strip the bad characters before forwarding (destroying the reconciliation reference) or hard-reject the file at the character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckE2eIdCharset()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` (credit transfers) and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` (direct debits) in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads each transaction's `<PmtId>/<EndToEndId>` (whitespace-trimmed), tests it against the same `SEPA_CHARSET_RE` regex from draft 49 (`^[A-Za-z0-9/\-?:().,'+ ]+$`), and emits one green `✓ EndToEndId values use only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters across all N transaction(s) …` row when every value is clean, or one red `✗ EndToEndId X contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row per offending value (keyed by the bad identifier so the maintainer can locate the source record). Transactions without an EndToEndId skip silently — the existing per-transaction renderer already surfaces the absence. Distinct from the uniqueness gate from draft 43: uniqueness covers content (no duplicates), this check covers form (only allowed characters). The two gates together cover the EPC SEPA Rulebook's invariants for the transaction-level reconciliation identifier. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `EndToEndId=E2E-001` → confirmation citing the 1-transaction count; same fixture with `E2E-Müller` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 298 → 300 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 50):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `CustomizationID` profile-conformance check. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 invoices and credit notes MUST declare `<cbc:CustomizationID>urn:cen.eu:en16931:2017#compliant#urn:fdc:peppol.eu:2017:poacc:billing:3.0</cbc:CustomizationID>` at the document root: the CEN prefix asserts EN 16931 compliance and the PEPPOL suffix narrows the document to the BIS Billing 3.0 subset. A schema-valid UBL 2.1 invoice that declares any other URN (or a stripped EN 16931 URN without the PEPPOL suffix) is still UBL but rejected by PEPPOL access points at the gateway profile check; the typical real-world cause is a Factur-X / ZUGFeRD or generic UBL template repurposed for PEPPOL without updating the URN. The new `ublCheckCustomizationId()` reads the root `<cbc:CustomizationID>` (trimmed), compares it to the canonical PEPPOL string stored in an inline `UBL_PEPPOL_CUSTOMIZATION_ID` constant, and emits one green `✓ CustomizationID declares PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 conformance (…)` row on exact match or a red `✗ CustomizationID X does not match PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 (Y) — PEPPOL access points reject documents at the profile gate` row otherwise, citing both the declared and expected URNs inline so the maintainer doesn't have to look up the PEPPOL spec. Missing element skips silently — the existing `CustomizationID` rendering already surfaces absence, and a PEPPOL access point's schema-validation step would catch it upstream. Distinct from the UNCL 1001 document-type-code check (draft 47, which validates per-document code values against the PEPPOL allowed set) and the UNCL 5305 VAT category-code check (draft 41, which validates per-line category letters): those validate codes carried inside the document; this check validates the profile the document claims conformance to (the first profile-level gate on the UBL side). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed UBL fixture with the canonical PEPPOL URN → confirmation row; same fixture with the URN reduced to `urn:cen.eu:en16931:2017` → mismatch row citing both the actual and expected values plus the PEPPOL profile-gate failure mode). Audit OK. Full suite: 296 → 298 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 49):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `GrpHdr/MsgId` EPC SEPA Rulebook character-set check — the first character-set gate, complementing the existing scalar and arithmetic invariants from drafts 27 / 29 / 35 / 38 / 40 / 42 / 43 / 44 / 46. ISO 20022 declares `MsgId` as `Max35Text` (any Unicode codepoint, length ≤ 35), but the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook §AT-43 and the parallel section in the SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook pin free-text fields and identifiers to a narrow ISO 20022 BasicLatin subset: `A-Z a-z 0-9 / - ? : ( ) . , ' +` and space. A `MsgId` carrying accented letters, em-dashes, or emoji (the typical real-world cause: a value pasted from a CRM template that wasn't sanitised) is schema-valid XML but rail-broken — EBA STEP2 / STET / RT1 either strip the offending characters before forwarding (which destroys the reconciliation reference) or hard-reject the file at the character-set gate. The new `sepaCheckMsgIdCharset()` reads `GrpHdr/MsgId` on pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers (camt.053 is statement-side and out of scope for this gate), tests against the regex `^[A-Za-z0-9/\-?:().,'+ ]+$`, and emits one green `✓ GrpHdr/MsgId X uses only EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed characters …` row on match or a red `✗ GrpHdr/MsgId X contains characters outside the EPC SEPA Rulebook allowed set …` row otherwise, citing the allowed set inline so the maintainer doesn't have to look up the rulebook. Header-only by design — `EndToEndId` uniqueness (draft 43) and `InstrId` presence (draft 19) have their own gates, and the character-set failure mode is dominated by the document-level identifier in practice (a CRM-driven MsgId is the most common entry point for non-Latin characters into the file). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EPC SEPA / ISO 20022 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `MsgId=MSG-2024-001` → confirmation citing the EPC SEPA allowed character set; same fixture with `MSG-2024-Ää` injected → mismatch row citing the offending value and the EPC SEPA character-set requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 296 → 298 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 48):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a line-ID uniqueness check (EN 16931 BR-21 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0) — the fourth file-wide accounting / structural invariant on the UBL side, alongside the line-sum + tax-inclusive invariants from draft 28 and the PayableAmount invariant from draft 45, and parallel in shape to the SEPA `EndToEndId` uniqueness gate from draft 43. EN 16931 BR-21 names `cbc:ID` as the InvoiceLine identifier; PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 access points additionally reject duplicates because downstream processors key per-line allowances, charges, and dispute references off the ID — a collision causes silent reconciliation failures even when the per-line arithmetic balances. UBL 2.1 declares `cbc:ID` as plain `Identifier` text, so duplicates are schema-valid but rail-broken; the typical real-world cause is a batch generator iterating over line items without advancing the sequence counter (exactly the same failure mode the SEPA EndToEndId gate catches one layer up). The new `ublCheckLineIdUnique()` walks every `<cac:InvoiceLine>` (or `<cac:CreditNoteLine>` for credit notes — selected via `detected.type`), extracts each line's `<cbc:ID>` (whitespace-trimmed), tallies occurrences in a `Map`, and surfaces a single green `✓ Line IDs unique across the document (N line(s)) per EN 16931 BR-21 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0` row when all values are distinct, or one red `✗ Line ID duplicated within document: X appears N times — EN 16931 BR-21 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 requires unique line identifiers` row per duplicated identifier. Skips silently when fewer than two lines carry an ID — single-line invoices trivially satisfy uniqueness, and the inspector doesn't add noise for the degenerate case. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (two InvoiceLines carrying distinct `cbc:ID=1` / `cbc:ID=2` → confirmation citing BR-21 and the 2-line count; both lines reusing `cbc:ID=1` → duplicate row citing the count and the BR-21 / PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 294 → 296 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 47):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a UNCL 1001 document-type-code check enforcing the PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 allowed sets. `<Invoice>` documents declare the type code via `<cbc:InvoiceTypeCode>` (EN 16931 BT-3) and PEPPOL BIS 3.0 restricts it to {380, 381, 384, 389, 875, 876, 877} — 380 (Commercial invoice) is by far the most common. `<CreditNote>` documents declare it via `<cbc:CreditNoteTypeCode>` and PEPPOL restricts it to {381, 396, 532}. Any other UNCL 1001 code is schema-valid plain integer text (the schema only requires `cbc:Code` shape) but rail-broken — PEPPOL access points reject the document at the type-code check, and the typical real-world cause is a template repurposed across UNCL document types (e.g. a debit-note code `383` carried in an invoice slot). The new `ublCheckDocumentTypeCode()` reads the type-code element selected by `detected.type` (`InvoiceTypeCode` for Invoice, `CreditNoteTypeCode` for CreditNote), trims whitespace, and emits one green `✓ Document type code N is allowed by PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931 (UNCL 1001)` row on a match or one red `✗ Document type code N is not in the PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 allowed set (…)` row citing the full allowed set via `intlList()` so the locale joiner applies (`380, 381, …, 875 und 876 und 877` in de, `… et 877` in fr). Missing element skips silently — the existing header rendering already surfaces what's declared, and `BR-02` (mandatory `InvoiceTypeCode`) absence is caught by the schema-validation step a real implementation would run upstream. Distinct from the VAT category-code check (draft 41, which validates `TaxCategory/ID` letters S/Z/E/…) — that check covers per-line and per-subtotal VAT categorisation; this check covers the document-level type identifier. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 / UNCL 1001 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed Invoice with `InvoiceTypeCode=380` → confirmation citing UNCL 1001 and PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0; same fixture with `InvoiceTypeCode=999` → mismatch row citing both the offending code and the allowed-set members). Audit OK. Full suite: 292 → 294 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 46):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a pain.008 `SeqTp` validation — the per-PmtInf direct-debit sequence-type gate from the EPC SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook, parallel in shape to the PmtMtd / ChrgBr / SvcLvl scalar checks from drafts 40 / 42 / 44 but pain.008-only since SeqTp is meaningless for credit transfers. ISO 20022 declares `PmtTpInf/SeqTp` as `Max4Text` so any 4-character value passes the schema; the EPC SDD Rulebook restricts the value to `FRST` (first collection of a recurring mandate), `RCUR` (recurring), `OOFF` (one-off), or `FNAL` (final collection). A pain.008 declaring `RECR` (a common typo for `RCUR`), `RECU`, or any other code is schema-valid XML but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) reject it at the rail-level sequence-type check, and the typical real-world cause is a template repurposed across recurring and one-off mandates without updating the field. The new `sepaCheckSeqTp()` walks every `<PmtInf>` in pain.008 wrappers (the function exits early for pain.001 / camt.053), locates `PmtTpInf/SeqTp` (both elements optional per the schema), reads the code (case-folded and whitespace-stripped for paste-safety), and accumulates the mismatches. On full success: one green `✓ SeqTp valid per EPC SDD Rulebook: N PmtInf block(s) declare one of FRST / RCUR / OOFF / FNAL` row across all matching blocks. On any failure: one red `✗ SeqTp invalid: PmtInf #i declares X but EPC SDD Rulebook allows only FRST / RCUR / OOFF / FNAL (ISO 20022 / EPC SDD)` row per offending block keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index so a maintainer scanning a multi-PmtInf file knows exactly which block to fix. Blocks that omit `PmtTpInf` or `SeqTp` skip silently — they already render with the rest of the PmtInf header and the maintainer can see the absence. With this draft, the four EPC-rulebook scalar gates are wired across both rulebooks: PmtMtd (TRF/DD per document type), ChrgBr (SLEV per the SCT/SDD settlement model), SvcLvl (SEPA per the rulebook's service-level designation), and SeqTp (FRST/RCUR/OOFF/FNAL per the SDD sequence-type table). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SDD vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.008 with `PmtTpInf/SeqTp=FRST` → confirmation citing the EPC SDD allowed set; same fixture with `SeqTp=RECR` → mismatch row citing the typo'd value and the four-code allowed set). Audit OK. Full suite: 290 → 292 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 45):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `PayableAmount` consistency check (EN 16931 BR-CO-16) — the third file-wide accounting gate on the UBL side, alongside the line-sum and tax-inclusive invariants shipped in draft 28. PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 invoices declare `LegalMonetaryTotal/PayableAmount` as the figure the buyer is actually asked to pay; BR-CO-16 pins it to `TaxInclusiveAmount − PrepaidAmount + PayableRoundingAmount`, with `PrepaidAmount` and `PayableRoundingAmount` defaulting to 0 when absent (the most common shape — the canonical small invoice carries neither). A mismatch is schema-valid arithmetic but rail-broken: PEPPOL access points reject the invoice at the BR-CO-16 check before it ever reaches the buyer, and the typical real-world cause is a prepaid amount applied in the buyer's accounting but not subtracted from `PayableAmount`, so the invoice asks for the wrong figure. The new `ublCheckPayable()` reads the four amounts from `LegalMonetaryTotal`, treats missing optional fields as 0 cents, computes the expected value in integer cents (`Math.round(value * 100)`) to dodge IEEE-754 drift, applies the standard ±1¢ rounding tolerance per the EN 16931 published policy, and surfaces one green `✓ PayableAmount X matches TaxInclusive − Prepaid + PayableRounding (EN 16931 BR-CO-16)` row when the invariant holds, or one red `✗ PayableAmount mismatch: TaxInclusive − Prepaid + PayableRounding = A ≠ declared PayableAmount B (EN 16931 BR-CO-16)` row citing both the computed and declared values. Skips silently when either `TaxInclusiveAmount` or `PayableAmount` is missing or unparseable — the existing `LegalMonetaryTotal` rendering already surfaces the raw values upstream, and the inspector doesn't add noise for half-filled documents. Distinct from the totals-consistency check (draft 28, which verifies `TaxExcl + Σ TaxAmount = TaxInclusive`) and the VAT-rate check (draft 37, which verifies per-subtotal arithmetic): those checks cover what goes *into* the tax-inclusive total, this one covers what comes *out* of it as the buyer-facing payable figure. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed Invoice with TaxInclusive=PayableAmount=119.00 EUR → confirmation citing BR-CO-16; same fixture with PayableAmount bumped to 120.00 EUR → mismatch row citing both 119.00 and 120.00 plus BR-CO-16). Audit OK. Full suite: 288 → 290 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 44):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `SvcLvl=SEPA` consistency check — the third of the three EPC-rulebook scalar gates at the PmtInf level, parallel in shape to the `PmtMtd` check shipped in draft 40 and the `ChrgBr` check shipped in draft 42. ISO 20022 declares `PmtTpInf/SvcLvl/Cd` as a free 4-character service-level code, with values like `NURG` (non-urgent), `URGP` (urgent), `PRPT` (priority), and `SEPA` (standard SCT/SDD); the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook both pin it to `SEPA` for the standard SCT/SDD flow. A pain.001 or pain.008 declaring `NURG` or any other non-`SEPA` code is schema-valid XML but rail-broken — clearing systems (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1) reject it at the rail-level service-level gate, and the typical real-world cause is a template file repurposed across SEPA and non-SEPA wire-instruction flows without updating the field. The new `sepaCheckSvcLvl()` walks every `<PmtInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, locates `PmtTpInf/SvcLvl/Cd` (both elements optional per the schema), reads the code (case-folded and whitespace-stripped for paste-safety), and accumulates the mismatches. On full success: one green `✓ SvcLvl matches EPC SEPA Rulebook: N PmtInf block(s) declare SEPA (standard SCT/SDD service level)` row across all matching blocks. On any failure: one red `✗ SvcLvl mismatch: PmtInf #i declares X but EPC SEPA Rulebook requires SEPA (ISO 20022 / EPC SCT/SDD)` row per offending block, keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index so a maintainer scanning a multi-PmtInf file knows exactly which block to fix. Blocks that omit `PmtTpInf` or `SvcLvl/Cd` skip silently — they already render with the rest of the PmtInf header and the maintainer can see the absence. With this draft, the three EPC-rulebook scalar gates are complete: PmtMtd (TRF/DD per document type), ChrgBr (SLEV per the SEPA settlement model), and SvcLvl (SEPA per the rulebook's service-level designation). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `<PmtTpInf><SvcLvl><Cd>SEPA</Cd></SvcLvl></PmtTpInf>` injected after `<PmtMtd>` → confirmation citing the SEPA expectation; same fixture with `Cd=NURG` → mismatch row citing both the declared NURG and the SEPA requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 286 → 288 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 43):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an `EndToEndId` uniqueness gate — the third file-wide accounting check, complementing the `NbOfTxs` + `CtrlSum` group-header invariants from draft 35 and the per-PmtInf scalar checks shipped in drafts 40 (`PmtMtd`) and 42 (`ChrgBr`). Both the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook (pain.001) and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook (pain.008) require the `EndToEndId` carried in `<PmtId>/<EndToEndId>` to be unique per Originator — it is the reconciliation key the beneficiary and the originator both use to track the payment end-to-end through the clearing system (EBA STEP2, STET, RT1), and a duplicate within a batch causes either a hard reject or, worse, a silent reconciliation collision downstream. ISO 20022 lets `EndToEndId` be any `Max35Text`, so duplicates are schema-valid but rail-broken; the typical real-world cause is a batch-generation script that forgot to advance a sequence counter while iterating over a customer list. The new `sepaCheckE2eIdUnique()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` and `<DrctDbtTxInf>` across all `<PmtInf>` blocks in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, collects each transaction's `EndToEndId` (whitespace-stripped), tallies the occurrences in a `Map`, and surfaces a single green `✓ EndToEndId values are unique across the file (N transaction(s)) per EPC SEPA Rulebook` row when all values are distinct, or one red `✗ EndToEndId duplicated within file: X appears N times — EPC SEPA Rulebook requires uniqueness per Originator (ISO 20022 / EPC SCT/SDD)` row per duplicated identifier. Skips silently when fewer than two transactions carry an `EndToEndId` — single-transaction files trivially satisfy uniqueness, and the inspector doesn't add noise for the degenerate case. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 extended with a second `<CdtTrfTxInf>` carrying a distinct `E2E-002` → confirmation citing the two-transaction count; same fixture with the second transaction reusing `E2E-001` → duplicate row citing `E2E-001 appears 2 times` and the EPC SEPA uniqueness requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 284 → 286 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 42):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `ChrgBr` (charge bearer) consistency check — the second of the two PmtInf-level scalar gates pinned by the EPC SEPA Rulebooks, parallel in shape to the PmtMtd check shipped in draft 40. ISO 20022 enumerates `ChrgBr` as one of `DEBT / CRED / SHAR / SLEV`; the EPC SEPA Credit Transfer Rulebook and SEPA Direct Debit Rulebook both pin it to `SLEV` ("charges shared, allocated by local law") — the SEPA settlement model assumes the originator and beneficiary each bear their own bank's charges, and a `DEBT` or `CRED` declaration contradicts the model so clearing systems reject the file. The new `sepaCheckChrgBr()` walks every `<PmtInf>` in pain.001 / pain.008 wrappers, reads `<ChrgBr>` (case-folded, whitespace-stripped), and accumulates the mismatches. On full success: one green `✓ ChrgBr matches EPC SEPA Rulebook: N PmtInf block(s) declare SLEV (charges shared, allocated by local law)` row across all matching blocks. On any failure: one red `✗ ChrgBr mismatch: PmtInf #i declares X but EPC SEPA Rulebook requires SLEV` row per offending block, keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index so a maintainer scanning a multi-PmtInf file knows exactly which block to fix. `ChrgBr` is optional per the ISO 20022 schema, so blocks that omit it skip silently — they already render with the rest of the PmtInf header and the maintainer can see the absence; the inspector doesn't add noise for fields that don't have to be there. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `ChrgBr=SLEV` injected after `<PmtMtd>` → confirmation citing the SLEV expectation; same fixture with `ChrgBr=DEBT` → mismatch row citing both the declared DEBT and the SLEV requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 282 → 284 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 41):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a VAT category-code cross-check (EN 16931 / UNCL 5305). EN 16931 declares a separate business-rule family for each allowed VAT category letter — BR-S (Standard), BR-Z (Zero), BR-E (Exempt), BR-AE (Reverse Charge), BR-K (intra-EEA), BR-G (Free export), BR-O (Outside scope), BR-L (Canary Islands), BR-M (Ceuta/Melilla) — and the rule families collectively define the allowed set `{S, Z, E, AE, K, G, O, L, M}`. Any letter outside this set is schema-valid plain text per UBL 2.1 but PEPPOL access points reject the invoice at the category-code check before it ever reaches the buyer. The new `ublCheckVatCategories()` walks every `<cac:TaxCategory>` reachable from the root (header-side `TaxTotal/TaxSubtotal/TaxCategory` and item-level `InvoiceLine/Item/ClassifiedTaxCategory` alike), reads each `<cbc:ID>` value, uppercases and dedupes, then validates against the inline `UBL_VAT_CATS` set. Confirmation row uses the existing `intlList()` helper so the locale joiner applies (`S und Z` in de, `S et Z` in fr). On failure: one warning row per offending code citing the full allowed set inline so the maintainer doesn't have to look up EN 16931. Distinct shape from the per-subtotal VAT-rate check (draft 37, which verifies `TaxableAmount × Percent === TaxAmount`) and the totals-consistency check (draft 28, which verifies `Σ TaxAmount === TaxInclusive - TaxExclusive`) — those checks are arithmetic on numeric fields and won't fire if the category letter is wrong but the math balances. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed `S` → confirmation row + UNCL 5305 reference; `XX` injected → warning row citing the allowed set). Audit OK. Full suite: 280 → 282 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 40):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains a `PmtMtd` consistency check, parallel to the BIC / currency / NbOfTxs / IBAN-BIC cross-checks shipped in drafts 27 / 29 / 35 / 38. ISO 20022 enumerates `PmtMtd` as one of `CHK / TRF / TRA / DD` at the `<PmtInf>` level; the EPC SEPA Rulebooks tie two of those values to specific message schemas — pain.001.001.xx (customer credit-transfer initiation) MUST carry `PmtMtd=TRF` and pain.008.001.xx (customer direct-debit initiation) MUST carry `PmtMtd=DD`. A pain.001 declaring `DD`, or a pain.008 declaring `TRF`, is schema-valid XML but rail-broken — clearing systems reject it because the declared payment method contradicts the message's purpose, and the typical real-world cause is a template file that was repurposed across the two flows without updating the field. The new `sepaCheckPmtMtd()` walks every `<PmtInf>` in the document, reads its `<PmtMtd>` element (case-folded and whitespace-stripped for paste-safety), and accumulates the mismatches. On full success: one green `✓ PmtMtd matches document type: N PmtInf block(s) declare TRF as expected for pain.001` row across all matching blocks. On any failure: one red `✗ PmtMtd mismatch: PmtInf #i declares X but pain.001 requires TRF (ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA rulebook)` row per offending block — keyed by the 1-based PmtInf index so a maintainer scanning a multi-PmtInf file knows exactly which block to fix. `PmtMtd` is technically optional per the schema, so blocks that omit the element are skipped silently rather than emitting a noisy "missing" warning — they already render with the rest of the PmtInf above and the maintainer can see the absence. Pairs naturally with the schema-version detection from draft 19 (the `detected.type` value drives both the banner and the expected-PmtMtd lookup). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed pain.001 with `PmtMtd=TRF` → confirmation citing the TRF expectation; same fixture with TRF rewritten to DD → mismatch row citing both the declared DD and pain.001's TRF requirement). Audit OK. Full suite: 278 → 280 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 39):** §2.7 PEPPOL UBL inspector gains a `DueDate` vs `IssueDate` temporal sanity check. EN 16931 declares `IssueDate` as BT-2 and `DueDate` as BT-9; neither EN 16931 nor the BIS Billing 3.0 schematron names a numbered business rule for the temporal ordering (only `Format` and `Code-list` rules apply to the dates themselves), but PEPPOL access-point implementations universally reject the backdated case because payment can't fall due before the invoice is even issued — it's the canonical data-entry error the cross-check exists to surface. The new `ublCheckDates()` reads both ISO 8601 dates from the root, parses them via `Date.parse()`, and surfaces an explicit row: green `✓ DueDate D is on or after IssueDate I` confirmation when both parse cleanly and `dueTs >= issueTs`, red `✗ DueDate D is earlier than IssueDate I — payment cannot fall due before the invoice is issued` row when the order is reversed. Skips silently when either date is missing or unparseable — the existing raw `Issue date` / `Due date` rows in `ublRenderHeader` already surface the underlying values, so a missing date is visible upstream without needing a duplicate warning here. Distinct from the totals consistency check (draft 28), VAT-rate check (draft 37), currency check (draft 31), and IBAN check (draft 36) — those are arithmetic / format invariants, this is the first *temporal* invariant on the UBL side, mirroring the LOTL freshness check from draft 34 in shape. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed Invoice from the shared fixture with IssueDate 2024-04-01 / DueDate 2024-05-01 → confirmation row mentioning both dates; same fixture with DueDate rewritten to 2024-03-15 → mismatch row citing both values). Audit OK. Full suite: 276 → 278 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 38):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains an IBAN ↔ BIC country-code consistency check — a different shape from the existing scalar cross-checks (IBAN MOD-97 from draft 16, BIC format from draft 27, ISO 4217 currency from draft 29, NbOfTxs/CtrlSum from draft 35). This one is *relational*: it cares about how two values pair within the same scope. ISO 13616 puts the country in IBAN chars 0-1; ISO 9362 puts the country in BIC chars 4-5 (the BIC country sub-code, immediately after the institution code). Within a SEPA scope, the two MUST agree: `<DbtrAcct>/<IBAN>` paired with `<DbtrAgt>/<BIC|BICFI>` at PmtInf level, and `<CdtrAcct>/<IBAN>` paired with `<CdtrAgt>/<BIC|BICFI>` at transaction level (both `CdtTrfTxInf` for credit transfers and `DrctDbtTxInf` for direct debits). A German IBAN routed via a French agent is almost always a copy-paste error and is rejected at clearing (EBA STEP2, EBA Clearing, STET, RT1 — all SEPA clearing rails surface "IBAN/BIC country mismatch" as a hard reject reason). The new `sepaPairIbanBic()` helper extracts `(IBAN, BIC)` sibling pairs from a parent element by walking the standard ISO 20022 child names (`DbtrAcct + DbtrAgt`, `CdtrAcct + CdtrAgt`); `sepaCheckIbanBicCountryParity()` aggregates pairs across every PmtInf and every transaction-info element, then renders one `✓ IBAN ↔ BIC country codes agree across all account/agent pairs (N)` confirmation row across all matching pairs or one `✗ IBAN ↔ BIC country mismatch: IBAN X declares country CC1 but BIC Y declares CC2` row per offending pair. Only pairs that have both an IBAN and a BIC (or BICFI) participate — half-filled pairs skip silently. Case-folded and whitespace-stripped on extraction so user-entered SEPA samples with formatting drift still pair correctly. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 13616 / 9362 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed German pain.001 → confirmation; German IBAN with COBADEFFXXX swapped for BNPAFRPPXXX → mismatch row citing both the full IBAN and the French BIC). Audit OK. Full suite: 274 → 276 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 37):** §2.7 UBL inspector gains a per-`TaxSubtotal` VAT-rate consistency check enforcing EN 16931 BR-CO-17 (and the parallel category-letter rules BR-S-08 / BR-Z-08 / BR-E-08 / BR-AE-08 / BR-K-08 / BR-G-08 / BR-O-08 / BR-IC-08, which are identical in effect). PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 invoices break down the VAT total into one `<cac:TaxSubtotal>` per rate, each declaring `TaxableAmount`, `TaxCategory/Percent`, and `TaxAmount` — the three values that EN 16931 requires to agree. The new `ublCheckVatRates()` walks every subtotal across every `<cac:TaxTotal>`, computes `Math.round((TaxableAmount × Percent) / 100)` in integer cents (matching EN 16931's "round once per subtotal" policy — double rounding via tenths-of-cent would over-tolerate small drift), and compares against the declared `TaxAmount` with the standard ±1¢ rounding tolerance the spec allows on fractional currencies. Per-subtotal `✓ TaxSubtotal #N: TaxAmount matches TaxableAmount × X% = Y (EN 16931 BR-CO-17)` confirmation rows or `✗ TaxSubtotal #N VAT-rate mismatch: declared TaxAmount A but TaxableAmount × X% = B` warning rows. Catches the canonical test-invoice error this rule exists to prevent: typing the wrong monetary amount for a given rate (e.g. 19.50 EUR tax on a 100.00 EUR base at 19% where the correct value is 19.00). Distinct from the totals check shipped in draft 28: that check verified `Σ subtotals === header total`, this one verifies each subtotal is internally consistent. The two checks together fully cover EN 16931's tax-arithmetic invariants. Subtotals missing any of the three fields skip silently (the per-rate breakdown already surfaces the raw values). Amounts formatted via `intlNumber()` with two fraction digits so locale decimal separators apply. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed 19% subtotal on a 100 EUR base → confirmation row mentioning BR-CO-17; 19.50 EUR declared on a 100 × 19% base, with the header `TaxAmount` rewritten to match so the totals check from draft 28 doesn't also fire → mismatch row citing both 19.50 and the expected 19.00). Audit OK. Full suite: 272 → 274 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 36):** §2.7 UBL inspector gains a Payee/Payer-account IBAN MOD-97 cross-check (parallel to the SEPA IBAN cross-check from draft 16). PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 invoices declare the supplier's bank coordinates in `<cac:PaymentMeans>/<cac:PayeeFinancialAccount>/<cbc:ID>` (or `<cac:PayerFinancialAccount>` for self-billing); the schema doesn't enforce that the value is an IBAN, so the inspector first regex-checks each ID against the ISO 13616 IBAN shape (`CC + 2 digits + 11–30 alnum`, length 15–34) and only runs the `ibanValidate()` MOD-97 walk on values that match. Confirmation row `✓ Payee/Payer financial account IBANs valid (ISO 13616 MOD-97) (N)` on full success; one warning row per failing IBAN otherwise. Non-IBAN account numbers (BBANs, US ABA routing numbers, anything outside the IBAN regex) are silently skipped — they're not the wrong identifier, just a different one. Closes a real gap where a maintainer could paste a PEPPOL invoice with a typo'd IBAN and see the inspector parse it cleanly, while a downstream payments processor would reject it at MOD-97 time. Same shape and surfacing as the SEPA IBAN cross-check, with the regex prefilter being the only meaningful difference (UBL allows non-IBAN account numbers; SEPA forbids them). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 13616 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (well-formed `DE89370400440532013000` injected into a `<cac:PaymentMeans>` block surfaces the confirmation row + ISO 13616 reference; final-digit flip → warning row mentioning the offending IBAN). Audit OK. Full suite: 270 → 272 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 35):** §2.4 SEPA inspector gains `NbOfTxs` + `CtrlSum` group-header invariant checks — the same shape as the UBL totals consistency check from draft 28 and the parallel BIC / currency cross-checks shipped in drafts 27 / 29. pain.001 (credit-transfer initiation) and pain.008 (direct-debit) files declare two header-level totals in `GrpHdr`: `NbOfTxs` (count) and `CtrlSum` (sum of transaction amounts). EPC SEPA rulebooks make a mismatch a hard reject reason on the bank side, so surfacing it on the inspector matches the real-world failure mode. The new `sepaCheckGrpHdrInvariants()` walks every `<CdtTrfTxInf>` + `<DrctDbtTxInf>` across all `PmtInf` blocks, then: (a) compares the count against `GrpHdr/NbOfTxs` — green `✓ NbOfTxs matches transaction count (N)` confirmation or red `✗ NbOfTxs mismatch: header declares X but file contains Y transactions` warning; (b) sums every transaction-level `<InstdAmt>` (credit-transfers) / `<Amt>` (direct-debits) in integer cents (`Math.round(value * 100)` to dodge IEEE-754 drift) with ±1¢ tolerance per the ISO 20022 implementation-guide rounding policy — green `✓ CtrlSum matches transaction total (sum)` or red `✗ CtrlSum mismatch: header declares X but transaction amounts sum to Y` warning. Both gates are independent; the CtrlSum check only fires when CtrlSum is present (it's optional per the schema). Amounts are formatted via `intlNumber()` with `minimumFractionDigits: 2` so the locale's decimal separator applies (`100,00` in de/fr, `100.00` in en). 4 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 20022 / EPC SEPA vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Three new Playwright cases (happy path — both invariants confirm on SEPA_PAIN001; NbOfTxs overcounted from 1→2 surfaces the count mismatch; CtrlSum undersummed from 100.00→50.00 surfaces the amount mismatch). Audit OK. Full suite: 267 → 270 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 34):** §2.6 LOTL / TSL viewer gains a `NextUpdate` freshness check (ETSI TS 119 612 §5.3.13). The existing scheme-information block already rendered the raw `NextUpdate/dateTime` value as a key-value row; the inspector now additionally parses that timestamp, compares it against `Date.now()`, and surfaces a freshness row beneath it — green `✓ Trust list is current — next update due in N days (ETSI TS 119 612 §5.3.13)` when the horizon is still in the future, red `✗ Trust list is stale — NextUpdate was N days ago; refetch from ec.europa.eu before relying on this list (ETSI TS 119 612 §5.3.13)` when it has lapsed. The signal matters because the LOTL / TSL operator commits to a refresh cadence; accepting trust decisions from a stale XML can mean continuing to trust a CA that has since been withdrawn or downgraded at the EU level. Day delta is rendered via the existing `intlNumber()` so locale thousands separators apply on month-scale numbers. Guarded: no row is added if `NextUpdate` is missing or unparseable (the existing raw row still shows). Mirrors the JWT `exp` / `nbf` pattern of "show the raw claim, then a separate ✓ / ✗ row that gates acceptance." 2 new message strings (`lotl.warn.stale`, `lotl.ok.current`) + 1 row label (`lotl.row.freshness`) × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ETSI TS 119 612 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases — NextUpdate 30 days in the future → current row + ETSI reference; NextUpdate 90 days in the past → stale row + `ec.europa.eu` refresh pointer. Audit OK. Full suite: 265 → 267 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 33):** Two more JWT-inspector header-side rows shipped, completing the RFC 7515 §4.1 "the four headers a maintainer most often wants to eyeball" set (alg / kid / typ / crit). (a) **`typ` (token type, RFC 7515 §4.1.9 + RFC 8725 §3.11)** — informational muted badge sourced from the header. RFC 8725 §3.11 names explicit token typing as the standard defense against token-type confusion attacks: an OAuth access token tagged `at+jwt` (RFC 9068) must not be accepted as an OIDC `id_token+jwt`, an SD-JWT VC tagged `vc+sd-jwt` must not be accepted as a plain `JWT`, etc. Surfacing `typ` lets a maintainer reading a token confirm the declared media type matches the slot the token is being used in, without grepping the raw header JSON. (b) **`crit` (critical headers, RFC 7515 §4.1.11)** — danger red badge. When `crit` is present the verifier MUST understand every header parameter named in the array, or reject the token; silently accepting a token with unknown critical extensions is the textbook security failure the field exists to prevent. The inspector can't know what extensions the maintainer's verifier supports, so it surfaces the names via the existing `intlList()` helper (`exp und b64` in de, `exp et b64` in fr) along with the RFC 7515 §4.1.11 reference. Only fires when `crit` is a non-empty array per the RFC's "MUST be a non-empty array" requirement. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against RFC 7515 + 8725 + 9068 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). One new Playwright case asserting both rows against a hand-crafted token with `typ: "at+jwt"` and `crit: ["exp", "b64"]`. Audit OK. Full suite: 264 → 265 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 32):** Two more JWT-inspector informational rows shipped, both following the same shape as the draft 30 `sub` row. (a) **`kid` (key ID, RFC 7515 §4.1.4)** sourced from the header — surfaced as a muted badge right after the alg=none warning slot. JWKS-driven verifiers use this hint to pick the right key from a key set; surfacing it lets a maintainer cross-reference a token against a JWKS URL without parsing the raw header JSON by eye. (b) **`jti` (JWT ID, RFC 7519 §4.1.7)** sourced from the payload — surfaced as a muted badge right after the `sub` row. Issuers attach `jti` for replay-defense and audit-log correlation; reading it from a badge is faster than grepping the raw payload. Both honor string or numeric values per RFC 7519's leave-it-to-the-application typing rule (some issuers use sequence numbers, some use UUIDs). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against RFC 7515 + 7519 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). One new Playwright case asserting both rows render against a hand-crafted token with kid in the header and jti in the payload. Audit OK. Full suite: 263 → 264 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 31):** UBL ISO 4217 currency-code cross-check shipped (§2.7 polish). Parallel to the SEPA cross-check from draft 29, the PEPPOL UBL inspector now walks every UBL element carrying a `currencyID="..."` attribute (the UBL counterpart of ISO 20022's `Ccy="..."` — present on every `*Amount` element: `LineExtensionAmount`, `TaxAmount`, `PriceAmount`, `TaxInclusiveAmount`, `PayableAmount`, …) and verifies each unique code against the inline `ISO_4217_ACTIVE` set that already powers the SEPA cross-check (no second copy — the UBL function reaches the same constant). On full success: one confirmation row listing each unique code via `intlList()` so the locale joiner applies (`EUR und USD` in de, `EUR et USD` in fr). On failure: one warning row per offending code. Catches the canonical typo failure mode (`ERU` for `EUR`) which the totals-consistency check from draft 28 wouldn't see, because all amounts can still balance against themselves with the wrong code declared. Closes the §2.7 spec line "currency code is ISO 4217" which was described but not enforced. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against e-invoicing vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). Two new Playwright cases (EUR happy path; ERU typo flagged across every currencyID attribute). Audit OK. Full suite: 261 → 263 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 30):** Two JWT-inspector polishes shipped. (a) **`alg: "none"` warning row** (RFC 8725 §3.1 / JWT BCP). The `alg=none` algorithm-confusion attack is the canonical JWT footgun — any token whose header declares it is by definition unsigned, and accepting it as authentic has caused real-world bypasses. jwtRender now surfaces an unconditional red badge immediately after the header/payload/signature blocks whenever `header.alg.toLowerCase() === 'none'` (case-insensitive because `None` / `NONE` show up in the wild). (b) **`sub` (subject) claim row** (RFC 7519 §4.1.2). The subject identifier the JWT carries claims about now renders as its own informational badge after the existing `iat` row — a maintainer reading a token no longer has to eyeball the raw JSON to find the user it identifies. Both string and numeric `sub` values are honored (RFC 7519 leaves the type to the application). 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against RFC 8725 + 7519 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern).
