Two new Playwright cases (alg=none surfaces RFC 8725 mention with the JWT header row text; RFC 7519 §A.1 fixture's `sub=1234567890` surfaces in the new row). Audit OK. Full suite: 259 → 261 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 29):** SEPA ISO 4217 currency-code cross-check shipped (§2.4 polish). The inspector now walks every element with a `Ccy` attribute (`<Amt>`, `<InstdAmt>`, `<NtryAmt>`, `<Bal>/<Amt>`, `<EqvtAmt>` and any other `Ccy`-bearing element ISO 20022 may add in a future minor revision) and verifies the currency code against an inline ISO 4217:2015 (Amendment 174) active-currencies set (≈ 180 codes, current as of 2025). On full success: one confirmation row listing each unique code via the existing `intlList()` helper, so the German variant reads `EUR und USD`, French reads `EUR et USD`, etc. On failure: one warning row per offending code. Historic codes (ITL, DEM, ROL pre-2005, etc.) are intentionally excluded from the active set — pasting an unmigrated wire format surfaces the cleanup task rather than being silently accepted. Closes the §2.4 spec line "currency code is ISO 4217" which had been described but not enforced since the original SEPA inspector landed in draft 16. 2 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). Two new Playwright cases (EUR happy path; ERU typo flagged). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 28):** UBL totals consistency check shipped (§2.7 polish). The PEPPOL UBL inspector now verifies the two EN 16931 invariants that are independent of any optional `PrepaidAmount` / `PayableRoundingAmount` complications and surfaces an explicit ✓ / ✗ row for each:

- **Line-sum invariant.** `Σ InvoiceLine/LineExtensionAmount === LegalMonetaryTotal/LineExtensionAmount`. Catches misadded line totals (the most common e-invoice arithmetic error in the wild).
- **Tax-inclusive invariant.** `LegalMonetaryTotal/TaxExclusiveAmount + Σ TaxTotal/TaxAmount === LegalMonetaryTotal/TaxInclusiveAmount`. Catches mismatches between the header VAT total and the sum of per-scheme tax amounts.

Comparison is done in integer cents (`Math.round(value * 100)`) with a ±1¢ drift tolerance per the EN 16931 rounding policy, so a single rounding-induced 0.01 EUR delta does not produce a false positive but a 0.10 EUR misadd does. Only `TaxTotal/TaxAmount` (the per-scheme grand totals) are summed for invariant #2 — `TaxSubtotal/TaxAmount` rows are skipped via a parent localName check so per-rate sub-rows don't get double-counted.

5 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against EN 16931 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). Three new Playwright cases (well-formed Invoice passes both invariants, declared LineExtensionAmount mismatch flagged, declared TaxInclusive mismatch flagged). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 27):** SEPA BIC format cross-check shipped (§2.4 polish). The inspector already cross-checked every `<IBAN>` element against MOD-97 via the standalone IBAN tool's validator; the same pattern now applies to every `<BIC>` and `<BICFI>` element (`<BIC>` is the pain.001.001.03 form, `<BICFI>` the 2009+ schema form — both are walked). On full success: one confirmation row `BIC format valid (ISO 9362) (N)`; on failure: one warning row per bad BIC. Catches the canonical typo failure mode (BIC pasted into the wrong column, character flips, lowercase) that the IBAN MOD-97 alone wouldn't surface. 4 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against ISO 9362 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). Two new Playwright cases (valid-BIC happy path, malformed-bank-code rejection). Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 26):** Main-JWT `iss` / `aud` verifier cross-checks shipped. The collapsible verifier-checks panel under the JWT input is now split into two labeled groups — "JWT (RFC 7519)" with `iss` + `aud` inputs, and "SD-JWT KB-JWT" with the existing KB-JWT `aud` + `nonce` inputs. After the time-claim badges, jwtRender consults the new inputs and renders independent ✓ / ✗ rows: green when `iss` exactly matches the expected issuer (RFC 7519 §4.1.1); green when `aud` contains the expected value (§4.1.3 — string or array form honored); red with the received value on mismatch; red with an explicit "no iss/aud claim in JWT" row when the claim is absent (so a verifier never silently passes a check). 13 new strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against RFC 7519 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). Three new Playwright cases (iss match, iss mismatch surfaces received value, aud array-form match). Audit OK. Full suite expected ≈ 250 → 253 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 25):** Two cross-cutting polishes shipped.

- **JWT time-claim row coverage.** Decoder now renders `exp`, `nbf`, and `iat` as independent badges (red / green / neutral) instead of only `exp`. RFC 7519 §4.1.5 makes `nbf` a separate acceptance gate — a token can be `exp`-valid but `nbf`-future and the inspector now surfaces that on its own line. `iat` is informational only (issuers populate it for audit/replay-window math; it does not gate acceptance). 3 new strings (`jwt.nbf.notYet` / `jwt.nbf.active` / `jwt.iat.issued`) × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against RFC 7519 vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern). 2 new Playwright cases (nbf future → red row; nbf past → active green row). The existing RFC 7519 §A.1 fixture test was extended to additionally assert the iat ISO row, so the path is exercised against a canonical wire-format token.
- **Phase 1 §1.5 #4 close-out — plural-key parity audit.** `scripts/audit-release.js` walks every key in `STRINGS.en` whose final segment matches one of the six CLDR plural rule names (`.zero|.one|.two|.few|.many|.other`), groups them by base key, and fails if any group is missing `.other` (the universal Intl.PluralRules fallback — without it, `intlPlural()` would fall through to the bare base key and surface `undefined` to the user). Non-en locales are intentionally not checked: German plurals collapse to `.other`, Chinese has no plural distinction at all — requiring `.one` in those locales would be wrong. The plural-key convention is now documented inline at the top of `scripts/export-strings-csv.js` so reviewers and contributors discover it without having to read the spec.

Full suite: 247 → 250 passed. Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 24):** Phase 7 §2.8 KB-JWT verifier-side `aud` / `nonce` cross-checks shipped. The JWT inspector exposes an optional collapsible verifier panel with two inputs (expected audience, expected nonce); when filled they are compared against the KB-JWT payload claims and rendered as explicit ✓ / ✗ rows, with the received value surfaced on mismatch and an explicit "missing claim" row when the KB-JWT omits the field. RFC 7519 array-form `aud` is honored (match if the expected value is in the array). Inputs are not persisted — per-decode replay-defense values, not preferences. 13 new UI strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts). Three new Playwright cases (aud match, aud mismatch with received value, nonce mismatch with received value). Full suite: 244 → 247 passed. Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged. Closes the draft 23 `aud` / `nonce` deferral; the only remaining §2.8 follow-up is the ISO mdoc CBOR decoder.

**Progress (draft 23):** Phase 7 §2.8 KB-JWT verification + sd_hash binding shipped. When the SD-JWT input carries a trailing KB-JWT and the issuer-signed payload declares a `cnf.jwk`, the inspector imports the JWK via Web Crypto (ES256 / ES384 / EdDSA / RS-family supported) and verifies the KB-JWT signature inline. The `sd_hash` claim is recomputed against `base64url(SHA-256(<jwt>~<disc>~…~))` per SD-JWT draft §4.3 so a swapped KB-JWT or substituted disclosures are flagged with an explicit ✗ row. Seven new UI strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). Two new Playwright cases using on-the-fly node-generated P-256 keypairs (happy-path verify; tampered-signature rejection). Full suite: 242 → 244 passed. Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 22):** Phase 7 polish slice — §2.4 SEPA gains per-PmtInf `ReqdExctnDt` (requested execution date; both schema shapes handled) and per-transaction `MndtId` (mandate ID, pain.008 direct debits via `DrctDbtTx/MndtRltdInf/MndtId`). §2.7 UBL gains a "Copy as CSV" button on the line items table — fixed-column-order export (`#,Name,Quantity,UnitCode,Price,PriceCurrency,LineAmount,LineCurrency`) with RFC 4180 quoting. Five new UI strings × 5 locales (2 SEPA + 3 UBL; en/fr/de reviewed against the banking/e-invoicing glossary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per project pattern). Three new Playwright cases. Full suite: 239 → 242 passed. Audit OK. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 21):** SD-JWT cleartext-payload reconstruction view shipped + `bytesToBase64()` consolidation. The verifier-side disclosure-resolution algorithm from SD-JWT draft §7 now renders as a `Disclosed payload (reconstructed)` block right after the disclosure table — every matched `_sd` digest is replaced by its disclosed claim, array `{"...": digest}` placeholders are spliced back in, and `_sd`/`_sd_alg` are stripped. Digests without a matching disclosure are dropped (the holder withheld them; the verifier wouldn't see them either). One new UI string (`jwt.sdjwt.reconstructed`) × 5 locales. One new Playwright case. The duplicate `bytesToBase64()` definition (the second one hoisted over the first and dropped the `urlSafe` flag) was deleted; sdJwtRender lost its inline base64url workaround. Full suite: 238 → 239 passed. No new vendored deps, no CSP change, tool count unchanged.

**Progress (draft 20):** Phase 7 §2.8 first slice shipped — SD-JWT decoder folded into the existing JWT inspector (no new top-level tool, tool count stays at 45). When the input contains a `~`, the inspector splits off the leading JWT (decoded as today), then for each disclosure base64url-decodes it to its `[salt, name, value]` or `[salt, value]` triple and computes the SHA-256 digest over the wire-bytes; that digest is cross-checked against every `_sd` array reachable from the payload (top-level and nested), so each disclosure renders with a `✓ referenced in payload _sd` or `✗ unreferenced` flag. The `_sd_alg` payload claim is honored (defaults to `sha-256` per the SD-JWT draft §5.1). A trailing segment that parses as `<b64url>.<b64url>.<b64url>` is treated as the key-binding JWT and surfaced as a separate decoded JSON block; KB-JWT signature verification, holder-key resolution from `cnf`, and the cleartext-payload reconstruction view are deferred. 10 new UI strings (`jwt.sdjwt.*`) × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against the SD-JWT draft's vocabulary; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review). Three new Playwright cases (digest-matched disclosures, orphaned disclosure flagged, KB-JWT surfaced). Full suite: 235 → 238 passed. ISO mdoc CBOR decoder + the rest of §2.8 deferred to a follow-up. One incidental bug noted but worked around inline: the file ships two `bytesToBase64()` definitions and the second hoists over the first, dropping the `urlSafe` flag — sdJwtRender inlines its own base64url path; a cleaner consolidation is a separate cleanup.

**Progress (draft 19):** SEPA inspector polish — schema-version detection (`pain.001.001.03 (customer credit-transfer initiation)` in the document banner instead of just `pain.001`) and per-transaction `<InstrId>` surfaced alongside `<EndToEndId>`. One new UI string (`sepa.tx.instrId`) × 5 locales. Two new Playwright cases. Full suite 233 → 235 passed. No tool-count change. The two items called out as "not in this slice" in the draft 16 notes are now done; the only remaining §2.4 deferral is XAdES-signed SEPA files, which is properly §2.5 territory.

**Progress (draft 18):** Phase 7 §2.7 first slice shipped — PEPPOL UBL e-invoice inspector. Namespace-blind `DOMParser` walk by `localName` (same pattern as the SEPA and LOTL inspectors) handles both OASIS UBL 2.1 root document types: `<Invoice>` (PEPPOL BIS Billing 3.0 / EN 16931) and `<CreditNote>` (which reuses the same line-item path but pulls `CreditNoteLine` / `CreditedQuantity` instead of `InvoiceLine` / `InvoicedQuantity`). Surfaced fields: `CustomizationID`, `ProfileID`, invoice number, issue and due dates, document currency, supplier / customer name (`PartyName/Name` preferred, falls back to `PartyLegalEntity/RegistrationName`), the four `LegalMonetaryTotal` amounts, per-line item (quantity with `unitCode`, unit price, item name, line amount), and per-rate `TaxSubtotal` breakdown. All currency-tagged amounts render with their `currencyID`. No vendored deps — reused the `sepaFirst` / `sepaTextOf` / `sepaAllLocal` helpers as generic namespace-blind XML walkers. ~160 lines of parser + render JS, ~32 new UI strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed against standard e-invoicing terminology; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts pending native review per the project pattern), 5 SEO prose keys en-only via the existing Phase 7 carve-out. Tool count 44 → 45 — JSON-LD `featureList` + `featureList_count`, meta / og / twitter descriptions, and README all updated. Three new Playwright cases (Invoice happy path with line + tax breakdown, CreditNote happy path, non-UBL XML rejected). Full suite: 233 passed. Factur-X / ZUGFeRD hybrid PDF + UN/CEFACT CII deferred until the §2.5 PDF attachment extractor lands. One incidental fix landed in the same change: the shared `sepaFirst()` / `sepaCollectIbans()` walkers were upgraded from `getElementsByTagName(name)` to `getElementsByTagNameNS('*', name)` so they match by local name across every namespace — the unprefixed form was missing PEPPOL UBL's `cbc:` / `cac:` elements. The SEPA and LOTL inspectors are unaffected (their docs use default namespaces, where both forms return the same nodes).

**Progress (draft 17):** Phase 7 §2.6 eIDAS LOTL / TSL viewer shipped. Same namespace-blind `DOMParser` pattern as the SEPA inspector (§2.4): walk by `localName`, prefer `xml:lang="en"` for multi-language `<Name>` blocks. Two modes — LOTL renders the per-country `<OtherTSLPointer>` entries with their `<TSLLocation>` URLs; TSL renders per-`<TrustServiceProvider>` services with type / status / starting time and acknowledges embedded `<X509Certificate>` blocks without parsing them inline (defers to the TLS Certificate Parser). The §0.2 / §2.6 "no fetching" rule is honored — a footer note on every parse points the user to `ec.europa.eu` for the official LOTL file. No vendored deps. ~190 lines of parser + render JS, ~25 new UI strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed; zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts), 5 SEO prose keys en-only. Tool count 43 → 44 — JSON-LD featureList + count, meta / og / twitter descriptions, README updated. Three new Playwright cases (LOTL mode happy path, TSL mode happy path, non-trust-list XML rejected). Full suite: 230 passed.

**Progress (draft 16):** Phase 7 §2.4 SEPA ISO 20022 XML inspector shipped — first larger Phase-7 tool. Namespace-blind `DOMParser` walk by `localName` handles pain.001 (credit-transfer initiation, all minor revisions), camt.053 (bank-to-customer statement), and pain.008 (direct-debit, inherits the pain.001 renderer). Every `<IBAN>` element is cross-checked against the existing `ibanValidate()` MOD-97 implementation and bad checksums surface inline as warning rows. UI mirrors the IBAN / BIC / VAT layout (textarea + Parse button). No vendored deps. ~200 lines of parser + render JS, ~12 new UI strings × 5 locales (en/fr/de reviewed, zh-CN/hi machine-quality drafts per project pattern), 5 SEO prose keys en-only via the existing Phase 7 carve-out. Tool count 42 → 43 — JSON-LD `featureList` + `featureList_count`, meta/og/twitter descriptions, and README all updated. Four new Playwright cases (pain.001 happy path, camt.053 happy path, MOD-97 failure flagged, non-ISO-20022 XML rejected). Full suite: 227 passed.

**Progress (draft 15):** Phase 1 §1.6 command palette shipped end-to-end. Modal dialog (~14 lines of CSS, 13 lines of HTML) with `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal` / `aria-labelledby` + `role="listbox"` results, triggered by `⌘K` / `Ctrl+K`, closes on `Esc` / overlay-click. Filter haystack = tool-id + localized label + English synonyms, locale-folded via `toLocaleLowerCase(lang)`; multi-term queries AND'd. `TOOL_SYNONYMS` ships English-only (42 entries) per the §1.6 directive to defer locale-specific synonyms until §1.3 native review — polyglot fallback ensures English queries always match in any locale. Three new UI strings (`palette.title`, `palette.placeholder`, `palette.empty`) translated for all five locales; STRINGS now 1101 keys × 5 locales. Seven new Playwright cases (Ctrl+K open/Esc close, "swift" → BIC, "epoch" → timestamp, multi-term "ed signature" → Ed25519, ArrowDown+Enter, empty state, fr + en-query polyglot invariant). Full suite: 223 passed.

**Progress (draft 14):** Phase 1 §1.5 third slice — first `Intl.ListFormat` call-site. The BIC validator's multi-tag row now reads `intlList(r.tags)` instead of `r.tags.join(', ')`, so a German-locale BIC with location ending in `0` and branch `XXX` renders as `Test-BIC (Ort endet auf 0) und Hauptniederlassung (XXX)`. Other `.join(', ')` sites (TLS SANs, CIDR / cron internal fields, X.509 DN parts) intentionally left alone because they are technical token lists, not prose. One new Playwright case asserts the German `und` connector. Full suite: 216 passed. With this slice, all four `Intl.*` helpers (`NumberFormat`, `RelativeTimeFormat`, `PluralRules`, `ListFormat`) have at least one production call-site; §1.5 is closed for Phase 1.

One new Playwright case asserts the refactor by flipping `documentElement.dir = 'rtl'` and reading `getComputedStyle(...).insetInlineEnd` on `.tool-card::after`. Full suite: 215 passed. The legacy `regex.match.count: '{n} match(es)'` key was retired in the same commit — the parenthesized fallback was the textbook example of why `Intl.PluralRules` exists. STRINGS now 1098 keys × 5 locales. Two new Playwright cases (en singular/plural + fr singular/plural). Full suite: 214 passed. `intlList` is scaffolded but not yet wired to a call-site; first candidate is multi-recipient encryption or Shamir share enumeration, deferred until the surrounding strings get a §1.3 review.

Legacy `timestamp.rel.*` template strings kept as fallback for browsers that pre-date `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat`. Three new Playwright cases (fr PBKDF2, de CIDR, fr relative phrasing). Full suite: 212 passed. No new vendored deps; no CSP change; no new top-level strings. `PluralRules` / `ListFormat` deferred.

**Progress (draft 9):** PWA precache service worker shipped (§4.5). `sw.js` precaches the static shell (locale variants, vendored libs, icons, manifest, privacy page) on install and serves cache-first thereafter. Registration is HTTPS-gated, scope `/`, silent on failure. The document CSP keeps `connect-src 'none'`; a narrow path-scoped `_headers` override grants the SW response `connect-src 'self'` (needed for the install-time precache fetches and nothing else). No new vendored dependencies, no innerHTML, no behavior change for non-PWA users. Audit (`scripts/audit-release.js`) still passes — CSP regex matches the default document rule, which is unchanged.

## 7. Tensions and non-goals

These are not deferred — they are **rejected** for this project.

1. **No PII validators.** Italian Codice Fiscale, Polish PESEL, Spanish DNI/NIE, German Steuer-ID, French INSEE/NIR, Dutch BSN, UK NINO, US SSN — all rejected. They are personally identifying numbers and a validator is borderline a data-handling tool. Commercial identifiers (IBAN, VAT, BIC, GS1, ISIN, CUSIP) are fine.
2. **No EU compliance theater.** Cookie banner generators, DPA templates, "GDPR audit checklists", "DPIA wizards", privacy-policy generators — all rejected. They are a different product and would dilute the focused, technical character of the site. Refer users to dedicated compliance products.
3. **No country auto-detection.** Locale comes from `navigator.language` and `localStorage`. The site does not infer country from IP, timezone, or any other signal. The same site is served to everyone.
4. **No "translation breadth over quality."** Five well-translated locales beat fifteen badly-translated ones. New locales ship only after Pass 3 (native review) of §1.3. The README's current note ("fr/de/zh/hi are machine-quality awaiting review") gets honored: those four go through native review *before* any new Tier 1 locale ships.
5. **No fetching trust lists or revocation data.** The eIDAS and LOTL tools (§2.5, §2.6) decode what you paste; they do not phone home. This is a feature, not a limitation.
6. **No WASM unless we have to.** Pure JS first. WASM only if a real user complains and there's no other way to deliver acceptable performance (the Argon2 trade-off in §2.9 is the canonical case).
7. **No build step beyond what already exists.** Vendored scripts only. No npm install, no bundler, no transpiler. If a new tool requires a real build system, it's not the right fit for this site.
8. **No telemetry of any kind.** Including "anonymous usage metrics" or "error reporting." If something breaks, users file an issue.
9. **No accounts, no sync, no cloud anything.** The single most-requested "feature" for tools like this. Permanent no.
10. **No mobile-app wrapper.** PWA install (§4.5) is enough. No Cordova, no Capacitor, no Tauri. The browser is the platform.

---

## 8. Acceptance criteria per workstream

A workstream is "done" when:

- **i18n infrastructure (Phase 1):** RTL renders correctly with `dir="rtl"` set on the existing site; plural strings use `Intl.PluralRules`; command palette accepts at least one French synonym and finds the right tool.
- **A new locale (Phases 2, 3, 8):** all 900 rows in `strings.csv` have `<locale>_status = reviewed`; all 7 passes (§1.3) completed and signed off; locale present in `hreflang`, language picker, `build-i18n-variants.js`; `/<locale>/index.html` exists and crawls; one external reviewer (native speaker, technically literate) has clicked through every tool in the locale and not flagged issues.
- **A new tool (Phases 4, 7):** runs offline (verified by disabling network in DevTools); CSP unchanged or extension justified; constant-time comparisons where applicable; output round-trips; existing test harness covers golden path; entry in `STRINGS` and in the command palette.
- **A regulator preset (Phase 6):** linked to the regulator's actual document (not a summary); dated in the help text; parameters in a JSON config (no hardcoded logic); audit-release script flags it for re-review one year out.
- **Distribution (Phase 5):** mirror pushes succeed automatically on tag; SHA-256s reproducible from a clean clone; onion address resolves; PWA install prompt fires on Chrome and Firefox.

---

## 9. Open questions

These need answers before the relevant phase starts:

- Who are the native reviewers? Building a small list of reviewers per Tier 1 locale (one per locale minimum) is a prerequisite to Phase 2. Reach out via privacy-tools mailing lists, Tor community, Codeberg communities.
- Is there appetite for a `MAINTAINERS.md` listing per-locale reviewers (with their consent) as a transparency measure?
- Should the single-file portable build (§4.6) be linked from the site itself or only from the repo? Linking from the site adds clarity but also adds a download that could lag the live site.
- For the EUDI Wallet decoder (§2.8): wait for the ARF to stabilize (likely mid-2026), or ship against the current spec and accept churn?
- For the Argon2 trade-off (§2.9): is the WASM CSP change worth the perf? Possibly skip Argon2 entirely until Web Crypto adds it natively (under discussion in W3C).

---

## 10. Maintenance

This spec is living. When a phase completes, update the rollout section to reflect what shipped. When a regulator preset's source document is superseded, update §3. When a translation glossary entry in §1.7 is corrected by a native reviewer, update the table and note the reviewer's pseudonym (with consent) and date.

The spec is the source of truth for *intent*; the code is the source of truth for *behavior*. Where they disagree, fix one or the other — never let the gap widen.
